-\lambda s(\lambda)\bm{\Sigma}-\lambda\bm{I}=\bm{A}-\lambda s(\lambda)\tau{\bm{u}}{\bm{u}}^{\top}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

By Woodbury identity, we have

(\bm{A}-\lambda s\tau{\bm{u}}{\bm{u}}^{\top})^{-1}=\bm{A}^{-1}+\frac{\lambda s\tau}{1-\lambda s\tau\,{\bm{u}}^{\top}\bm{A}^{-1}{\bm{u}}}\,\bm{A}^{-1}{\bm{u}}{\bm{u}}^{\top}\bm{A}^{-1}.

If
{\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{u}}=0
and
{\bm{b}}^{\prime\top}{\bm{u}}=0
, then
{\bm{b}}^{\top}(\bm{A}^{-1}{\bm{u}})=O(1)\cdot({\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{u}})=0
, hence the rank-one correction vanishes:

{\bm{b}}^{\top}(\bm{A}-\lambda s\tau{\bm{u}}{\bm{u}}^{\top})^{-1}{\bm{b}}^{\prime}={\bm{b}}^{\top}\bm{A}^{-1}{\bm{b}}^{\prime}.

Applying this with
{\bm{b}}={\bm{v}}_{k}
,
{\bm{b}}^{\prime}={\bm{v}}_{\ell}
(note
{\bm{v}}_{k}^{\top}{\bm{u}}=0
by (
D.1
)) yields that the
\tau
-spike does not affect (
D.55
). Also, Lemma
34
implies that
{\bm{b}}^{\top}(\bm{A}-\lambda s\tau{\bm{u}}{\bm{u}}^{\top})^{-1}{\bm{b}}^{\prime}={\bm{b}}^{\top}\bm{A}^{-1}{\bm{b}}^{\prime}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
for
{\bm{b}}={\bm{b}}^{\prime}={\bm{m}}/\|{\bm{m}}\|
.

For
{\bm{v}}_{k}^{\top}\bm{A}^{-1}{\bm{v}}_{\ell}
, since
\bm{\Sigma}_{0}=(1-b_{\sigma}^{2})\bm{I}+b_{\sigma}^{2}\bm{S}
is orthogonally invariant (shifted Wishart),
the anisotropic local law from
[
47
]
implies
{\bm{v}}_{k}^{\top}\bm{A}^{-1}{\bm{v}}_{\ell}=\delta_{k\ell}m_{\mu}(\lambda)+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
,
giving (
D.55
). Here, we have removed the rank-one
\tau{\bm{u}}{\bm{u}}^{\top}
term in
\bm{\Sigma}
when applying (
D.58
).

For the quadratic from with respect to
{\bm{m}}
,
we apply (
D.58
) with
{\bm{b}}={\bm{b}}^{\prime}={\bm{m}}/\|{\bm{m}}\|
and then show that

{\bm{m}}^{\top}\bm{A}^{-1}{\bm{m}}=\|{\bm{m}}\|^{2}m_{\mu}(\lambda)+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

even though
{\bm{m}}
depends on
\bm{Z}
.
Write

\bm{A}^{-1}=\Big(-\lambda s(\lambda)\big((1-b_{\sigma}^{2})\bm{I}+b_{\sigma}^{2}\bm{S}\big)-\lambda\bm{I}\Big)^{-1}=\alpha(\lambda)\,(\bm{S}-\zeta(\lambda)\bm{I})^{-1},

for explicit scalars
\alpha(\lambda)
and
\zeta(\lambda)
depending on
\lambda
(and bounded away from
\mathrm{supp}({\rho_{\psi}^{\rm MP}})
for
\lambda\in\mathcal{D}
).
From Lemma
37
, we can derive that

{\bm{m}}^{\top}(\bm{S}-\zeta\bm{I})^{-1}{\bm{m}}=m_{{\rm MP},\psi}(\zeta)\,\|{\bm{m}}\|^{2}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

(D.59)

uniformly for
\zeta=\zeta(\lambda)
in the relevant domain.

For the cross term
{\bm{v}}_{k}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{R}(\lambda){\bm{m}}
,
we use (
D.58
) with
{\bm{b}}={\bm{v}}_{k}
,
{\bm{b}}^{\prime}={\bm{m}}
.
The deterministic equivalent reduces to
{\bm{v}}_{k}^{\top}\bm{A}^{-1}{\bm{m}}
since
{\bm{v}}_{k}\perp{\bm{u}}
.
Because
{\bm{v}}_{k}^{\top}{\bm{m}}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
(Lemma
34
) and
\|\bm{A}^{-1}\|=O(1)
on
\mathcal{D}
,
we get
{\bm{v}}_{k}^{\top}\bm{A}^{-1}{\bm{m}}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, yielding (
D.57
).
∎

D.4.4
Analyses of Additive Spikes and Alignments

Define the rank-three spike matrix

\bm{P}:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\bm{1}_{N}{\bm{m}}^{\top}+\frac{\theta_{\mathrm{SNR}}b_{\sigma}}{\sqrt{N}}\big({\bm{g}}_{1}{\bm{v}}_{1}^{\top}+{\bm{g}}_{2}{\bm{v}}_{2}^{\top}\big)=\sum_{k=0}^{2}\sqrt{\beta_{k}}\,{\bm{a}}_{k}{\bm{b}}_{k}^{\top},

(D.60)

where

{\bm{a}}_{0}:=\frac{\bm{1}_{N}}{\sqrt{N}},\quad{\bm{b}}_{0}:=\frac{{\bm{m}}}{\|{\bm{m}}\|},\quad{\bm{a}}_{1}:=\frac{{\bm{g}}_{1}}{\|{\bm{g}}_{1}\|},\quad{\bm{b}}_{1}:={\bm{v}}_{1},\quad{\bm{a}}_{2}:=\frac{{\bm{g}}_{2}}{\|{\bm{g}}_{2}\|},\quad{\bm{b}}_{2}:={\bm{v}}_{2},

and (by Lemmas
34
and
35
), asymptotically,

\beta_{0}=\|{\bm{m}}\|^{2}\to\beta_{\rm mean}=\frac{c_{\sigma}^{2}}{2}\psi=\tau,\qquad\beta_{1}=\beta_{2}=\theta_{\mathrm{SNR}}^{2}b_{\sigma}^{2}\to\beta_{\rm linear}=\frac{r^{2}\psi}{2}b_{\sigma}^{2}.

(D.61)

Then, we recall
\bm{Y}^{\sharp}=\bm{G}+\bm{P}
and
\bm{K}^{\sharp}=\bm{Y}^{\sharp\top}\bm{Y}^{\sharp}
defined by Proposition
31
.

Lemma 39
.

Let
\bm{G}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times n}
and
\bm{K}_{0}:=\bm{G}^{\top}\bm{G}
.
Assume
\bm{K}_{0}
has an
isolated
outlier eigenvalue

\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)}
with unit-norm eigenvector
\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
so that for any
\epsilon>0
,

\bm{K}_{0}\,\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}=\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)}\,\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau},\qquad\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)}\notin\mathrm{supp}({\mu})+(-\epsilon,\epsilon)

for all large
n
.
Define the associated unit left singular vector of
\bm{G}
as

\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\tau}:=\frac{\bm{G}\,\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}}{\sqrt{\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{N},\qquad\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\tau}\|=1.

Consider the rank–
3
additive spike matrix
\bm{P}=\sum_{k=0}^{2}\sqrt{\beta_{k}}\,\bm{a}_{k}\bm{b}_{k}^{\top}
with
\|\bm{a}_{k}\|=\|\bm{b}_{k}\|=1
and
\beta_{k}=O(1),

\bm{Y}^{\sharp}=\bm{G}+\bm{P},
and
\bm{K}^{\sharp}=\bm{Y}^{\sharp\top}\bm{Y}^{\sharp}.

Assume the
decoupling conditions
:

\max_{k\in\{0,1,2\}}|\bm{b}_{k}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\qquad\max_{k\in\{0,1,2\}}|\bm{a}_{k}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\tau}|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(D.62)

Then:

(i)

(Eigenvalue stability.)

There exists an eigenvalue
\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{\sharp}\in\operatorname{spec}(\bm{K}^{\sharp})
such that

\big|\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{\sharp}-\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)}\big|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Hence,
\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{\sharp}
is an outlier eigenvalue of
\bm{K}^{\sharp}
.

(ii)

(Eigenvector-alignment stability.)

Fix any constant
\delta>0
.
Let
\bm{\Pi}_{\delta}
be the spectral projector of
\bm{K}^{\sharp}
onto
I_{\delta}:=(\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)}-\delta,\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)}+\delta)
.
Then

\|(\bm{I}-\bm{\Pi}_{\delta})\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}\|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

In particular, the normalized vector

\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}^{\sharp}:=\frac{\bm{\Pi}_{\delta}\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}}{\|\bm{\Pi}_{\delta}\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}\|}\in\mathrm{Range}(\bm{\Pi}_{\delta})

satisfies
|\langle\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}^{\sharp},\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}\rangle|^{2}=1-o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.
Thus
\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}^{\sharp}
inherits the same eigenvector alignment limit as

\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}
(e.g. alignment with
\bm{u}=\bm{1}_{n}/\sqrt{n}
in Lemma
29
).

(iii)

(Projected resolvents remove the singularity at
\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)}
.)

Define the orthogonal projectors

\bm{P}_{R}^{\perp}:=\bm{I}_{n}-\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}^{\top},\qquad\bm{P}_{L}^{\perp}:=\bm{I}_{N}-\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\tau}\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\tau}^{\top},

and the spike-removed resolvents

\displaystyle\bm{Q}_{R}^{\perp}(\lambda)

\displaystyle:=\bm{P}_{R}^{\perp}(\bm{K}_{0}-\lambda\bm{I})^{-1}\bm{P}_{R}^{\perp},

\displaystyle\bm{Q}_{L}^{\perp}(\lambda)

\displaystyle:=\bm{P}_{L}^{\perp}(\bm{G}\bm{G}^{\top}-\lambda\bm{I})^{-1}\bm{P}_{L}^{\perp},

which admit a continuous extension to
\lambda=\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)}
.
For each fixed
k,\ell\in\{0,1,2\}
,

\bm{b}_{k}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{R}^{\perp}(\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)})\,\bm{b}_{\ell}\quad\text{and}\quad\bm{a}_{k}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{L}^{\perp}(\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)})\,\bm{a}_{\ell}

are well-defined and remain
O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. Hence, applying (
D.62
), we have

\bm{b}_{k}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{R}(\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)})\,\bm{b}_{\ell}=\bm{b}_{k}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{R}^{\perp}(\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)})\,\bm{b}_{\ell}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

(D.63)

and

\bm{a}_{k}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{L}(\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)})\,\bm{a}_{\ell}=\bm{a}_{k}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{L}^{\perp}(\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)})\,\bm{a}_{\ell}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(D.64)

Proof.

We can expand
\bm{K}^{\sharp}
as a low-rank perturbation of
\bm{K}_{0}
.
By definition,

\bm{K}^{\sharp}=(\bm{G}+\bm{P})^{\top}(\bm{G}+\bm{P})=\bm{K}_{0}+\underbrace{\bm{G}^{\top}\bm{P}+\bm{P}^{\top}\bm{G}+\bm{P}^{\top}\bm{P}}_{=:\ \bm{\Delta}}.

Thus
\bm{\Delta}
has rank at most
9
and
\|\bm{\Delta}\|=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
(since
\|\bm{G}\|=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
and
\|\bm{P}\|=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
).

Part (i).
Let
\bm{A}
be symmetric,
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
, and
\bm{v}
a unit vector. Then

\mathrm{dist}\big(\lambda,\operatorname{spec}(\bm{A})\big)\ \leq\ \|(\bm{A}-\lambda\bm{I})\bm{v}\|.

(D.65)

Indeed, considering eigen-decomposition
\bm{A}=\sum_{i}\lambda_{i}\bm{q}_{i}\bm{q}_{i}^{\top}
and
\bm{v}=\sum_{i}c_{i}\bm{q}_{i}
,
we have
\|(\bm{A}-\lambda\bm{I})\bm{v}\|^{2}=\sum_{i}(\lambda_{i}-\lambda)^{2}|c_{i}|^{2}\geq\min_{i}(\lambda_{i}-\lambda)^{2}\sum_{i}|c_{i}|^{2}=\mathrm{dist}(\lambda,\operatorname{spec}(\bm{A}))^{2}
. Since
\bm{K}_{0}\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}=\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)}\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}
, we have

(\bm{K}^{\sharp}-\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)}\bm{I})\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}=(\bm{K}_{0}-\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)}\bm{I})\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}+\bm{\Delta}\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}=\bm{\Delta}\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}.

Therefore,

\mathrm{dist}\big(\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)},\operatorname{spec}(\bm{K}^{\sharp})\big)\ \leq\ \|\bm{\Delta}\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}\|.

It remains to show
\|\bm{\Delta}\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}\|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.

Write
\bm{P}=\bm{A}\bm{\Theta}\bm{B}^{\top}
with

\bm{A}=[\bm{a}_{0},\bm{a}_{1},\bm{a}_{2}]
,
\bm{B}=[\bm{b}_{0},\bm{b}_{1},\bm{b}_{2}]
and

\bm{\Theta}=\mathrm{diag}(\sqrt{\beta_{0}},\sqrt{\beta_{1}},\sqrt{\beta_{2}})
.
Then

\displaystyle\bm{G}^{\top}\bm{P}\,\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}

\displaystyle=\bm{G}^{\top}\bm{A}\bm{\Theta}\,(\bm{B}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}),

\displaystyle\bm{P}^{\top}\bm{P}\,\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}

\displaystyle=\bm{B}\bm{\Theta}(\bm{A}^{\top}\bm{A})\bm{\Theta}\,(\bm{B}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}),

\displaystyle\bm{P}^{\top}\bm{G}\,\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}

\displaystyle=\bm{B}\bm{\Theta}\,\bm{A}^{\top}(\bm{G}\,\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau})=\sqrt{\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)}}\ \bm{B}\bm{\Theta}\,(\bm{A}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\tau}),

where we used
\bm{G}\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}=\sqrt{\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)}}\,\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\tau}
.
Hence,

\|\bm{\Delta}\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}\|\ \leq\ \|\bm{G}^{\top}\bm{A}\bm{\Theta}\|\cdot\|\bm{B}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}\|+\sqrt{\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)}}\ \|\bm{B}\bm{\Theta}\|\cdot\|\bm{A}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\tau}\|+\|\bm{B}\bm{\Theta}(\bm{A}^{\top}\bm{A})\bm{\Theta}\|\cdot\|\bm{B}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}\|.

Now
\|\bm{G}^{\top}\bm{A}\bm{\Theta}\|\leq\|\bm{G}\|\,\|\bm{A}\|\,\|\bm{\Theta}\|=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
,

\|\bm{B}\bm{\Theta}\|=O(1)
, and
\|\bm{B}\bm{\Theta}(\bm{A}^{\top}\bm{A})\bm{\Theta}\|=O(1)
.
By the decoupling assumptions (
D.62
),

\|\bm{B}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}\|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\qquad\|\bm{A}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\tau}\|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Therefore
\|\bm{\Delta}\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}\|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, and combining with (
D.65
) yields

\mathrm{dist}\big(\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)},\operatorname{spec}(\bm{K}^{\sharp})\big)=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

which proves
(i)
.

Part (ii).

Let
\bm{K}^{\sharp}=\sum_{i}\widehat{\lambda}_{i}^{\sharp}\widehat{\bm{v}}_{i}\widehat{\bm{v}}_{i}^{\top}
be an eigen-decomposition,
and write
\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}=\sum_{i}c_{i}\widehat{\bm{v}}_{i}
.
Then

\|(\bm{K}^{\sharp}-\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)}\bm{I})\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}\|^{2}=\sum_{i}(\widehat{\lambda}_{i}^{\sharp}-\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)})^{2}|c_{i}|^{2}=\|\bm{\Delta}\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}\|^{2}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Split the sum into indices with
\widehat{\lambda}_{i}^{\sharp}\in I_{\delta}
and those with
\widehat{\lambda}_{i}^{\sharp}\notin I_{\delta}
.
For
\widehat{\lambda}_{i}^{\sharp}\notin I_{\delta}
we have
|\widehat{\lambda}_{i}^{\sharp}-\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)}|\geq\delta
, hence

\delta^{2}\sum_{\widehat{\lambda}_{i}^{\sharp}\notin I_{\delta}}|c_{i}|^{2}\ \leq\ \sum_{\widehat{\lambda}_{i}^{\sharp}\notin I_{\delta}}(\widehat{\lambda}_{i}^{\sharp}-\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)})^{2}|c_{i}|^{2}\ \leq\ \|(\bm{K}^{\sharp}-\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)}\bm{I})\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}\|^{2}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Therefore
\sum_{\widehat{\lambda}_{i}^{\sharp}\notin I_{\delta}}|c_{i}|^{2}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, i.e.

\|(\bm{I}-\bm{\Pi}_{\delta})\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}\|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, proving
(ii)
.
This argument makes
no
assumption about how many eigenvalues lie inside
I_{\delta}
,
so it remains valid under collisions when there are more than one eigenvalues of
\bm{K}^{\sharp}
in
I_{\delta}
.

Part (iii).

Let
\{\widehat{\lambda}_{i}^{(0)},\widehat{\bm{u}}_{i}^{(0)}\}
be an eigen-decomposition of
\bm{K}_{0}
with

\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)}
corresponding to
\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}
.
Then for
\lambda\neq\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)}
,

(\bm{K}_{0}-\lambda\bm{I})^{-1}=\frac{1}{\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)}-\lambda}\,\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}^{\top}+\sum_{i\neq\tau}\frac{1}{\widehat{\lambda}_{i}^{(0)}-\lambda}\,\widehat{\bm{u}}_{i}^{(0)}\widehat{\bm{u}}_{i}^{(0)\top}.

Multiplying by
\bm{P}_{R}^{\perp}
on both sides removes the pole term, giving

\bm{Q}_{R}^{\perp}(\lambda)=\sum_{i\neq\tau}\frac{1}{\widehat{\lambda}_{i}^{(0)}-\lambda}\,\widehat{\bm{u}}_{i}^{(0)}\widehat{\bm{u}}_{i}^{(0)\top},

which is continuous at
\lambda=\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)}
(since the sum is over
i\neq\tau
).
The same argument applies to
\bm{Q}_{L}^{\perp}(\lambda)
using the eigen-decomposition of
\bm{G}\bm{G}^{\top}
.
Hence the quadratic forms in
(iii)
are well-defined and
O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.
This is exactly what is needed to write the additive outlier determinant equation in a way
that still makes sense at the collision point
\lambda=\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)}
.
∎

Lemma 40

(Verification of the decoupling conditions
(
D.62
)
for
\bm{K}_{0}
)
.

Under the assumptions of Lemma
29
, additionally suppose the separated regime, that is,
\tau>\tau_{\mathrm{crit}}
and
z^{\prime}(-1/\Lambda_{\tau})>0
. Under these conditions, the matrix
\bm{K}_{0}=\bm{G}^{\top}\bm{G}
possesses a unique isolated outlier eigenvalue, denoted by
\lambda_{\tau}
, associated with a unit-norm eigenvector
\bm{u}_{\tau}
.
Define the associated left singular vector

\bm{w}_{\tau}:=\bm{G}\bm{u}_{\tau}/\sqrt{\lambda_{\tau}}
.
Recall the spike directions

\bm{b}_{0}=\frac{\bm{m}}{\|\bm{m}\|},\quad\bm{b}_{1}=\bm{v}_{1},\quad\bm{b}_{2}=\bm{v}_{2},\qquad\bm{a}_{0}=\frac{\bm{1}_{N}}{\sqrt{N}},\quad\bm{a}_{1}=\frac{\bm{g}_{1}}{\|\bm{g}_{1}\|},\quad\bm{a}_{2}=\frac{\bm{g}_{2}}{\|\bm{g}_{2}\|}.

Then the decoupling conditions (
D.62
) hold:

\max_{k\in\{0,1,2\}}|\bm{b}_{k}^{\top}\bm{u}_{\tau}|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\qquad\max_{k\in\{0,1,2\}}|\bm{a}_{k}^{\top}\bm{w}_{\tau}|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Proof.

First we consider the right decoupling condition
\max_{k}|\bm{b}_{k}^{\top}\bm{u}_{\tau}|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.
By the XOR construction,
\bm{v}_{1}^{\top}\bm{u}=\bm{v}_{2}^{\top}\bm{u}=0
where
{\bm{u}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bm{1}_{n}
.
Applying (
D.23
) in Lemma
29
with
\bm{v}=\bm{v}_{k}
gives

\bm{v}_{k}^{\top}\bm{u}_{\tau}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\qquad k\in\{1,2\}.

Moreover, Lemma
34
gives
\bm{u}^{\top}\bm{m}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
and
\|\bm{m}\|\to\sqrt{\beta_{\mathrm{mean}}}>0
.
Therefore, we can obtain

\bm{b}_{0}^{\top}\bm{u}=\frac{\bm{m}^{\top}\bm{u}}{\|\bm{m}\|}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Applying Lemma
29
again with
\bm{v}=\bm{b}_{0}
in (
D.23
) yields
\bm{b}_{0}^{\top}\bm{u}_{\tau}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Taking the maximum over
k\in\{0,1,2\}
completes the first part.

Second, we consider the left decoupling condition
\max_{k}|\bm{a}_{k}^{\top}\bm{w}_{\tau}|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.
Let
\bm{G}^{e}
be
\widetilde{\bm{G}}
defined in Lemma
33
, so Lemma
33
indicates that
\|\bm{G}-\bm{G}^{e}\|\prec N^{-1/2}
and
\bm{G}^{e}
is independent of

(\bm{g}_{1},\bm{g}_{2})
.
Define
\bm{K}_{0}^{e}:=(\bm{G}^{e})^{\top}\bm{G}^{e}
and let
(\lambda_{\tau}^{e},\bm{u}_{\tau}^{e})
denote the isolated eigenpair of
\bm{K}_{0}^{e}
corresponding to the outlier of
\bm{K}_{0}
. In fact, Lemma
29
holds for
\bm{K}_{0}^{e}
as well.
Set
\bm{w}_{\tau}^{e}:=\bm{G}^{e}\bm{u}_{\tau}^{e}/\sqrt{\lambda_{\tau}^{e}}
. Since

\|\bm{K}_{0}-\bm{K}_{0}^{e}\|=\|\bm{G}^{\top}\bm{G}-(\bm{G}^{e})^{\top}\bm{G}^{e}\|\leq(\|\bm{G}\|+\|\bm{G}^{e}\|)\,\|\bm{G}-\bm{G}^{e}\|=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)\cdot O_{\mathbb{P}}(N^{-1/2})=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

we can claim that

\|\bm{w}_{\tau}-\bm{w}_{\tau}^{e}\|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(D.66)

by Davis–Kahan Theorem. Also, we have

\|\bm{u}_{\tau}-\bm{u}_{\tau}^{e}\|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
and
|\lambda_{\tau}-\lambda_{\tau}^{e}|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.

Fix
k\in\{1,2\}
. Since
\bm{a}_{k}
is unit,

|\bm{a}_{k}^{\top}\bm{w}_{\tau}|\leq|\bm{a}_{k}^{\top}\bm{w}_{\tau}^{e}|+\|\bm{w}_{\tau}-\bm{w}_{\tau}^{e}\|=|\bm{a}_{k}^{\top}\bm{w}_{\tau}^{e}|+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

by (
D.66
).
Condition on
\bm{w}_{\tau}^{e}
(which is measurable w.r.t.
\bm{G}^{e}
).
By Lemmas
32
and
33
,
\bm{g}_{k}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\bm{I}_{N})
is independent of
\bm{G}^{e}
, hence independent of

\bm{w}_{\tau}^{e}
. Therefore
\bm{g}_{k}^{\top}\bm{w}_{\tau}^{e}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
and

\|\bm{g}_{k}\|=\sqrt{N}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{N})
, so

|\bm{a}_{k}^{\top}\bm{w}_{\tau}^{e}|=\frac{|\bm{g}_{k}^{\top}\bm{w}_{\tau}^{e}|}{\|\bm{g}_{k}\|}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{N})}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Hence
|\bm{a}_{k}^{\top}\bm{w}_{\tau}|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
for
k\in\{1,2\}
.

Lastly, we consider
\bm{a}_{0}=\bm{1}_{N}/\sqrt{N}
.
Let
\bm{\Pi}_{\delta}
be the spectral projector of
\bm{G}\bm{G}^{\top}
onto interval

(\lambda_{\tau}-\delta,\lambda_{\tau}+\delta)
for some fixed and small
\delta>0
.
On the event that the outlier is simple,
\bm{\Pi}_{\delta}=\bm{w}_{\tau}\bm{w}_{\tau}^{\top}
, so

|\bm{a}_{0}^{\top}\bm{w}_{\tau}|^{2}=\bm{a}_{0}^{\top}\bm{\Pi}_{\delta}\,\bm{a}_{0}=-\frac{1}{2\pi i}\oint_{\Gamma}\bm{a}_{0}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{L}(z)\bm{a}_{0}\,dz,\qquad\bm{Q}_{L}(z):=\big(\bm{G}\bm{G}^{\top}-z\bm{I}_{N}\big)^{-1},

where
\Gamma:=\{z\in\mathbb{C}:|z-\lambda_{\tau}|=\delta/2\}
and
\bm{a}_{0}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{L}(z)\bm{a}_{0}
is well-defined on
\Gamma
. Following the proof of Lemma
36
we can apply Theorem 2.10 in
[
28
]
to get: uniformly for
z\in\Gamma
,

\bm{a}_{0}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{L}(z)\bm{a}_{0}=s(z)+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

where
s(z)=\tilde{m}_{\mu}(z)
is analytic in a neighborhood of
\lambda_{\tau}
containing
\Gamma
.
Therefore
\oint_{\Gamma}s(z)\,dz=0
and we obtain
|\bm{a}_{0}^{\top}\bm{w}_{\tau}|^{2}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.
∎

Lemma 41

(Master equation for
additive
outliers of
\bm{K}^{\sharp}
)
.

Fix
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
such that

\lambda\notin\mathrm{supp}({\mu})\cup\{0\}.

(D.67)

Define
\bm{Q}_{L}(\lambda)=(\bm{G}\bm{G}^{\top}-\lambda\bm{I}_{N})^{-1}
and

\bm{Q}_{R}(\lambda)=(\bm{K}_{0}-\lambda\bm{I}_{n})^{-1}
.
Let us define three
3\times 3
matrices as

\bm{A}(\lambda):=\big(\bm{a}_{k}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{L}(\lambda)\bm{a}_{\ell}\big)_{k,\ell=0}^{2},\qquad\bm{B}(\lambda):=\big(\bm{b}_{k}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{R}(\lambda)\bm{b}_{\ell}\big)_{k,\ell=0}^{2},

(D.68)

and
\bm{\Theta}:=\mathop{\mathrm{}}{diag}(\sqrt{\beta_{0}},\sqrt{\beta_{1}},\sqrt{\beta_{2}})
.
Then
\lambda
is an outlier eigenvalue of
\bm{K}^{\sharp}
if

\det\Big(\bm{I}_{3}-\lambda\,\bm{\Theta}\,\bm{A}(\lambda)\,\bm{\Theta}\cdot\bm{B}(\lambda)\Big)=0.

(D.69)

Moreover, (
D.69
) characterizes
all
outlier eigenvalues of
\bm{K}^{\sharp}
, induced by
\{{\bm{a}}_{k},{\bm{b}}_{k}\}_{k=0}^{2}
,
that lie in any compact set
\mathcal{D}\subset\mathbb{C}
satisfying (
D.67
).

Proof.

Notice that from the last part of Lemma
39
, we know all the entries of
\bm{A}(\lambda)
and
\bm{B}(\lambda)
are well-defined and finite, even if
\lambda=\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)}\notin\mathrm{supp}({\mu})\cup\{0\}
where
\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)}
is characterized by Lemma
29
.

Define

\bm{H}^{\sharp}:=\begin{pmatrix}\bm{0}&\bm{Y}^{\sharp}\\
\bm{Y}^{\sharp\top}&\bm{0}\end{pmatrix},\qquad\bm{H}_{0}:=\begin{pmatrix}\bm{0}&\bm{G}\\
\bm{G}^{\top}&\bm{0}\end{pmatrix}.

If
\rho\neq 0
, then
\rho\in\operatorname{spec}(\bm{H}^{\sharp})
iff
\rho^{2}\in\operatorname{spec}(\bm{K}^{\sharp})
.
Set
\lambda:=\rho^{2}
.
We now consider the low rank matrix
\bm{P}=\bm{A}_{0}\bm{\Theta}\bm{B}_{0}^{\top}
where

\bm{A}_{0}:=[\bm{a}_{0},\bm{a}_{1},\bm{a}_{2}]\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times 3}
and

\bm{B}_{0}:=[\bm{b}_{0},\bm{b}_{1},\bm{b}_{2}]\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times 3}
.
Then

\bm{H}^{\sharp}=\bm{H}_{0}+\bm{\Delta},\qquad\bm{\Delta}:=\begin{pmatrix}\bm{0}&\bm{P}\\
\bm{P}^{\top}&\bm{0}\end{pmatrix}.

Factor
\bm{\Delta}=\bm{U}\bm{C}\bm{U}^{\top}
with

\bm{U}:=\begin{pmatrix}\bm{A}_{0}&\bm{0}\\
\bm{0}&\bm{B}_{0}\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{(N+n)\times 6},\qquad\bm{C}:=\begin{pmatrix}\bm{0}&\bm{\Theta}\\
\bm{\Theta}&\bm{0}\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{6\times 6}.

Under (
D.67
),
\rho\notin\operatorname{spec}(\bm{H}_{0})
so
(\bm{H}_{0}-\rho\bm{I})
is invertible and

\det(\bm{H}^{\sharp}-\rho\bm{I})=\det(\bm{H}_{0}-\rho\bm{I})\,\det\Big(\bm{I}_{6}+\bm{C}\,\bm{U}^{\top}(\bm{H}_{0}-\rho\bm{I})^{-1}\bm{U}\Big).

Hence
\rho\in\operatorname{spec}(\bm{H}^{\sharp})\setminus\operatorname{spec}(\bm{H}_{0})
iff the
6\times 6
determinant above vanishes.
For
\lambda=\rho^{2}\notin\operatorname{spec}(\bm{K}_{0})\cup\operatorname{spec}(\bm{G}\bm{G}^{\top})
we have

(\bm{H}_{0}-\rho\bm{I})^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}-\rho(\bm{G}\bm{G}^{\top}-\lambda\bm{I})^{-1}&\bm{G}(\bm{K}_{0}-\lambda\bm{I})^{-1}\\
(\bm{K}_{0}-\lambda\bm{I})^{-1}\bm{G}^{\top}&-\rho(\bm{K}_{0}-\lambda\bm{I})^{-1}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}-\rho\,\bm{Q}_{L}(\lambda)&\bm{G}\bm{Q}_{R}(\lambda)\\
\bm{Q}_{R}(\lambda)\bm{G}^{\top}&-\rho\,\bm{Q}_{R}(\lambda)\end{pmatrix}.

Using the identity
\bm{Q}_{L}(\lambda)\bm{G}=\bm{G}\bm{Q}_{R}(\lambda)
, the
6\times 6
determinant reduces
(by an explicit Schur complement) to the equivalent
3\times 3
condition

\det\Big(\bm{I}_{3}-\lambda\,\bm{\Theta}\,\bm{A}_{0}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{L}(\lambda)\bm{A}_{0}\,\bm{\Theta}\cdot\bm{B}_{0}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{R}(\lambda)\bm{B}_{0}\Big)=0,

which is exactly (
D.69
).
The final uniform statement over
\mathcal{D}
follows because all steps are analytic in
\lambda
on the domain
(
D.67
).
∎

Lemma 42

(Scalar additive outlier equations)
.

Let
\lambda\in\mathcal{D}
and
s=s(\lambda)
satisfy (
D.33
).
Then the determinant equation (
D.69
) reduces to three scalar equations:

1-\beta_{k}\,T(s)=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\qquad k\in\{0,1,2\}.

(D.70)

Equivalently, each additive spike of strength
\beta\in\{\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\beta_{2}\}
produces a separated outlier eigenvalue at

\lambda=z(s)+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)\quad\text{for any real }s\text{ with }z^{\prime}(s)>0\text{ satisfying }\ \beta\,T(s)=1.

Proof.

Fix
\lambda\in\mathcal{D}
and let
s=s(\lambda)
.
By Lemma
36
, using
{\bm{a}}_{0}=\bm{1}/\sqrt{N}
,
{\bm{a}}_{1}={\bm{g}}_{1}/\|{\bm{g}}_{1}\|
,
{\bm{a}}_{2}={\bm{g}}_{2}/\|{\bm{g}}_{2}\|
,
we have

\bm{A}_{k\ell}(\lambda)={\bm{a}}_{k}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{L}(\lambda){\bm{a}}_{\ell}=\delta_{k\ell}\,s+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\qquad k,\ell\in\{0,1,2\}.

Thus
\bm{A}(\lambda)=s\,\bm{I}_{3}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.

By Lemma
38
and orthogonality
{\bm{v}}_{k}^{\top}{\bm{u}}=0
,
{\bm{v}}_{k}^{\top}{\bm{m}}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
,
we obtain

\bm{B}_{k\ell}(\lambda)={\bm{b}}_{k}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{R}(\lambda){\bm{b}}_{\ell}=\delta_{k\ell}\,m_{\mu}(\lambda)+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\qquad k,\ell\in\{0,1,2\}.

Thus
\bm{B}(\lambda)=m_{\mu}(\lambda)\,\bm{I}_{3}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.

Plugging the above approximations of
\bm{A}(\lambda)
and
\bm{B}(\lambda)
into (
D.69
) gives

\det\Big(\bm{I}_{3}-\lambda\,s\,m_{\mu}(\lambda)\,\mathop{\mathrm{}}{diag}(\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\beta_{2})\Big)=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

so
\lambda
satisfies at least one of the following equations

1-\beta_{k}\,\lambda\,s\,m_{\mu}(\lambda)=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

for
k=0,1,2.

By Lemmas
18
and
19
,
\lambda sm_{\mu}(\lambda)=T(s)
, proving (
D.70
).
∎

Lemma 43

(Eigenvector alignment for additive outliers)
.

Fix a distinct spike strength value
\beta_{\star}\in\{\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\beta_{2}\}
, and define

J_{\star}:=\{k\in\{0,1,2\}:\beta_{k}=\beta_{\star}\},\qquad r_{\star}:=|J_{\star}|.

Let

\bm{\Pi}_{B,\star}:=\sum_{k\in J_{\star}}{\bm{b}}_{k}{\bm{b}}_{k}^{\top},\qquad\bm{\Pi}_{A,\star}:=\sum_{k\in J_{\star}}{\bm{a}}_{k}{\bm{a}}_{k}^{\top}.

Assume that
s_{\star}\in\mathbb{R}
satisfies

\beta_{\star}T(s_{\star})=1,\qquad z^{\prime}(s_{\star})>0,\qquad\lambda_{\star}:=z(s_{\star})\in\mathbb{R}\setminus(\mathrm{supp}({\mu})\cup\{0\}).

Then, there exists a sufficiently small
\delta>0
with interval
I_{\delta}=(\lambda_{\star}-\delta,\lambda_{\star}+\delta)\subset\mathbb{R}\setminus(\mathrm{supp}({\mu})\cup\{0\})
such that the spectral projector
\widehat{}\bm{P}_{\star}
of
\bm{K}^{\sharp}
onto interval
I_{\delta}
and the corresponding spectral projector
\widehat{}\bm{P}_{\star}^{(L)}
of
\bm{Y}^{\sharp}\bm{Y}^{\sharp\top}
onto
I_{\delta}
satisfy:

\operatorname{Tr}\!\bigl(\widehat{}\bm{P}_{\star}\bm{\Pi}_{B,\star}\bigr)=r_{\star}\cdot\gamma_{R}(s_{\star})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

(D.71)

and

\operatorname{Tr}\!\bigl(\widehat{}\bm{P}_{\star}^{(L)}\bm{\Pi}_{A,\star}\bigr)=r_{\star}\cdot\gamma_{L}(s_{\star})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

(D.72)

where

\gamma_{R}(s):=\frac{m_{\mu}(z(s))\,z^{\prime}(s)}{\beta_{\star}T^{\prime}(s)}=-\frac{T(s)}{\beta_{\star}s^{2}T^{\prime}(s)}\,\varphi(s)=-\frac{\varphi(s)}{\beta_{\star}^{\,2}s^{2}T^{\prime}(s)}.

(D.73)

and

\gamma_{L}(s):=\frac{s\,z^{\prime}(s)}{\beta_{\star}T^{\prime}(s)}=-\frac{z(s)}{\beta_{\star}T^{\prime}(s)}\,\varphi(s).

(D.74)

In particular, if
r_{\star}=1
and
J_{\star}=\{k\}
, then the unique outlier eigenvalue inside
I_{\delta}

is simple, and if
\widehat{\bm{v}}_{k}
denotes its unit right eigenvector, then

|\langle\widehat{\bm{v}}_{k},{\bm{b}}_{k}\rangle|^{2}=\gamma_{R}(s_{\star})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(D.75)

Proof.

By Lemma
42
, every additive outlier of
\bm{K}^{\sharp}
in
\mathcal{D}
is asymptotically
described by a real solution
s
of

\beta\,T(s)=1,\qquad z^{\prime}(s)>0,\qquad\lambda=z(s),

for some
\beta\in\{\beta_{0},\beta_{1},\beta_{2}\}
.
Since
z^{\prime}(s_{\star})>0
, Lemma
18
implies that
\lambda_{\star}=z(s_{\star})
lies in a connected
component of
\mathbb{R}\setminus\mathrm{supp}({\mu})
, and
s(\lambda)
is real-analytic near
\lambda_{\star}
.

Define

f_{\star}(\lambda):=1-\beta_{\star}T(s(\lambda)).

Then
f_{\star}(\lambda_{\star})=0
, and

f_{\star}^{\prime}(\lambda_{\star})=-\beta_{\star}T^{\prime}(s_{\star})\,\frac{ds}{d\lambda}(\lambda_{\star})=-\beta_{\star}\frac{T^{\prime}(s_{\star})}{z^{\prime}(s_{\star})}.

(D.76)

Hence
f_{\star}^{\prime}(\lambda_{\star})\neq 0
whenever
T^{\prime}(s_{\star})\neq 0
, so
f_{\star}
has a simple zero at
\lambda_{\star}
. Now choose a sufficient small constant
\delta>0
such that:

I_{\delta}\subset\mathcal{D}
;
\lambda_{\star}
is the only zero of
f_{\star}
inside
I_{\delta}
;
and for every
\beta\neq\beta_{\star}
, the function
1-\beta T(s(\lambda))
does not vanish on
I_{\delta}
.
By Lemma
42
, the eigenvalues of
\bm{K}^{\sharp}
inside
I_{\delta}
are precisely the
outlier cluster associated with the repeated block
J_{\star}
; its total multiplicity is
r_{\star}
.

Let

\bm{R}^{\sharp}(\lambda):=(\bm{K}^{\sharp}-\lambda\bm{I}_{n})^{-1},\qquad\bm{Q}_{R}(\lambda):=(\bm{K}_{0}-\lambda\bm{I}_{n})^{-1},\qquad\bm{Q}_{L}(\lambda):=(\bm{G}\bm{G}^{\top}-\lambda\bm{I}_{N})^{-1}.

Recall from Lemma
41
that

\bm{Y}^{\sharp}=\bm{G}+\bm{P},\qquad\bm{P}=\bm{A}_{0}\bm{\Theta}\bm{B}_{0}^{\top},\qquad\bm{A}_{0}=[{\bm{a}}_{0},{\bm{a}}_{1},{\bm{a}}_{2}],\quad\bm{B}_{0}=[{\bm{b}}_{0},{\bm{b}}_{1},{\bm{b}}_{2}].

Introduce the linearizations

\bm{H}^{\sharp}:=\begin{pmatrix}0&\bm{Y}^{\sharp}\\
\bm{Y}^{\sharp\top}&0\end{pmatrix},\qquad\bm{H}_{0}:=\begin{pmatrix}0&\bm{G}\\
\bm{G}^{\top}&0\end{pmatrix},

and

\bm{U}:=\begin{pmatrix}\bm{A}_{0}&0\\
0&\bm{B}_{0}\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{(N+n)\times 6},\qquad\bm{C}:=\begin{pmatrix}0&\bm{\Theta}\\
\bm{\Theta}&0\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{6\times 6},

so that
\bm{H}^{\sharp}=\bm{H}_{0}+\bm{U}\bm{C}\bm{U}^{\top}
.

Fix
\rho
with
\rho^{2}=\lambda
, and set

\bm{R}_{0}(\rho):=(\bm{H}_{0}-\rho\bm{I}_{N+n})^{-1},\qquad\bm{S}(\rho):=\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{R}_{0}(\rho)\bm{U}.

By the Woodbury identity,

\displaystyle(\bm{H}^{\sharp}-\rho\bm{I})^{-1}

\displaystyle=\bm{R}_{0}(\rho)-\bm{R}_{0}(\rho)\bm{U}\bm{C}\bigl(\bm{I}_{6}+\bm{S}(\rho)\bm{C}\bigr)^{-1}\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{R}_{0}(\rho).

(D.77)

Multiplying (
D.77
) on the left by
\bm{U}^{\top}
and on the right by
\bm{U}
, we obtain the exact identity

\bm{U}^{\top}(\bm{H}^{\sharp}-\rho\bm{I})^{-1}\bm{U}=\bigl(\bm{I}_{6}+\bm{S}(\rho)\bm{C}\bigr)^{-1}\bm{S}(\rho),

(D.78)

since

\bm{S}-\bm{S}\bm{C}(\bm{I}_{6}+\bm{S}\bm{C})^{-1}\bm{S}=(\bm{I}_{6}+\bm{S}\bm{C})^{-1}\bm{S}.

Now write
\lambda=\rho^{2}
. Using the block formula for
(\bm{H}_{0}-\rho\bm{I})^{-1}
from Lemma
41
, we have

\bm{S}(\rho)=\begin{pmatrix}-\rho\,\bm{A}(\lambda)&\bm{X}(\lambda)\\
\bm{X}(\lambda)^{\top}&-\rho\,\bm{B}(\lambda)\end{pmatrix},\qquad\bm{X}(\lambda):=\bm{A}_{0}^{\top}\bm{G}\bm{Q}_{R}(\lambda)\bm{B}_{0}=\bm{A}_{0}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{L}(\lambda)\bm{G}\bm{B}_{0}.

Here
\bm{A}(\lambda)
and
\bm{B}(\lambda)
are defined by (
D.68
).
Also, the lower-right block of
(\bm{H}^{\sharp}-\rho\bm{I})^{-1}
equals

-\rho\,(\bm{K}^{\sharp}-\lambda\bm{I}_{n})^{-1}=-\rho\,\bm{R}^{\sharp}(\lambda).

Therefore, taking the lower-right
3\times 3
block in (
D.78
), there exists
a
3\times 3
matrix-valued function
\bm{H}_{B}(\lambda)
, analytic on a neighborhood of
\mathcal{D}
, such that

\bm{B}_{0}^{\top}\bm{R}^{\sharp}(\lambda)\bm{B}_{0}=\bm{B}(\lambda)\,\bm{M}(\lambda)^{-1}+\bm{H}_{B}(\lambda),\qquad\bm{M}(\lambda):=\bm{I}_{3}-\lambda\,\bm{\Theta}\,\bm{A}(\lambda)\,\bm{\Theta}\,\bm{B}(\lambda).

(D.79)

Hence every pole of
\bm{B}_{0}^{\top}\bm{R}^{\sharp}(\lambda)\bm{B}_{0}
inside
\mathcal{D}
comes from a zero of
\det\bm{M}(\lambda)
,
that is, from an additive outlier of
\bm{K}^{\sharp}
.

By Lemmas
36
and
38
, uniformly for
\lambda\in\mathcal{D}\subset\mathbb{C}
,

\bm{A}(\lambda)=s(\lambda)\bm{I}_{3}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\qquad\bm{B}(\lambda)=m_{\mu}(\lambda)\bm{I}_{3}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Therefore, uniformly on
\mathcal{D}
, we have

\bm{M}(\lambda)=\mathop{\mathrm{}}{diag}\!\bigl(1-\beta_{0}T(s(\lambda)),\,1-\beta_{1}T(s(\lambda)),\,1-\beta_{2}T(s(\lambda))\bigr)+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

(D.80)

because of
\lambda s(\lambda)m_{\mu}(\lambda)=T(s(\lambda))
.

Restrict now to the block
J_{\star}
.
and let

E_{\star}:=\sum_{k\in J_{\star}}\mathbf{e}_{k}\mathbf{e}_{k}^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3},

where
\mathbf{e}_{0},\mathbf{e}_{1},\mathbf{e}_{2}
are the standard basis vectors of
\mathbb{R}^{3}
.
Since
\beta_{k}=\beta_{\star}
for all
k\in J_{\star}
,

E_{\star}\bm{M}(\lambda)E_{\star}=\bigl(1-\beta_{\star}T(s(\lambda))\bigr)E_{\star}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\qquad\lambda\in\mathcal{D}.

Hence,

E_{\star}\bm{M}(\lambda)^{-1}E_{\star}=\frac{1}{1-\beta_{\star}T(s(\lambda))}\,E_{\star}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\qquad\lambda\in\mathcal{D}.

(D.81)

Combining (
D.79
),
\bm{B}(\lambda)=m_{\mu}(\lambda)\bm{I}_{3}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, and
(
D.81
), we obtain

E_{\star}\bm{B}_{0}^{\top}\bm{R}^{\sharp}(\lambda)\bm{B}_{0}E_{\star}=\frac{m_{\mu}(\lambda)}{1-\beta_{\star}T(s(\lambda))}\,E_{\star}+\bm{H}_{\star}(\lambda)+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\qquad\lambda\in\mathcal{D},

(D.82)

where
\bm{H}_{\star}(\lambda):=E_{\star}\bm{H}_{B}(\lambda)E_{\star}
is analytic on a neighborhood of
\mathcal{D}
.

By the contour formula for spectral projectors,

\widehat{}\bm{P}_{\star}=-\frac{1}{2\pi i}\oint_{\Gamma_{\star}}\bm{R}^{\sharp}(\lambda)\,d\lambda,

for some circle
\Gamma_{\star}\subset\mathcal{D}
with
\Gamma_{\star}\cap\mathbb{R}=\{\lambda_{\star}-\delta,\lambda_{\star}+\delta\}
.
Therefore

\displaystyle\operatorname{Tr}\!\bigl(\widehat{}\bm{P}_{\star}\bm{\Pi}_{B,\star}\bigr)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\pi i}\oint_{\Gamma_{\star}}\operatorname{Tr}\!\bigl(\bm{\Pi}_{B,\star}\bm{R}^{\sharp}(\lambda)\bigr)\,d\lambda

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\pi i}\oint_{\Gamma_{\star}}\operatorname{Tr}\!\bigl(E_{\star}\bm{B}_{0}^{\top}\bm{R}^{\sharp}(\lambda)\bm{B}_{0}E_{\star}\bigr)\,d\lambda.

(D.83)

Insert (
D.82
). The analytic term
\bm{H}_{\star}(\lambda)
has zero contour integral, and

\operatorname{Tr}(E_{\star})=r_{\star}
, so

\operatorname{Tr}\!\bigl(\widehat{}\bm{P}_{\star}\bm{\Pi}_{B,\star}\bigr)=-\frac{1}{2\pi i}\oint_{\Gamma_{\star}}\frac{r_{\star}\,m_{\mu}(\lambda)}{1-\beta_{\star}T(s(\lambda))}\,d\lambda+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(D.84)

Now
1-\beta_{\star}T(s(\lambda))
has a simple zero at
\lambda_{\star}
, and by (
D.76
)

\frac{d}{d\lambda}\Bigl(1-\beta_{\star}T(s(\lambda))\Bigr)\Big|_{\lambda=\lambda_{\star}}=-\beta_{\star}\frac{T^{\prime}(s_{\star})}{z^{\prime}(s_{\star})}.

Hence,
-\mathrm{Res}_{\lambda=\lambda_{\star}}\frac{m_{\mu}(\lambda)}{1-\beta_{\star}T(s(\lambda))}=\frac{m_{\mu}(\lambda_{\star})\,z^{\prime}(s_{\star})}{\beta_{\star}T^{\prime}(s_{\star})}
and Cauchy Residue Theorem implies that

\operatorname{Tr}\!\bigl(\widehat{}\bm{P}_{\star}\bm{\Pi}_{B,\star}\bigr)=r_{\star}\cdot\frac{m_{\mu}(\lambda_{\star})\,z^{\prime}(s_{\star})}{\beta_{\star}T^{\prime}(s_{\star})}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

which is (
D.71
).
By Lemma
19
,
T(s)=z(s)\,s\,m_{\mu}(z(s))
and
m_{\mu}(z(s))=\frac{T(s)}{z(s)s}.

Substituting into (
D.71
),

\gamma_{R}(s)=\frac{T(s)}{\beta_{\star}z(s)s}\cdot\frac{z^{\prime}(s)}{T^{\prime}(s)}=-\frac{\varphi(s_{\star})}{\beta_{\star}^{\,2}s_{\star}^{2}T^{\prime}(s_{\star})}

since
\varphi(s):=-\frac{s\,z^{\prime}(s)}{z(s)}

and
\beta_{\star}T(s_{\star})=1
.

The proof on the left singular vectors is identical, replacing
\bm{K}^{\sharp}
by
\bm{Y}^{\sharp}\bm{Y}^{\sharp\top}
,
\bm{B}_{0}
by
\bm{A}_{0}
,
and
\bm{B}(\lambda)
by
\bm{A}(\lambda)
. Since
\bm{A}(\lambda)=s(\lambda)\bm{I}_{3}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, the numerator becomes

s(\lambda)
instead of
m_{\mu}(\lambda)
, yielding

\operatorname{Tr}\!\bigl(\widehat{}\bm{P}_{\star}^{(L)}\bm{\Pi}_{A,\star}\bigr)=r_{\star}\cdot\frac{s_{\star}z^{\prime}(s_{\star})}{\beta_{\star}T^{\prime}(s_{\star})}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

If
r_{\star}=1
, then
\widehat{}\bm{P}_{\star}=\widehat{\bm{v}}_{k}\widehat{\bm{v}}_{k}^{\top}
, so

|\langle\widehat{\bm{v}}_{k},{\bm{b}}_{k}\rangle|^{2}=\operatorname{Tr}(\widehat{}\bm{P}_{\star}\Pi_{B,\star})=\gamma_{R}(s_{\star})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

proving (
D.75
).
∎

Notice that from Lemmas
28
and
29
, we observe that the mean spike generated by
\beta_{0}
are asymptotically identified with the separated covariance outlier of
\bm{K}_{0}
generated by
\tau
if they exist. The following lemma shows that this mean spike is collision-safe and produces a stable additive outlier of
\bm{K}^{\sharp}
with non-trivial overlap on the mean spike direction
{\bm{b}}_{0}
, even in the presence of the linear outlier when
\beta_{\rm lin}>0
.

Lemma 44
.

Assume
c_{\sigma}\neq 0
,
z^{\prime}(s_{\rm un})>0
, and, when
\beta_{\rm lin}>0
and
z^{\prime}(s_{\rm lin})>0
, assume
s_{\rm un}\neq s_{\rm lin}
. Let

\lambda_{\rm un}:=z(s_{\rm un}).

Then there exists a sufficiently small deterministic constant
\delta>0
such that, with probability tending to one,

I_{\rm un,\delta}:=(\lambda_{\rm un}-\delta,\lambda_{\rm un}+\delta)

(D.85)

is disjoint from
\mathrm{supp}({\mu})\cup\{0\}
and from the linear outlier location
z(s_{\rm lin})
(when present), and
\bm{K}^{\sharp}
has exactly one
additive mean-spike
eigenvalue in
I_{\rm un,\delta}
.

More precisely, let
\Gamma_{\rm un}
be the positively oriented circle of radius
\delta/2
centered at
\lambda_{\rm un}
and write, for
z\in\Gamma_{\rm un}
,

\bm{Q}_{R}(z)=\frac{\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}^{\top}}{\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)}-z}+\bm{Q}_{R}^{\perp}(z),\qquad\bm{Q}_{L}(z)=\frac{\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\tau}\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\tau}^{\top}}{\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)}-z}+\bm{Q}_{L}^{\perp}(z),

where
\bm{Q}_{R}^{\perp},\bm{Q}_{L}^{\perp}
are the projected resolvents from Lemma
39
. Then uniformly on
\Gamma_{\rm un}
,

\displaystyle\bm{A}^{\perp}(z):=\bigl({\bm{a}}_{k}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{L}^{\perp}(z){\bm{a}}_{\ell}\bigr)_{k,\ell=0}^{2}

\displaystyle=s(z)\bm{I}_{3}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

(D.86)

\displaystyle\bm{B}^{\perp}(z):=\bigl({\bm{b}}_{k}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{R}^{\perp}(z){\bm{b}}_{\ell}\bigr)_{k,\ell=0}^{2}

\displaystyle=m_{\mu}(z)\bm{I}_{3}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

(D.87)

and therefore

\det\Big(\bm{I}_{3}-z\,\bm{\Theta}\,\bm{A}^{\perp}(z)\,\bm{\Theta}\,\bm{B}^{\perp}(z)\Big)=\bigl(1-\tau T(s(z))\bigr)\bigl(1-\beta_{\rm lin}T(s(z))\bigr)^{2}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

(D.88)

uniformly on
\Gamma_{\rm un}
. Let
\widehat{}\bm{P}_{{\rm mean},{\rm un}}
be the spectral projector of
\bm{K}^{\sharp}
onto the unique additive outlier in
I_{\rm un,\delta}
. Then,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Tr}\bigl(\widehat{}\bm{P}_{{\rm mean},{\rm un}}\,{\bm{b}}_{0}{\bm{b}}_{0}^{\top}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\gamma_{R}(s_{\rm un})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

(D.89)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Tr}\bigl(\widehat{}\bm{P}_{{\rm mean},{\rm un}}\,{\bm{u}}{\bm{u}}^{\top}\bigr)

\displaystyle=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(D.90)

Proof.

Since
z^{\prime}(s_{\rm un})>0
, Lemma
28
implies
\tau>\tau_{\rm crit}
and hence
\bm{K}_{0}
has a separated covariance outlier
\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)}=\lambda_{\rm un}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
by Lemma
29
. Choose
\delta>0
so small that the interval (
D.85
) is disjoint from the bulk and from the linear outlier location when the latter exists.

On the contour
\Gamma_{\rm un}
, the raw resolvents are well-defined. By the spectral decomposition of
\bm{K}_{0}
and Lemma
39
,

\bm{Q}_{R}(z)=\frac{\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}^{\top}}{\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)}-z}+\bm{Q}_{R}^{\perp}(z),\qquad\bm{Q}_{L}(z)=\frac{\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\tau}\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\tau}^{\top}}{\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)}-z}+\bm{Q}_{L}^{\perp}(z),

and the pole parts contribute only
o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
to quadratic forms against the spike directions because

\max_{k\in\{0,1,2\}}|{\bm{b}}_{k}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\qquad\max_{k\in\{0,1,2\}}|{\bm{a}}_{k}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{w}}_{\tau}|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

by Lemma
40
, while
|\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)}-z|\asymp 1
uniformly on
\Gamma_{\rm un}
. Therefore the same proofs as in Lemmas
36
and
38
, now applied to the projected resolvents, yield (
D.86
) and (
D.87
). The factorization (
D.88
) then follows exactly as in Lemma
42
.

Because
s_{\rm un}
is the unique solution of
\tau T(s)=1
on the connected component containing
I_{\rm un,\delta}
, because
z^{\prime}(s_{\rm un})>0
, and because the linear factor is bounded away from zero on
\Gamma_{\rm un}
by the separation assumption
s_{\rm un}\neq s_{\rm lin}
, Rouché’s theorem implies that the projected determinant has exactly one zero inside
\Gamma_{\rm un}
, hence
\bm{K}^{\sharp}
has exactly one additive mean-spike eigenvalue in
I_{\rm un,\delta}
.

Finally, (
D.89
) is the same computation as in Lemma
43
with the projected resolvents replacing the raw ones. Also, (
D.90
) follows from the same contour representation with the test vector
{\bm{u}}
, because the only possible residue is proportional to quadratic forms of the type
{\bm{u}}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{R}^{\perp}(z){\bm{b}}_{0}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
and because
{\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{b}}_{0}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
by Lemma
34
.
∎

Lemma 45
.

Assume
c_{\sigma}\neq 0
,
z^{\prime}(s_{\rm un})>0
, and, when
\beta_{\rm lin}>0
and
z^{\prime}(s_{\rm lin})>0
, assume
s_{\rm un}\neq s_{\rm lin}
. Let
\lambda_{\rm un}=z(s_{\rm un})
and let
I_{\rm un,\delta}
be as in Lemma
44
. Then, with probability tending to one,
\bm{K}^{\sharp}
has exactly two eigenvalues in
I_{\rm un,\delta}
: one inherited from the covariance outlier of
\bm{K}_{0}
and one produced by the additive mean spike. If
\widehat{}\bm{P}_{\rm un}^{\sharp}
denotes the spectral projector onto this two-dimensional cluster, then

\displaystyle\|\widehat{}\bm{P}_{\rm un}^{\sharp}{\bm{u}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\varphi(s_{\rm un})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

(D.91)

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|\widehat{}\bm{P}_{\rm un}^{\sharp}\frac{{\bm{m}}}{\|{\bm{m}}\|}}}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\varphi(s_{\rm un})}{\tau^{2}s_{\rm un}^{2}T^{\prime}(s_{\rm un})}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

(D.92)

\displaystyle{\bm{u}}^{\top}\widehat{}\bm{P}_{\rm un}^{\sharp}\frac{{\bm{m}}}{\|{\bm{m}}\|}

\displaystyle=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(D.93)

Proof.

By Lemma
39
, the separated covariance outlier of
\bm{K}_{0}
persists under the rank-three additive perturbation and yields one eigenvalue of
\bm{K}^{\sharp}
inside
I_{\rm un,\delta}
. By Lemma
44
, the additive mean spike yields exactly one further eigenvalue in the same interval. Since the linear spikes are excluded from
I_{\rm un,\delta}
by construction, there are exactly two eigenvalues in the interval. The
{\bm{u}}
-alignment comes from the inherited covariance direction. Indeed, by Lemma
29
,

|\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}^{\top}{\bm{u}}|^{2}=\varphi(s_{\rm un})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Since
\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}
lies asymptotically inside
\mathrm{Range}(\widehat{}\bm{P}_{\rm un}^{\sharp})
by Lemma
39
, we obtain (
D.91
); the additive mean-spike contribution to the
{\bm{u}}
-mass is negligible by (
D.90
).
Similarly, the
{\bm{m}}/\|{\bm{m}}\|
-alignment comes entirely from the additive mean-spike component: the inherited covariance eigenvector contributes only
o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
in the
{\bm{b}}_{0}={\bm{m}}/\|{\bm{m}}\|
direction by (
D.23
) in Lemma
29
together with Lemma
34
, while Lemma
44
yields the main term (
D.92
). The cross term (
D.93
) follows by combining the two preceding decoupling statements.
∎

Proposition 46

(No label alignment with any separated outliers)
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
3
, let
\widehat{}\bm{V}_{\rm out}
denote the orthogonal projector onto the direct sum of all separated outlier eigenvalues of
\bm{K}
. Then

\frac{1}{n}\|\widehat{}\bm{V}_{\rm out}{\bm{y}}\|^{2}=\frac{1}{n}{\bm{y}}^{\top}\widehat{}\bm{V}_{\rm out}{\bm{y}}\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}0.

(D.94)

Proof.

By Proposition
31
, it suffices to prove the statement for
\bm{K}^{\sharp}
. In the separated regime covered by Theorem
3
, the outlier projector of
\bm{K}^{\sharp}
decomposes as the orthogonal sum of the uninformative cluster projector
\widehat{}\bm{P}_{\rm un}^{\sharp}
from Lemma
45
and, when present, the linear cluster projector
\widehat{}\bm{P}_{\rm lin}^{\sharp}=\widehat{}\bm{P}_{\star}
from Lemma
43
by setting
\lambda_{\star}=z(s_{\rm lin})
and
r_{\star}=2
in the latter. Hence,

\widehat{}\bm{V}_{\rm out}^{\sharp}=\widehat{}\bm{P}_{\rm un}^{\sharp}+\widehat{}\bm{P}_{\rm lin}^{\sharp}.

It is therefore enough to show

\frac{1}{n}{\bm{y}}^{\top}\widehat{}\bm{P}_{\rm un}^{\sharp}{\bm{y}}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\qquad\frac{1}{n}{\bm{y}}^{\top}\widehat{}\bm{P}_{\rm lin}^{\sharp}{\bm{y}}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Set
\bar{\bm{y}}:={\bm{y}}/\sqrt{n}
. By the XOR construction,

\bar{\bm{y}}^{\top}{\bm{u}}=0,\qquad\bar{\bm{y}}^{\top}{\bm{v}}_{1}=0,\qquad\bar{\bm{y}}^{\top}{\bm{v}}_{2}=0.

Moreover Lemma
34
gives
\bar{\bm{y}}^{\top}{\bm{m}}/\|{\bm{m}}\|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. Recall that
s_{\rm cov}=-1/\Lambda_{\tau}
,
s_{\rm mean}=T^{-1}(1/\beta_{\rm mean})
and
s_{\rm lin}=T^{-1}(1/\beta_{\rm lin})
. Denote
\lambda_{0}=z(s_{\rm mean})
,
\lambda_{1}=\lambda_{2}=z(s_{\rm lin})
, and
\lambda_{3}=z(s_{\rm cov})
. Lemma
28
implies
\lambda_{0}=\lambda_{3}
.

For the linear outliers, consider
\lambda=\lambda_{1}=\lambda_{2}
. Then, by Lemmas
39
and
42
, there exists a small constant
\delta>0
such that for sufficiently large
n
,
\textnormal{dist}(I_{\delta},\mathrm{supp}({\mu})\cup\{0\})>\delta
and all eigenvalues of
\bm{K}^{\sharp}
inside
I_{\delta}
will converge to
\lambda
in probability, where
I_{\delta}:=(\lambda-\delta,\lambda+\delta).

Let
\widehat{}\bm{V}_{\lambda}^{\sharp}
be the spectral projector of
\bm{K}^{\sharp}
onto outlier cluster in
I_{\delta}
,
and let
\Gamma_{\lambda}\subset\mathbb{C}
be a small contour enclosing that cluster and no other part of
\operatorname{spec}(\bm{K}^{\sharp})
.
Then

\widehat{}\bm{V}_{\lambda}^{\sharp}=-\frac{1}{2\pi i}\oint_{\Gamma_{\lambda}}(\bm{K}^{\sharp}-z\bm{I})^{-1}\,dz.

Hence

\bar{\bm{y}}^{\top}\widehat{}\bm{V}_{\lambda}^{\sharp}\bar{\bm{y}}=-\frac{1}{2\pi i}\oint_{\Gamma_{\lambda}}\bar{\bm{y}}^{\top}(\bm{K}^{\sharp}-z\bm{I})^{-1}\bar{\bm{y}}\,dz.

(D.95)

Now use the same linearization trick as in Lemma
43
. Recall that
\bm{A}_{0}=[{\bm{a}}_{0},{\bm{a}}_{1},{\bm{a}}_{2}]
,
\bm{B}_{0}=[{\bm{b}}_{0},{\bm{b}}_{1},{\bm{b}}_{2}]
, and
\bm{\Theta}=\mathop{\mathrm{}}{diag}(\sqrt{\beta_{0}},\sqrt{\beta_{1}},\sqrt{\beta_{2}})
.
Then one obtains the exact projected-resolvent representation

(\bm{K}^{\sharp}-z\bm{I})^{-1}=\bm{Q}_{R}(z)+\bm{Q}_{R}(z)\bm{B}_{0}\,\bm{S}(z)\,\bm{B}_{0}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{R}(z),

(D.96)

where
\bm{S}(z)=z\bm{\Theta}\bm{A}(z)\bm{\Theta}\bigl(\bm{I}_{3}-z\bm{\Theta}\bm{A}(z)\bm{\Theta}\bm{B}(z)\bigr)^{-1}
is a
3\times 3
matrix-valued meromorphic function, and all poles inside
\mathcal{D}
come from the additive outlier equation described by Lemma
42
. Hence,
\bm{S}(z)
is analytic and bounded on
\Gamma_{\lambda}
.
Insert (
D.96
) into (
D.95
). The possible pole term is

\bar{\bm{y}}^{\top}\widehat{}\bm{V}_{\lambda}^{\sharp}\bar{\bm{y}}=-\frac{1}{2\pi i}\oint_{\Gamma_{\lambda}}\bar{\bm{y}}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{R}(z)\bar{\bm{y}}\,dz-\frac{1}{2\pi i}\oint_{\Gamma_{\lambda}}\bar{\bm{y}}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{R}(z)\bm{B}_{0}\,\bm{S}(z)\,\bm{B}_{0}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{R}(z)\bar{\bm{y}}dz.

By (
D.55
),
\bar{\bm{y}}\bm{Q}_{R}(z)\bar{\bm{y}}=m_{\mu}(z)+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
and

\bm{B}_{0}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{R}(z)\bar{\bm{y}}=\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{b}}_{0}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{R}(z)\bar{\bm{y}}\\
{\bm{b}}_{1}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{R}(z)\bar{\bm{y}}\\
{\bm{b}}_{2}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{R}(z)\bar{\bm{y}}\end{bmatrix}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

uniformly on the contour
z\in\Gamma_{\lambda}
.
Since
m_{\mu}(z)
is analytic on
\Gamma_{\lambda}
, its contour integral vanishes. Hence
\bar{\bm{y}}^{\top}\widehat{}\bm{P}_{\rm lin}^{\sharp}\bar{\bm{y}}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

For the uninformative eigenvalues, let
\Gamma_{\rm un}
be the contour from Lemma
44
. Using the resolvent decomposition into the covariance pole plus the projected resolvent,

\bm{Q}_{R}(z)=\frac{\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}^{\top}}{\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)}-z}+\bm{Q}_{R}^{\perp}(z),

we note first that
\bar{\bm{y}}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
by (
D.23
) in Lemma
29
, because
\bar{\bm{y}}^{\top}{\bm{u}}=0
. Hence the covariance pole contributes only
o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
to the contour integral for
\bar{\bm{y}}^{\top}\widehat{}\bm{P}_{\rm un}^{\sharp}\bar{\bm{y}}
. Similarly as the linear outlier case, we have that uniformly on
\Gamma_{\rm un}
,
\bar{\bm{y}}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{R}^{\perp}(z)\bar{\bm{y}}=m_{\mu}(z)+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),
and
\bar{\bm{y}}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{R}^{\perp}(z){\bm{b}}_{k}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\quad k=0,1,2,
so the contour integral of the analytic main term vanishes and the remainder is
o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. Therefore
\bar{\bm{y}}^{\top}\widehat{}\bm{P}_{\rm un}^{\sharp}\bar{\bm{y}}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.

Combining the two clusters yields (
D.94
) for
\bm{K}^{\sharp}
, and hence also for
\bm{K}
.
∎

D.5
Proof of Theorem
3

By Proposition
31
,
\|\bm{K}-\bm{K}^{\sharp}\|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. Therefore it suffices to analyze
\bm{K}^{\sharp}
cluster by cluster.

Item
(i)
follows from Lemma
29
(i): the bulk ESD of
\bm{K}_{0}
is
\mu
, and the finite-rank perturbation from
\bm{K}_{0}
to
\bm{K}^{\sharp}
does not change the limiting ESD.

For item
(ii)
, Lemma
28
shows that the covariance parameter and the mean parameter are identical on the uninformative branch:

s_{\rm cov}=s_{\rm un}=s_{\rm mean}.

Thus the covariance and mean mechanisms collide at the same first-order location
\lambda_{\rm un}=z(s_{\rm un})
. Lemma
45
proves that
\bm{K}^{\sharp}
has exactly two eigenvalues in a small interval around
\lambda_{\rm un}
and that the corresponding spectral projector satisfies (
4.2
)–(
4.4
). By Proposition
31
, the same statements hold for
\bm{K}
.

Next, for item
(iii)
, the linear spikes are away from the covariance outlier by
s_{\rm un}\neq s_{\rm lin}
. Therefore Lemmas
42
and
43
apply directly with
\beta_{1}=\beta_{2}=\beta_{\rm lin}
, giving the two linear outlier eigenvalues at
z(s_{\rm lin})
and the projector-overlap formula (
4.5
).

Finally, item
(iv)
is exactly Proposition
46
.

Remark
.

The deterministic limits in (
4.2
)–(
4.4
) are projector-level statements. If
\widehat{\bm{v}}_{{\rm un},1},\widehat{\bm{v}}_{{\rm un},2}
is any orthonormal basis of
\mathrm{Range}(\widehat{}\bm{P}_{\rm un})
, then

\sum_{j=1}^{2}|\langle\widehat{\bm{v}}_{{\rm un},j},{\bm{u}}\rangle|^{2}=\|\widehat{}\bm{P}_{\rm un}{\bm{u}}\|^{2},\qquad\sum_{j=1}^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\widehat{\bm{v}}_{{\rm un},j},\frac{{\bm{m}}}{\|{\bm{m}}\|}}}\right\rangle}}\right|^{2}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|\widehat{}\bm{P}_{\rm un}\frac{{\bm{m}}}{\|{\bm{m}}\|}}}\right\|^{2}.

Therefore only the
sum
of the squared overlaps is canonical at first order. If numerically one observes a balanced basis inside the two-dimensional cluster, then each individual eigenvector carries asymptotically one half of the projector mass in the corresponding direction.

Appendix E
Kernel Spectral Clustering for XOR

In this section, we present the performance of the kernel spectral clustering for XOR in the finite-SNR proportional limit regime given by Theorem
4
. Results in this section on kernel spectral clustering are applications of the main results of
[
19
]
.

Recall the four-component XOR Gaussian mixture data
\bm{X}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times n}
defined in (
3.5
), with cluster means

{\bm{\mu}}_{1}=\frac{r}{\sqrt{d}}\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{1}_{d/2}\\
-\mathbf{1}_{d/2}\end{pmatrix},\quad{\bm{\mu}}_{2}=\frac{r}{\sqrt{d}}\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{1}_{d/2}\\
\mathbf{1}_{d/2}\end{pmatrix},\quad{\bm{\mu}}_{3}=-{\bm{\mu}}_{1},\quad{\bm{\mu}}_{4}=-{\bm{\mu}}_{2},

and common covariance
\bm{C}=\mathbf{I}_{d}
. Define the matrix of cluster means
\bm{M}:=[{\bm{\mu}}_{1},{\bm{\mu}}_{2},{\bm{\mu}}_{3},{\bm{\mu}}_{4}]\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times 4}
, and cluster-indicator matrix
\bm{J}

\bm{J}\in\{0,1\}^{n\times 4},\qquad J_{i,a}=1\Leftrightarrow{\bm{x}}_{i}\text{ belongs to cluster }a,

so that each cluster
a\in[4]
has size
n_{a}=n/4
. For an affinity function
f:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
, define the Kernel matrix
\bm{K}^{(f)}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
as

(\bm{K}^{(f)})_{ij}=f\Bigl(\|{\bm{x}}_{i}-{\bm{x}}_{j}\|^{2}\Bigr),\qquad i,j\in[n]

(E.1)

We consider the normalized Laplacian, which is defined as

\bm{L}:=n\,\bm{D}^{-1/2}\bm{K}^{(f)}\,\bm{D}^{-1/2},\quad\bm{D}=\mathop{\mathrm{}}{diag}(\bm{K}^{(f)}\mathbf{1}_{n})

where
\bm{D}=\mathop{\mathrm{}}{diag}(\bm{K}^{(f)}\mathbf{1}_{n})
is the degree matrix. Similar to the results on the spectra of the CK in Section
4
, the analysis of the eigenvalues on
\bm{L}
relies on a LE for
\bm{L}
. We refer to Theorem 1 of
[
19
]
for more details.

It is not difficult to prove that under Assumption
2
, the pairwise distances between distinct samples
{\bm{x}}_{i}
and
{\bm{x}}_{j}
of the XOR dataset concentrate around
\tau=2
(see Lemma
11
for details). The core of the analysis of
\bm{L}
then relies upon a careful entry-wise Taylor expansion of the Kernel
\bm{K}^{(f)}
around the limiting value
\tau
. We therefore make the following assumption on the kernel function
f:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
.

Assumption 47
.

Let
\tau=2
. The kernel function
f\in C^{3}
is three-times differentiable in some neighborhood of
\tau
. Further,
f(\tau)>0
and
f^{\prime}(\tau)\neq 0
.

Theorem 48
.

Suppose
\mathrm{SNR}

r^{2}=\Theta(1)
and let Assumptions
2
and
47
hold.

(i)

(BBP for two linear informative outliers.)
If
r^{2}>2\sqrt{\psi^{-1}}
, then there exists two isolated eigenvalues of
\bm{L}
, which are asymptotically approximated by

-\frac{2f^{\prime}(\tau)}{f(\tau)}\rho+\frac{f(0)-f(\tau)+\tau f^{\prime}(\tau)}{f(\tau)}

(E.2)

where

\rho=\psi\bigl(1+\frac{r^{2}}{2}\bigr)+\frac{2+r^{2}}{r^{2}}

(E.3)

(ii)

(BBP for additional non-informative outlier.)
Let
\ell_{+}=\frac{5f^{\prime}(\tau)}{4f(\tau)}-\frac{f^{\prime\prime}(\tau)}{f^{\prime}(\tau)}
. If, in addition,
(1-\ell_{+})^{2}>\psi^{-1}
, then there is an additional corresponding isolated eigenvalue of
\bm{L}
, which is approximated by

\lambda_{+}:=-\frac{2f^{\prime}(\tau)}{f(\tau)}\rho_{+}+\frac{f(0)-f(\tau)+\tau f^{\prime}(\tau)}{f(\tau)},

in probability, where
\rho_{+}=\ell_{+}\bigl(\psi-\frac{1}{1-\ell_{+}}\bigr)
.
These and the trivial eigenvalue
n
represent all isolated eigenvalues of
\bm{L}
.

(iii)

(Eigenvector alignment.)
Moreover, if
\rho
is such an isolated eigenvalue of
\bm{L}
corresponding to (
E.2
), let
\bm{\Pi}_{\rho}
denote the orthogonal projection onto the two-dimensional eigenspace corresponding to
\rho
. Then we have

\frac{1}{d}\bm{J}^{\top}\bm{\Pi}_{\rho}\bm{J}\rightarrow\frac{1}{1+\frac{r^{2}}{2}}\Bigl(\psi-\frac{4}{r^{4}}\Bigr)\frac{r^{2}}{16}\begin{pmatrix}1&-1\\
-1&1\end{pmatrix}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{2}

(E.4)

where
\otimes
denotes the Kronecker product. In particular, in probability

\frac{1}{d}{\bm{y}}^{\top}\bm{\Pi}_{\rho}{\bm{y}}\rightarrow 0.

where
{\bm{y}}
denotes the XOR-labels.

Remark
.

The trivial eigenvalue
n
, and the possible additional isolated eigenvalue of
\bm{L}
corresponding to
\lambda_{+}
carry no relevant clustering information. The other non-trivial isolating eigenvalues of
\bm{L}
characterized in Theorem
48
(i) turn out to be the only eigenvalues that carry relevant clustering information. Similar to the eigenvectors of the CK matrix, the last statement of Theorem
48
implies that the outlier eigenvectors of the Laplacian
\bm{L}
are asymptotically orthogonal to the XOR-labels
{\bm{y}}
.

E.1
Proof of Kernel Spectral Clustering

Proof of Theorem
48
.

The common covariance
\bm{C}=\mathbf{I}_{d}
of the XOR dataset greatly simplifies the analysis of the spectrum of
\bm{L}
. We apply Corollary 1 from
[
19
]
, which gives the isolating eigenvalues of
\bm{L}
in terms of the isolated eigenvalues of
\bm{C}+\bm{M}\mathcal{D}(c)\bm{M}^{\top}
, where
\bm{M}=[{\bm{\mu}}_{1},{\bm{\mu}}_{2},{\bm{\mu}}_{3},{\bm{\mu}}_{4}]
,
{\bm{c}}=(\frac{1}{4})_{\alpha=1}^{4}
is the vector of class proportions, and
\mathcal{D}(\cdot)
is the diagonal operator.
For the XOR dataset, this matrix becomes

\bm{C}+\bm{M}\mathcal{D}(c)\bm{M}^{\top}=\mathbf{I}_{d}+\frac{1}{4}\sum_{k=1}^{4}{\bm{\mu}}_{k}{\bm{\mu}}_{k}^{\top}=\mathbf{I}_{d}+\frac{r^{2}}{d}\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{1}_{\frac{d}{2}}\mathbf{1}_{\frac{d}{2}}^{\top}&\mathbf{0}\\
\mathbf{0}&\mathbf{1}_{\frac{d}{2}}\mathbf{1}_{\frac{d}{2}}^{\top}\end{bmatrix}

(E.5)

The eigenvalues of (
E.5
) are
\ell=1+\frac{r^{2}}{2}
, of multiplicity two, and the rest are equal to 1. The phase transition in Corollary 1 can therefore be restated as:

|\ell-1|=\frac{r^{2}}{2}>\sqrt{\psi^{-1}}.

In this case, by Corollary 1 of
[
19
]
the isolating eigenvalues of
\bm{L}
are asymptotically well approximated via

-\frac{2f^{\prime}(\tau)}{f(\tau)}\rho+\frac{f(0)-f(\tau)+\tau f^{\prime}(\tau)}{f(\tau)},

in probability, where
\rho=\psi\bigl(1+\frac{r^{2}}{2}\bigr)+\frac{2+r^{2}}{r^{2}}
.
Similarly, define
\ell_{+}=\frac{5f^{\prime}(\tau)}{4f(\tau)}-\frac{f^{\prime\prime}(\tau)}{f^{\prime}(\tau)}
. The additional isolating eigenvalue of
\bm{L}
corresponding to
\rho_{+}=\ell_{+}\bigl(\psi-\frac{1}{1-\ell_{+}}\bigr)
emerges if

(1-\ell_{+})^{2}>\psi^{-1}

in which case, the corresponding outlier eigenvalue of
\bm{L}
converges to, in probability,

-2\frac{f^{\prime}(\tau)}{f(\tau)}\rho_{+}+\frac{f(0)-f(\tau)+\tau f^{\prime}(\tau)}{f(\tau)}.

(E.6)

For eigenvector alignment of the eigenvalues relevant for clustering, we observe the two-dimensional eigenspace corresponding to
\ell=1+\frac{r^{2}}{2}
is spanned by the unit-norm eigenvectors
\{\Upsilon_{1},\Upsilon_{2}\}
, where

\Upsilon_{1}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{d/2}}\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{1}_{d/2}\\
\mathbf{0}_{d/2}\end{pmatrix},\qquad\Upsilon_{2}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{d/2}}\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{0}_{d/2}\\
\mathbf{1}_{d/2}\end{pmatrix}.

(E.7)

Let
\Upsilon_{\rho}=(\Upsilon_{1},\Upsilon_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times 2}
be the column-concatenated eigenvectors, then we apply Corollary 2 of
[
19
]
to obtain

\tfrac{1}{d}\bm{J}^{\top}\bm{\Pi}_{\rho}\bm{J}=\frac{1}{\ell}\Bigl(\psi-\frac{1}{(1-\ell)^{2}}\Bigr)\mathcal{D}(c)\bm{M}^{\top}\Upsilon_{\rho}\Upsilon_{\rho}^{\top}\bm{M}\mathcal{D}(c)+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

(E.8)

Or equivalently,

\frac{1}{d}\bm{J}^{\top}\bm{\Pi}_{\rho}\bm{J}\rightarrow\frac{r^{2}}{16+8r^{2}}\Bigl(\psi-\frac{4}{r^{4}}\Bigr)\begin{pmatrix}1&-1\\
-1&1\end{pmatrix}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{2}.

(E.9)

in probability. We conclude the proof by observing the XOR-labels
{\bm{y}}
can be written as
{\bm{y}}=\bm{J}\begin{pmatrix}1\\
-1\\
1\\
-1\end{pmatrix}.

Applying this to (
E.9
), we have

\frac{1}{d}{\bm{y}}^{\top}\bm{\Pi}_{\rho}{\bm{y}}\rightarrow\frac{r^{2}}{16+8r^{2}}\bigl(\psi-\frac{4}{r^{4}}\bigr)\begin{pmatrix}1\\
-1\\
1\\
-1\end{pmatrix}^{\top}\begin{pmatrix}1&-1\\
-1&1\\
\end{pmatrix}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{2}\begin{pmatrix}1\\
-1\\
1\\
-1\end{pmatrix}=0.

in probability. Hence, we complete the proof of this theorem.
∎

Appendix F
Proof for Large-SNR Regime

This appendix gives the proof for Theorem
5
.
Throughout this section, we denote

r=r_{0}n^{1/4},\qquad r_{0}\in(0,\infty),\qquad\theta_{\mathrm{SNR}}:=r\sqrt{\frac{n}{2d}},

and

\tau:=\frac{c_{\sigma}^{2}}{2}\psi,\qquad\kappa_{0}:=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\theta_{\mathrm{SNR}}^{4}}{n}=\frac{r_{0}^{4}\psi^{2}}{4},\qquad\alpha:=c_{\sigma}\sqrt{\kappa_{0}},\qquad\beta:=\beta_{\rm quad}:=\alpha^{2}=c_{\sigma}^{2}\kappa_{0}.

(F.1)

We write

\overline{{\bm{y}}}:=\frac{{\bm{y}}}{\sqrt{n}},\qquad\bm{P}_{\rm lin}:={\bm{v}}_{1}{\bm{v}}_{1}^{\top}+{\bm{v}}_{2}{\bm{v}}_{2}^{\top},\qquad\bm{P}_{\perp}:=\bm{I}_{n}-\bm{P}_{\rm lin},\qquad\bm{K}_{\perp}:=\bm{P}_{\perp}\bm{K}\bm{P}_{\perp}.

Recall the functions
z,\varphi,T
from (
3.10
)–(
3.11
).
For
\beta>0
, define the label factor

F_{y}(s):=1-\beta T(s),\qquad s_{y}:=T^{-1}(1/\beta),

(F.2)

and define the non-label coupled factor

F_{\rm nl}(s):=1-2(\tau+\beta)T(s)+\tau(\tau+\beta)T(s)^{2}.

(F.3)

When
c_{\sigma}\neq 0
, equivalently
\tau>0
and
\beta>0
, the two
possible
T
-values for the non-label factor are

t_{\pm}:=\frac{1\pm\sqrt{\beta/(\tau+\beta)}}{\tau},\qquad s_{\pm}:=T^{-1}(t_{\pm}).

(F.4)

The factor
F_{\rm nl}
is the joint contribution of three effects: the
population-covariance spike of the centered null matrix, the mean spike, and the
large-SNR quadratic
{\bm{u}}
-channel. In particular, the original covariance
outlier studied in Lemma
29
location
s_{\tau}=T^{-1}(1/\tau)=-1/\Lambda_{\tau}
, when it exists, is
not a separate location once
\beta>0
; it is shifted into the two roots

s_{\pm}
.

F.1
Projected QE Model

Lemma 49

(Projected QE reduction)
.

Let

\bm{K}_{{\rm QE},\perp}:=(\bm{Y}_{\rm QE}\bm{P}_{\perp})^{\top}(\bm{Y}_{\rm QE}\bm{P}_{\perp}).

Then

\|\bm{Y}\bm{P}_{\perp}-\bm{Y}_{\rm QE}\bm{P}_{\perp}\|=O_{\prec}(n^{-1/4}),\qquad\|\bm{K}_{\perp}-\bm{K}_{{\rm QE},\perp}\|=O_{\prec}(n^{-1/4}).

Moreover,

\bm{Y}_{\rm QE}\bm{P}_{\perp}=\bm{Y}_{0}\bm{P}_{\perp}+\frac{\theta_{\mathrm{SNR}}^{2}c_{\sigma}}{2\sqrt{N}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\bm{g}}_{1}^{\odot 2}{\bm{v}}_{1}^{\odot 2\top}+{\bm{g}}_{2}^{\odot 2}{\bm{v}}_{2}^{\odot 2\top}}}\right),

(F.5)

because
\bm{P}_{\perp}{\bm{v}}_{k}=0
and, in the XOR basis,

\bm{P}_{\perp}{\bm{v}}_{k}^{\odot 2}={\bm{v}}_{k}^{\odot 2}
.

Proof.

The QE error follows from Proposition
8
after multiplication on
the right by the contraction
\bm{P}_{\perp}
. The linear QE term vanishes after the
projection because its right directions are
{\bm{v}}_{1},{\bm{v}}_{2}
. The projected
quadratic term has bounded operator norm: indeed,

\|{\bm{g}}_{k}^{\odot 2}\|=O_{\prec}(\sqrt{N})
,

\|{\bm{v}}_{k}^{\odot 2}\|=O(n^{-1/2})
, and

\theta_{\mathrm{SNR}}^{2}=O(n^{1/2})
. Also

\|\bm{Y}_{0}\bm{P}_{\perp}\|=O_{\prec}(1)
by the null CK bounds used in
Appendix
D
. Hence

\|\bm{Y}_{\rm QE}\bm{P}_{\perp}\|=O_{\prec}(1)
, and

\displaystyle\|\bm{K}_{\perp}-\bm{K}_{{\rm QE},\perp}\|\leq\bigl(\|\bm{Y}\bm{P}_{\perp}\|+\|\bm{Y}_{\rm QE}\bm{P}_{\perp}\|\bigr)\|\bm{Y}\bm{P}_{\perp}-\bm{Y}_{\rm QE}\bm{P}_{\perp}\|=O_{\prec}(n^{-1/4}).

This proves the lemma.
∎

Lemma 50

(Large-SNR finite-rank form)
.

Let

\bm{G}:=\bm{Y}_{0}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\mathbf{1}_{N}{\bm{m}}^{\top},\qquad{\bm{a}}_{0}:=\frac{\mathbf{1}_{N}}{\sqrt{N}},\qquad{\bm{b}}_{0}:=\frac{\bm{P}_{\perp}{\bm{m}}}{\|\bm{P}_{\perp}{\bm{m}}\|}

when
\|\bm{P}_{\perp}{\bm{m}}\|>0
. Define the centered square vectors

\widetilde{\bm{g}}_{k}:={\bm{g}}_{k}^{\odot 2}-\mathbf{1}_{N},\qquad{\bm{h}}_{u}:=\widetilde{\bm{g}}_{1}+\widetilde{\bm{g}}_{2},\qquad{\bm{h}}_{y}:=\widetilde{\bm{g}}_{1}-\widetilde{\bm{g}}_{2},

and

{\bm{a}}_{u}:=\frac{{\bm{h}}_{u}}{\|{\bm{h}}_{u}\|},\qquad{\bm{a}}_{y}:=\frac{{\bm{h}}_{y}}{\|{\bm{h}}_{y}\|}.

Then, in operator norm,

\bm{Y}_{\rm QE}\bm{P}_{\perp}=\bm{G}\bm{P}_{\perp}+{\bm{a}}_{0}\bigl(\sqrt{\tau}\,{\bm{b}}_{0}^{\top}+\alpha\,{\bm{u}}^{\top}\bigr)+\alpha\,{\bm{a}}_{u}{\bm{u}}^{\top}+\alpha\,{\bm{a}}_{y}\overline{{\bm{y}}}^{\top}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(F.6)

Furthermore,

\|\bm{P}_{\perp}{\bm{m}}\|^{2}\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}\tau,\qquad{\bm{b}}_{0}^{\top}{\bm{u}}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\qquad{\bm{b}}_{0}^{\top}\overline{{\bm{y}}}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\qquad{\bm{u}}^{\top}\overline{{\bm{y}}}=0,

and the left directions
{\bm{a}}_{0},{\bm{a}}_{u},{\bm{a}}_{y}
are asymptotically orthonormal.

Proof.

The XOR identities are

{\bm{v}}_{1}^{\odot 2}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}({\bm{u}}+\overline{{\bm{y}}}),\qquad{\bm{v}}_{2}^{\odot 2}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}({\bm{u}}-\overline{{\bm{y}}}).

Therefore

{\bm{g}}_{1}^{\odot 2}{\bm{v}}_{1}^{\odot 2\top}+{\bm{g}}_{2}^{\odot 2}{\bm{v}}_{2}^{\odot 2\top}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\Bigl[({\bm{h}}_{u}+2\mathbf{1}_{N}){\bm{u}}^{\top}+{\bm{h}}_{y}\overline{{\bm{y}}}^{\top}\Bigr].

(F.7)

The deterministic term in (
F.7
) gives

\frac{\theta_{\mathrm{SNR}}^{2}c_{\sigma}}{2\sqrt{N}}\cdot\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}}\mathbf{1}_{N}{\bm{u}}^{\top}=\frac{\theta_{\mathrm{SNR}}^{2}c_{\sigma}}{\sqrt{n}}\,{\bm{a}}_{0}{\bm{u}}^{\top}=\alpha\,{\bm{a}}_{0}{\bm{u}}^{\top}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

because
\theta_{\mathrm{SNR}}^{2}c_{\sigma}/\sqrt{n}\to c_{\sigma}\sqrt{\kappa_{0}}=\alpha
and
\alpha^{2}=\beta
.
Similarly,

\frac{\theta_{\mathrm{SNR}}^{2}c_{\sigma}}{2\sqrt{N}\sqrt{n}}{\bm{h}}_{u}{\bm{u}}^{\top}=\alpha\,{\bm{a}}_{u}{\bm{u}}^{\top}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\qquad\frac{\theta_{\mathrm{SNR}}^{2}c_{\sigma}}{2\sqrt{N}\sqrt{n}}{\bm{h}}_{y}\overline{{\bm{y}}}^{\top}=\alpha\,{\bm{a}}_{y}\overline{{\bm{y}}}^{\top}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

since

N^{-1}\|{\bm{h}}_{u}\|^{2}=4+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\qquad N^{-1}\|{\bm{h}}_{y}\|^{2}=4+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\qquad N^{-1}{\bm{h}}_{u}^{\top}{\bm{h}}_{y}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

The null mean term is

\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\mathbf{1}_{N}(\bm{P}_{\perp}{\bm{m}})^{\top}={\bm{a}}_{0}\|\bm{P}_{\perp}{\bm{m}}\|{\bm{b}}_{0}^{\top}=\sqrt{\tau}\,{\bm{a}}_{0}{\bm{b}}_{0}^{\top}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

by Lemma
34
; the same argument as in that lemma, with
the deterministic vector
\overline{{\bm{y}}}
, gives

\overline{{\bm{y}}}^{\top}{\bm{m}}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. Since

{\bm{m}}
is also asymptotically orthogonal to
{\bm{v}}_{1},{\bm{v}}_{2},{\bm{u}}
, projection by

\bm{P}_{\perp}
does not change its norm at first order and

{\bm{b}}_{0}^{\top}{\bm{u}}={\bm{b}}_{0}^{\top}\overline{{\bm{y}}}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. Finally

{\bm{a}}_{0}^{\top}{\bm{a}}_{u}={\bm{a}}_{0}^{\top}{\bm{a}}_{y}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
because
{\bm{h}}_{u},{\bm{h}}_{y}
are centered,
and
{\bm{a}}_{u}^{\top}{\bm{a}}_{y}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
by the displayed covariance estimate.
∎

F.2
The Covariance Spike and the Projected Resolvents

Set

\bm{K}_{0}:=(\bm{G}\bm{P}_{\perp})^{\top}(\bm{G}\bm{P}_{\perp}).

(F.8)

The population covariance of the centered rows of
\bm{G}\bm{P}_{\perp}
has the same
rank-one spike
\tau{\bm{u}}{\bm{u}}^{\top}
as in Appendix
D
,
because
{\bm{u}}\perp{\bm{v}}_{1},{\bm{v}}_{2}
. Thus, conditional on
\bm{Z}
,

\bm{P}_{\perp}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{P}_{\perp}=\bm{P}_{\perp}\bm{\Sigma}_{0}\bm{P}_{\perp}+\tau{\bm{u}}{\bm{u}}^{\top}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

The finite-rank projection
\bm{P}_{\perp}
does not change the limiting ESD of the
bulk covariance, hence the limiting Gram law is still
\mu
.

Lemma 51

(Projected null covariance BBP)
.

The ESD of
\bm{K}_{0}
converges weakly in probability to
\mu
. If

\tau>\tau_{\rm crit}
, let
\Lambda_{\tau}
be the population outlier from
(
D.14
). If in addition
z^{\prime}(-1/\Lambda_{\tau})>0
, then
\bm{K}_{0}

has one separated outlier

\widehat{\lambda}_{\tau}^{(0)}=z\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\frac{1}{\Lambda_{\tau}}}}\right)+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

If
\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}
is its unit right eigenvector, then

|\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}^{\top}{\bm{u}}|^{2}\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}\varphi\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\frac{1}{\Lambda_{\tau}}}}\right).

For every deterministic unit vector
{\bm{b}}
with
{\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{u}}=o(1)
,

|\widehat{\bm{u}}_{\tau}^{\top}{\bm{b}}|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. In particular the covariance outlier is
asymptotically orthogonal to
{\bm{b}}_{0}
and
\overline{{\bm{y}}}
, but it is generally
not orthogonal to the additive quadratic right direction
{\bm{u}}
.

Proof.

This is Lemma
29
applied to the projected matrix.
The assumptions used there are unchanged by removing the two deterministic
directions
{\bm{v}}_{1},{\bm{v}}_{2}
, since
{\bm{u}},{\bm{b}}_{0},\overline{{\bm{y}}}
are orthogonal to

{\bm{v}}_{1},{\bm{v}}_{2}
up to
o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, and the removed subspace has fixed dimension.
The last statement follows from the general alignment formula
(
D.23
) in Lemma
29
.
∎

Lemma 52

(Resolvent limits retaining the covariance pole)
.

Let
D\subset\mathbb{C}\setminus(\mathrm{supp}({\mu})\cup\{0\})
be a compact spectral
domain. For
\lambda\in D
, set

\bm{Q}_{R}(\lambda):=(\bm{K}_{0}-\lambda\bm{I}_{n})^{-1},\qquad\bm{Q}_{L}(\lambda):=((\bm{G}\bm{P}_{\perp})(\bm{G}\bm{P}_{\perp})^{\top}-\lambda\bm{I}_{N})^{-1},\qquad s=s(\lambda):=\widetilde{m}_{\mu}(\lambda).

Let

\bm{A}:=[{\bm{a}}_{0},{\bm{a}}_{u},{\bm{a}}_{y}],\qquad\bm{B}:=[{\bm{b}}_{0},{\bm{u}},\overline{{\bm{y}}}].

Uniformly on compact subsets avoiding the possible pole
1-\tau T(s)=0
,

\bm{A}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{L}(\lambda)\bm{A}=s(\lambda)\bm{I}_{3}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

(F.9)

and

\bm{B}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{R}(\lambda)\bm{B}=m_{\mu}(\lambda)\begin{pmatrix}1&0&0\\
0&(1-\tau T(s(\lambda)))^{-1}&0\\
0&0&1\end{pmatrix}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(F.10)

The singular denominator in the
{\bm{u}}
-entry is the analytic signature of the
covariance spike of
\bm{K}_{0}
.

Proof.

The left-resolvent estimate is the same anisotropic law and leave-one-out
argument as Lemma
36
; the possible covariance outlier does
not contribute to the displayed quadratic forms because its left singular vector
is asymptotically orthogonal to
{\bm{a}}_{0},{\bm{a}}_{u},{\bm{a}}_{y}
, as in
Lemma
40
. The random vectors
{\bm{a}}_{u},{\bm{a}}_{y}
are handled by
conditioning on the independent copy of the centered bulk and applying the
Hanson–Wright concentration
[
36
]
.

For the right resolvent, the deterministic equivalent in Lemma
38
for a general sample
covariance matrix gives

{\bm{b}}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{R}(\lambda){\bm{b}}^{\prime}={\bm{b}}^{\top}\bigl(-\lambda s\bm{\Sigma}-\lambda\bm{I}_{n}\bigr)^{-1}{\bm{b}}^{\prime}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Using

\bm{\Sigma}=\bm{\Sigma}_{0}+\tau{\bm{u}}{\bm{u}}^{\top}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
and the Sherman–Morrison formula,
with

\bm{A}_{0}(\lambda):=-\lambda(\bm{I}_{n}+s\bm{\Sigma}_{0})
, we get

\bigl(\bm{A}_{0}-\lambda s\tau{\bm{u}}{\bm{u}}^{\top}\bigr)^{-1}=\bm{A}_{0}^{-1}+\frac{\lambda s\tau}{1-\lambda s\tau\,{\bm{u}}^{\top}\bm{A}_{0}^{-1}{\bm{u}}}\bm{A}_{0}^{-1}{\bm{u}}{\bm{u}}^{\top}\bm{A}_{0}^{-1}.

The isotropic law for
\bm{\Sigma}_{0}
yields

{\bm{u}}^{\top}\bm{A}_{0}^{-1}{\bm{u}}=m_{\mu}(\lambda)+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\qquad\lambda sm_{\mu}(\lambda)=T(s).

Thus

{\bm{u}}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{R}(\lambda){\bm{u}}=\frac{m_{\mu}(\lambda)}{1-\tau T(s(\lambda))}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

For
{\bm{b}}\in\{{\bm{b}}_{0},\overline{{\bm{y}}}\}
,
{\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{u}}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, so the
rank-one Sherman–Morrison term is negligible and

{\bm{b}}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{R}(\lambda){\bm{b}}=m_{\mu}(\lambda)+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
; all cross terms are

o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
by the same argument.
∎

F.3
Master Determinant Equation for Coupled Outliers

Define the deterministic
3\times 3
coefficient matrix

\bm{C}:=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{\tau}&\alpha&0\\
0&\alpha&0\\
0&0&\alpha\end{pmatrix},

(F.11)

so that the finite-rank perturbation in (
F.6
) is

\bm{P}:=\bm{A}\bm{C}\bm{B}^{\top}={\bm{a}}_{0}(\sqrt{\tau}\,{\bm{b}}_{0}^{\top}+\alpha\,{\bm{u}}^{\top})+\alpha\,{\bm{a}}_{u}{\bm{u}}^{\top}+\alpha\,{\bm{a}}_{y}\overline{{\bm{y}}}^{\top}.

Let

\bm{Y}^{\sharp}:=\bm{G}\bm{P}_{\perp}+\bm{P},\qquad\bm{K}^{\sharp}:=\bm{Y}^{\sharp\top}\bm{Y}^{\sharp}.

By Lemmas
49
and
50
, we know that

\|\bm{K}_{\perp}-\bm{K}^{\sharp}\|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(F.12)

Lemma 53

(Coupled outlier determinant)
.

Let
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}\setminus(\mathrm{supp}({\mu})\cup\{0\})
and

s=s(\lambda)=\widetilde{m}_{\mu}(\lambda)
. Away from the possible covariance
pole of
\bm{K}_{0}
, the relative determinant for additive outliers of

\bm{K}^{\sharp}
satisfies

\displaystyle D_{\rm rel}(\lambda)

\displaystyle:=\det\Bigl(\bm{I}_{3}-\lambda\,\bm{C}^{\top}(\bm{A}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{L}(\lambda)\bm{A})\bm{C}(\bm{B}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{R}(\lambda)\bm{B})\Bigr)

(F.13)

\displaystyle=\bigl(1-\beta T(s)\bigr)\frac{1-2(\tau+\beta)T(s)+\tau(\tau+\beta)T(s)^{2}}{1-\tau T(s)}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Consequently, after multiplying by the covariance-spike factor of
\bm{K}_{0}
, the
full outlier equation for
\bm{K}^{\sharp}
outside
\mathrm{supp}({\mu})
is

\bigl(1-\beta T(s)\bigr)\bigl[1-2(\tau+\beta)T(s)+\tau(\tau+\beta)T(s)^{2}\bigr]=0.

(F.14)

Proof.

Similarly as Lemma
41
, the finite-rank additive determinant obtained from the linearization

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&\bm{Y}^{\sharp}\\
\bm{Y}^{\sharp\top}&0\end{smallmatrix}}}\right)

gives

D_{\rm rel}(\lambda)=\det\Bigl(\bm{I}_{3}-\lambda\,\bm{C}^{\top}(\bm{A}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{L}(\lambda)\bm{A})\bm{C}(\bm{B}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{R}(\lambda)\bm{B})\Bigr).

Substituting (
F.9
)–(
F.10
)
and
T(s)=\lambda sm_{\mu}(\lambda)
gives

D_{\rm rel}(\lambda)=\det\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{I}_{3}-T(s)\,\bm{C}^{\top}\bm{C}\begin{pmatrix}1&0&0\\
0&(1-\tau T(s))^{-1}&0\\
0&0&1\end{pmatrix}}}\right)+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Since

\bm{C}^{\top}\bm{C}=\begin{pmatrix}\tau&\alpha\sqrt{\tau}&0\\
\alpha\sqrt{\tau}&2\beta&0\\
0&0&\beta\end{pmatrix},

the label coordinate factors out and gives
1-\beta T(s)
. The remaining

2\times 2
determinant equals

\displaystyle\det\begin{pmatrix}1-\tau T&-\alpha\sqrt{\tau}\,T(1-\tau T)^{-1}\\
-\alpha\sqrt{\tau}\,T&1-2\beta T(1-\tau T)^{-1}\end{pmatrix}=\frac{1-2(\tau+\beta)T+\tau(\tau+\beta)T^{2}}{1-\tau T}.

This proves (
F.13
).

The denominator
1-\tau T(s)
is not an extra root of
\bm{K}^{\sharp}
; it is the
pole of the resolvent of the base matrix
\bm{K}_{0}
at the covariance outlier.
In the characteristic determinant of
\bm{K}^{\sharp}
, the zero of

\det(\bm{K}_{0}-\lambda\bm{I})
at this covariance location is multiplied by the pole
of
D_{\rm rel}
. The product is governed by the numerator in
(
F.13
). Equivalently, an argument-principle count
on a contour around any separated cluster counts zeros of the numerator
(
F.14
), with algebraic multiplicity. This is the
same mechanism as in the collision-safe treatment of the
uninformative cluster in Appendix
D
.
∎

Lemma 54

(Cluster count and no-label property)
.

Let
\lambda_{\star}\in\mathbb{R}\setminus(\mathrm{supp}({\mu})\cup\{0\})
be a limiting
location generated by the roots of (
F.14
), and let

m_{\star}
be its multiplicity as in (
4.11
). Then

\bm{K}^{\sharp}
has exactly
m_{\star}
eigenvalues in a sufficiently small
interval around
\lambda_{\star}
. If the interval contains no label root, then
the associated spectral projector
\widehat{}\bm{P}_{\star}^{\sharp}
satisfies
\overline{{\bm{y}}}^{\top}\widehat{}\bm{P}_{\star}^{\sharp}\overline{{\bm{y}}}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

If the interval contains the label root
s_{y}
, then

\overline{{\bm{y}}}^{\top}\widehat{}\bm{P}_{\star}^{\sharp}\overline{{\bm{y}}}\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}\frac{m_{\mu}(z(s_{y}))z^{\prime}(s_{y})}{\beta T^{\prime}(s_{y})}.

Proof.

Choose a small contour
\Gamma_{\star}
enclosing
\lambda_{\star}
, disjoint from

\mathrm{supp}({\mu})
, and containing no other limiting candidate. On this contour the
deterministic factors in (
F.14
) are bounded away
from zero except for the roots inside the contour. Lemma
53

and Rouché’s theorem give exactly
m_{\star}
zeros of the finite-dimensional
outlier determinant, after the covariance-pole cancellation described above.
The linearization determinant is equivalent to the eigenvalue equation of

\bm{K}^{\sharp}
outside the bulk, so
\bm{K}^{\sharp}
has exactly
m_{\star}

eigenvalues in the interval.

For the projector statements, use the contour representation

\overline{{\bm{y}}}^{\top}\widehat{}\bm{P}_{\star}^{\sharp}\overline{{\bm{y}}}=-\frac{1}{2\pi i}\oint_{\Gamma_{\star}}\overline{{\bm{y}}}^{\top}(\bm{K}^{\sharp}-\lambda\bm{I})^{-1}\overline{{\bm{y}}}\,d\lambda.

The same Woodbury-linearization calculation used for additive outlier overlaps
shows that, uniformly on
\Gamma_{\star}
,

\overline{{\bm{y}}}^{\top}(\bm{K}^{\sharp}-\lambda\bm{I})^{-1}\overline{{\bm{y}}}=\frac{m_{\mu}(\lambda)}{1-\beta T(s(\lambda))}+H_{y}(\lambda)+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

(F.15)

where
H_{y}
is analytic in a neighborhood of the contour. The non-label block
does not appear in the leading singular term because the left direction

{\bm{a}}_{y}
and the right direction
\overline{{\bm{y}}}
are asymptotically orthogonal
to the non-label directions
{\bm{a}}_{0},{\bm{a}}_{u}
and
{\bm{b}}_{0},{\bm{u}}
.

If the contour contains no label root, the leading term in
(
F.15
) is analytic and its contour integral is zero,
giving the no-label statement. If the contour contains
s_{y}
, then

1-\beta T(s(\lambda))
has a simple zero at
\lambda_{y}=z(s_{y})
, and

\frac{d}{d\lambda}\{1-\beta T(s(\lambda))\}\Big|_{\lambda=\lambda_{y}}=-\beta\frac{T^{\prime}(s_{y})}{z^{\prime}(s_{y})}.

Thus

-\operatorname*{Res}_{\lambda=\lambda_{y}}\frac{m_{\mu}(\lambda)}{1-\beta T(s(\lambda))}=\frac{m_{\mu}(z(s_{y}))z^{\prime}(s_{y})}{\beta T^{\prime}(s_{y})}.

This proves the label projector formula. Using

T(s)=z(s)sm_{\mu}(z(s))
,
\beta T(s_{y})=1
, and

\varphi(s)=-sz^{\prime}(s)/z(s)
, the same quantity equals

-\varphi(s_{y})/(\beta^{2}s_{y}^{2}T^{\prime}(s_{y}))
.
∎

F.4
Proof of Theorem
5

The bulk statement follows by comparing the full CK matrix with its QE model in
normalized trace norm. Proposition
8
gives

\|\bm{Y}-\bm{Y}_{\rm QE}\|=O_{\prec}(n^{-1/4})
, hence

\|\bm{Y}-\bm{Y}_{\rm QE}\|_{F}=O_{\prec}(n^{1/4})
. Since

n^{-1}\|\bm{Y}\|_{F}^{2}
and
n^{-1}\|\bm{Y}_{\rm QE}\|_{F}^{2}
are tight, the normalized the Schatten-1 norm difference of the Gram matrices satisfies

\frac{1}{n}\|\bm{Y}^{\top}\bm{Y}-\bm{Y}_{\rm QE}^{\top}\bm{Y}_{\rm QE}\|_{1}\leq\frac{\|\bm{Y}\|_{F}+\|\bm{Y}_{\rm QE}\|_{F}}{n}\|\bm{Y}-\bm{Y}_{\rm QE}\|_{F}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Thus
\bm{K}
and
\bm{Y}_{\rm QE}^{\top}\bm{Y}_{\rm QE}
have the same weak ESD
limit. Moreover,

\bm{Y}_{\rm QE}^{\top}\bm{Y}_{\rm QE}
differs from
\bm{G}^{\top}\bm{G}
by a
finite-rank matrix, and the limiting ESD is the null limit
\mu
.

For the diverging linear outliers, the linear QE term is

\bm{T}_{1}:=\frac{\theta_{\mathrm{SNR}}b_{\sigma}}{\sqrt{N}}({\bm{g}}_{1}{\bm{v}}_{1}^{\top}+{\bm{g}}_{2}{\bm{v}}_{2}^{\top}).

Since
N^{-1}\|{\bm{g}}_{k}\|^{2}\to 1
and
N^{-1}{\bm{g}}_{1}^{\top}{\bm{g}}_{2}\to 0
,

\bm{T}_{1}^{\top}\bm{T}_{1}=b_{\sigma}^{2}\theta_{\mathrm{SNR}}^{2}({\bm{v}}_{1}{\bm{v}}_{1}^{\top}+{\bm{v}}_{2}{\bm{v}}_{2}^{\top})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(\theta_{\mathrm{SNR}}^{2}).

All other terms have operator norm
O_{\prec}(1)
after separating the linear
part, whereas
\theta_{\mathrm{SNR}}^{2}=(r_{0}^{2}\psi/2)n^{1/2}(1+o(1))
. Weyl’s
inequality gives the two displayed eigenvalue asymptotics, and Davis–Kahan
gives convergence of the two-dimensional projector to
\bm{P}_{\rm lin}
. Since

\overline{{\bm{y}}}\perp{\bm{v}}_{1},{\bm{v}}_{2}
, the linear outlier space has no asymptotic
label alignment.

It remains to analyze the order-one spectrum of
\bm{K}_{\perp}
. By
(
F.12
), it suffices to analyze
\bm{K}^{\sharp}
.
Lemma
53
gives the full outlier equation
(
F.14
). The roots of the label factor are exactly

s_{y}=T^{-1}(1/\beta)
; the roots of the non-label factor are exactly

s_{\pm}=T^{-1}(t_{\pm})
with
t_{\pm}
in (
F.4
). By the
proof of Lemma
42
, a real
root produces a separated eigenvalue if and only if
z^{\prime}(s)>0
. The
multiplicity and collision-safe statement follows from
Lemma
54
. The same lemma gives the label
projector formula and the no-label statement for non-label clusters. Finally,
(
F.12
) transfers the locations and spectral
projectors from
\bm{K}^{\sharp}
to
\bm{K}_{\perp}
by standard stability of isolated
spectral projectors under an
o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
operator-norm perturbation.

Appendix G
Proof for Pretrained Weight Regime

In this section we prove Theorem
6
. The
main point is that the spiked weight creates two different effects. The linear
Hermite component creates one diverging rank-one spike in the sample direction

{\bm{s}}=\bm{X}^{\top}\mathbf{1}_{d}
. After this direction is removed, the quadratic
Hermite component leaves an order-one rank-one feature proportional to

{\bm{q}}={\bm{s}}^{\odot 2}
. Since
{\bm{q}}
has a non-negligible component along

{\bm{u}}=\mathbf{1}_{n}/\sqrt{n}
, and since the left vector

{\bm{a}}^{\odot 2}
is not orthogonal to
\mathbf{1}_{N}
, the order-one BBP
equation is not the scalar equation for
{\bm{q}}-({\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{q}}){\bm{u}}
.

Throughout this section, we use the following notation for the sample-direction spike and its projection:

{\bm{s}}:=\bm{X}^{\top}\mathbf{1}_{d},\qquad{\bm{q}}:={\bm{s}}^{\odot 2},\qquad\widehat{\bm{s}}:=\frac{{\bm{s}}}{\|{\bm{s}}\|},\qquad\bm{\Pi}_{s}:=\bm{I}_{n}-\widehat{\bm{s}}\widehat{\bm{s}}^{\top},\qquad\bm{K}_{s}:=\bm{\Pi}_{s}\bm{K}\bm{\Pi}_{s}.

The compressed kernel
\bm{K}_{s}
is the deterministic proxy for the non-giant
spectrum of
\bm{K}
. When
b_{\sigma}\neq 0
, the top eigenvector of
\bm{K}

converges to
\widehat{\bm{s}}
, so the separated order-one eigenvalues of
\bm{K}

away from the top eigenvalue are matched by those of
\bm{K}_{s}
up to

o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.

G.1
Moment Estimates and Deterministic Directions

Lemma 55

(Moments of
{\bm{s}}
and
{\bm{q}}
)
.

Recall the notations in Theorem
6
. Under the assumptions of Theorem
6
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\|{\bm{s}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}\kappa,

(G.1)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\|{\bm{q}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}\eta,

(G.2)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}{\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{q}}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}\kappa,\qquad\frac{1}{n}{\bm{y}}^{\top}{\bm{q}}\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}-\frac{r^{2}}{2},

(G.3)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}{\bm{v}}_{2}^{\top}{\bm{s}}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}\frac{r}{\sqrt{2}},\qquad{\bm{v}}_{1}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{s}}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\qquad\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}{\bm{y}}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{s}}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

(G.4)

\displaystyle{\bm{q}}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{s}}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(G.5)

Consequently,

\displaystyle\|\bm{\Pi}_{s}{\bm{v}}_{1}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}1,

\displaystyle\|\bm{\Pi}_{s}{\bm{v}}_{2}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}\frac{1}{\kappa},

(G.6)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\|\bm{\Pi}_{s}{\bm{q}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}\eta,

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\frac{\bm{\Pi}_{s}{\bm{q}}}{\|\bm{\Pi}_{s}{\bm{q}}\|},{\bm{u}}}}\right\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\eta},

(G.7)

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\frac{\bm{\Pi}_{s}{\bm{q}}}{\|\bm{\Pi}_{s}{\bm{q}}\|},\frac{{\bm{y}}}{\sqrt{n}}}}\right\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}\chi_{y}.

(G.8)

Moreover
\bm{\Pi}_{s}{\bm{q}}/\|\bm{\Pi}_{s}{\bm{q}}\|
is asymptotically orthogonal to

{\bm{v}}_{1}
, to
\bm{\Pi}_{s}{\bm{v}}_{2}/\|\bm{\Pi}_{s}{\bm{v}}_{2}\|
, and to the mean-spike direction
