{\bm{m}}/\|{\bm{m}}\|
from Appendix
D
, in the sense of the
right-resolvent quadratic forms used below.

Proof.

Since
{\bm{b}}=\mathbf{1}_{d}=\sqrt{d}\,{\bm{u}}_{2}
, the deterministic shifts of

s_{j}=\mathbf{1}_{d}^{\top}{\bm{x}}_{j}
on the four balanced XOR blocks are
0,0,r,-r
and
the noise part is i.i.d.
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
. Hence

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}s_{j}^{2}=\frac{1}{2}\cdot 1+\frac{1}{2}\cdot(1+r^{2})=\kappa,

and

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}s_{j}^{4}=\frac{1}{2}\cdot 3+\frac{1}{2}\cdot(3+6r^{2}+r^{4})=3+3r^{2}+\frac{r^{4}}{2}=\eta.

The law of large numbers gives (
G.1
)–(
G.2
). The
mean of
s_{j}^{2}
is
1
on the first two blocks and
1+r^{2}
on the last
two blocks. Since
{\bm{u}}=\mathbf{1}_{n}/\sqrt{n}
and

{\bm{y}}=(\mathbf{1}_{n/2},-\mathbf{1}_{n/2})
, this gives
(
G.3
). Also

{\bm{v}}_{2}^{\top}{\bm{s}}=r\sqrt{n/2}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, while

{\bm{v}}_{1}^{\top}{\bm{s}}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
and
{\bm{y}}^{\top}{\bm{s}}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{n})
, proving
(
G.4
).

Finally,
{\bm{q}}^{\top}{\bm{s}}=\sum_{j}s_{j}^{3}
. The third moments in the third and
fourth XOR blocks are
r^{3}+3r
and
-r^{3}-3r
, so the average third moment is
zero. Therefore
\sum_{j}s_{j}^{3}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{n})
, and after division by

\|{\bm{s}}\|\asymp\sqrt{n}
we get (
G.5
). The projection statements
(
G.6
)–(
G.8
) follow by
subtracting the
\widehat{\bm{s}}
-component and using
(
G.1
)–(
G.5
). For example,

\|\bm{\Pi}_{s}{\bm{v}}_{2}\|^{2}=1-|{\bm{v}}_{2}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{s}}|^{2}\to 1-\frac{r^{2}}{2\kappa}=\frac{1}{\kappa},

and the projection changes
{\bm{q}}
by an
O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
-norm vector.

The last statement follows from the same leave-one-out argument as
Lemma
38
. The block means of
{\bm{q}}
have no component
along
{\bm{v}}_{1}
or
{\bm{v}}_{2}
, and the additional projection by
{\bm{s}}
only
changes normalized inner products by
o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. For the mean direction,

m_{j}=F(\|{\bm{z}}_{j}\|^{2})
is of order
d^{-1/2}
, while the covariance of

s_{j}^{2}
with
\|{\bm{z}}_{j}\|^{2}-1
is
O(d^{-1})
per sample. Hence

{\bm{q}}^{\top}{\bm{m}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, whereas
\|{\bm{q}}\|\asymp\sqrt{n}
and

\|{\bm{m}}\|\asymp 1
. The normalized overlap is therefore
o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, and the
resolvent version is obtained by the same deterministic-equivalent estimate.
∎

Lemma 56
.

Let

{\bm{a}}_{0}:=\frac{\mathbf{1}_{N}}{\sqrt{N}},\qquad{\bm{h}}:=\frac{{\bm{a}}^{\odot 2}}{\|{\bm{a}}^{\odot 2}\|}.

Then

\|{\bm{a}}^{\odot 2}\|^{2}=\frac{3}{N}(1+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)),\qquad{\bm{a}}_{0}^{\top}{\bm{h}}\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}\rho:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}.

(G.9)

Moreover
{\bm{h}}
is asymptotically orthogonal to the Gaussian left spike
directions
{\bm{g}}_{1}/\|{\bm{g}}_{1}\|
and
{\bm{g}}_{2}/\|{\bm{g}}_{2}\|
, and the left-resolvent
quadratic forms satisfy, uniformly for
\lambda
in compact subsets of

\mathbb{C}\setminus(\mathrm{supp}({\mu})\cup\{0\})
,

\bm{A}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{L}(\lambda)\bm{A}=s(\lambda)\begin{pmatrix}1&0&0&\rho\\
0&1&0&0\\
0&0&1&0\\
\rho&0&0&1\end{pmatrix}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

(G.10)

where

\bm{A}:=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\bm{a}}_{0},\frac{{\bm{g}}_{1}}{\|{\bm{g}}_{1}\|},\frac{{\bm{g}}_{2}}{\|{\bm{g}}_{2}\|},{\bm{h}}}}\right],\qquad\bm{Q}_{L}(\lambda):=(\bm{G}\bm{G}^{\top}-\lambda\bm{I}_{N})^{-1},\qquad s(\lambda):=\widetilde{m}_{\mu}(\lambda).

Proof.

The first two limits are immediate from the Gaussian moments

\mathbb{E}a_{i}^{2}=N^{-1}
and
\mathbb{E}a_{i}^{4}=3N^{-2}
:

\sum_{i}a_{i}^{4}=\frac{3}{N}(1+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)),\qquad{\bm{a}}_{0}^{\top}{\bm{h}}=\frac{N^{-1/2}\sum_{i}a_{i}^{2}}{(\sum_{i}a_{i}^{4})^{1/2}}\to\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}.

The vector
{\bm{a}}
is independent of
(\bm{W},\bm{X})
, hence
{\bm{h}}
is independent
of
\bm{G},{\bm{g}}_{1},{\bm{g}}_{2}
. Its centered component

{\bm{h}}-\rho{\bm{a}}_{0}
has independent sub-exponential coordinates with variance

1-\rho^{2}+o(1)
, after normalization. The same Hanson–Wright and
anisotropic-law argument used in Lemma
36
gives
(
G.10
). The off-diagonal entry with
{\bm{a}}_{0}

retains the deterministic overlap
\rho
, while all other off-diagonal entries
vanish by independence and orthogonality.
∎

G.2
Quadratic Equivalent for Spiked Weight

Recall the finite-SNR surrogate model from Proposition
31
,

\bm{Y}_{0}^{\sharp}:=\bm{G}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\mathbf{1}_{N}{\bm{m}}^{\top}+\frac{\theta_{\rm snr}b_{\sigma}}{\sqrt{N}}({\bm{g}}_{1}{\bm{v}}_{1}^{\top}+{\bm{g}}_{2}{\bm{v}}_{2}^{\top}),\qquad\theta_{\rm snr}=r\sqrt{\frac{n}{2d}}.

(G.11)

Lemma 57
.

Define

\bm{Y}_{\rm sw}^{\sharp}:=\bm{Y}_{0}^{\sharp}+\frac{b_{\sigma}\theta}{\sqrt{N}}{\bm{a}}{\bm{s}}^{\top}+\frac{c_{\sigma}\theta^{2}}{2\sqrt{N}}{\bm{a}}^{\odot 2}{\bm{q}}^{\top}.

(G.12)

Then

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|\bm{Y}-\bm{Y}_{\rm sw}^{\sharp}}}\right\|=O_{\prec}(n^{-1/4}).

(G.13)

Consequently, with

\bm{Y}_{s}^{\sharp}:=(\bm{Y}_{0}^{\sharp}+\frac{c_{\sigma}\theta_{0}^{2}}{2}{\bm{a}}^{\odot 2}{\bm{q}}^{\top})\bm{\Pi}_{s},\qquad\bm{K}_{s}^{\sharp}:=(\bm{Y}_{s}^{\sharp})^{\top}\bm{Y}_{s}^{\sharp},

(G.14)

we have

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|\bm{Y}\bm{\Pi}_{s}-\bm{Y}_{s}^{\sharp}}}\right\|=O_{\prec}(n^{-1/4}),\qquad\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|\bm{K}_{s}-\bm{K}_{s}^{\sharp}}}\right\|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(G.15)

Proof.

Write

\bm{W}_{1}\bm{X}=\bm{W}\bm{Z}+\Delta_{\rm lin}+\Delta_{\rm sp},\qquad\Delta_{\rm lin}:=\bm{W}\bm{M},\qquad\Delta_{\rm sp}:=\theta{\bm{a}}{\bm{s}}^{\top}.

The entries of
\Delta_{\rm lin}
are
O_{\prec}(n^{-1/2})
, while

\|\Delta_{\rm sp}\|_{\infty}=O_{\prec}(N^{-1/4})
. Taylor expansion at

\bm{W}\bm{Z}
, using the boundedness of
\sigma^{(3)}
, gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sigma(\bm{W}_{1}\bm{X})=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sigma(\bm{W}\bm{Z})+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sigma^{\prime}(\bm{W}\bm{Z})\odot\Delta_{\rm lin}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sigma^{\prime}(\bm{W}\bm{Z})\odot\Delta_{\rm sp}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{N}}\sigma^{\prime\prime}(\bm{W}\bm{Z})\odot\Delta_{\rm sp}^{\odot 2}+\bm{R},

where the omitted terms

(2\sqrt{N})^{-1}\sigma^{\prime\prime}(\bm{W}\bm{Z})\odot\Delta_{\rm lin}^{\odot 2}
,

\sqrt{N}^{-1}\sigma^{\prime\prime}(\bm{W}\bm{Z})\odot(\Delta_{\rm lin}\odot\Delta_{\rm sp})
,
and the third-order remainder all have operator norm
O_{\prec}(n^{-1/4})
.
Indeed, the mixed term has entries of size

O_{\prec}(n^{-1/2}N^{-1/4})
, and after multiplication by
1/\sqrt{N}
its
operator norm is bounded by

\sqrt{Nn}/\sqrt{N}\cdot O_{\prec}(n^{-1/2}N^{-1/4})=O_{\prec}(N^{-1/4})
; the
remainder is analogous.

The first two displayed terms are reduced to
\bm{Y}_{0}^{\sharp}
by
Proposition
31
. For the spiked linear term,

\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sigma^{\prime}(\bm{W}\bm{Z})\odot\Delta_{\rm sp}=\frac{b_{\sigma}\theta}{\sqrt{N}}{\bm{a}}{\bm{s}}^{\top}+\bm{E}_{1},

and
\|\bm{E}_{1}\|=O_{\prec}(n^{-1/4})
. This follows by writing

\bm{E}_{1}=(\theta/\sqrt{N})\operatorname{diag}({\bm{a}})(\sigma^{\prime}(\bm{W}\bm{Z})-b_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{N}\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top})\operatorname{diag}({\bm{s}})
, using

\|\operatorname{diag}({\bm{a}})\|=O_{\prec}(N^{-1/2})
,

\|\sigma^{\prime}(\bm{W}\bm{Z})-b_{\sigma}\mathbf{1}\mathbf{1}^{\top}\|=O_{\prec}(\sqrt{N}+\sqrt{n})
,
and
\|\operatorname{diag}({\bm{s}})\|=O_{\prec}(1)
. The quadratic spiked term satisfies

\frac{1}{2\sqrt{N}}\sigma^{\prime\prime}(\bm{W}\bm{Z})\odot\Delta_{\rm sp}^{\odot 2}=\frac{c_{\sigma}\theta^{2}}{2\sqrt{N}}{\bm{a}}^{\odot 2}{\bm{q}}^{\top}+\bm{E}_{2},\qquad\|\bm{E}_{2}\|=O_{\prec}(n^{-1/4}),

by the same Hermite-coefficient concentration: after factoring

\operatorname{diag}({\bm{a}}^{\odot 2})
and
\operatorname{diag}({\bm{q}})
, the
nonconstant part of
\sigma^{\prime\prime}(\bm{W}\bm{Z})
has operator norm

O_{\prec}(\sqrt{N}+\sqrt{n})
, while

\|\operatorname{diag}({\bm{a}}^{\odot 2})\|=O_{\prec}(N^{-1})
. Combining these
estimates proves (
G.13
).

Right multiplication by
\bm{\Pi}_{s}
kills the diverging linear spike exactly,

(b_{\sigma}\theta/\sqrt{N}){\bm{a}}{\bm{s}}^{\top}\bm{\Pi}_{s}=0
, and

\theta^{2}/\sqrt{N}=\theta_{0}^{2}
. Hence the first estimate in
(
G.15
) follows. The matrices
\bm{Y}\bm{\Pi}_{s}
and

\bm{Y}_{s}^{\sharp}
have operator norm
O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
:
\bm{Y}_{0}^{\sharp}\bm{\Pi}_{s}
is
bounded by Proposition
31
, and the quadratic term has
norm
\frac{|c_{\sigma}|\theta_{0}^{2}}{2}\|{\bm{a}}^{\odot 2}\|\,\|\bm{\Pi}_{s}{\bm{q}}\|=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

by Lemmas
55
and
56
. Therefore, we have

\|\bm{K}_{s}-\bm{K}_{s}^{\sharp}\|\leq(\|\bm{Y}\bm{\Pi}_{s}\|+\|\bm{Y}_{s}^{\sharp}\|)\|\bm{Y}\bm{\Pi}_{s}-\bm{Y}_{s}^{\sharp}\|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

∎

G.3
The Compressed Covariance Spike

The order-one analysis must keep the covariance spike already present in the
centered null matrix
\bm{G}
in Lemma
29
. The spiked weight does not change this covariance
spike at leading order; however, after removal of the giant direction
{\bm{s}}
,
the spike direction is projected from
{\bm{u}}
to
\bm{\Pi}_{s}{\bm{u}}
. We analyze this projected covariance spike below.

Let
\bm{\Sigma}
denote the conditional population covariance of the unscaled
centered row underlying
\bm{G}
, as in Appendix
D
. Thus

\bm{\Sigma}=\bm{\Sigma}_{0}+\tau{\bm{u}}{\bm{u}}^{\top}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\qquad\bm{\Sigma}_{0}=(1-b_{\sigma}^{2})\bm{I}_{n}+b_{\sigma}^{2}\bm{Z}^{\top}\bm{Z},\qquad\tau=\frac{c_{\sigma}^{2}}{2}\psi.

Define

\widehat{\bm{u}}_{s}:=\frac{\bm{\Pi}_{s}{\bm{u}}}{\|\bm{\Pi}_{s}{\bm{u}}\|},\qquad\tau_{s}:=\tau\|\bm{\Pi}_{s}{\bm{u}}\|^{2},\qquad\bm{K}_{0,s}:=(\bm{G}\bm{\Pi}_{s})^{\top}(\bm{G}\bm{\Pi}_{s}).

Lemma 58

(Covariance spike after
{\bm{s}}
-deflation)
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
6
,

\bm{\Pi}_{s}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\Pi}_{s}=\bm{\Pi}_{s}\bm{\Sigma}_{0}\bm{\Pi}_{s}+\tau_{s}\widehat{\bm{u}}_{s}\widehat{\bm{u}}_{s}^{\top}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\qquad\tau_{s}=\tau+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\qquad\|\widehat{\bm{u}}_{s}-{\bm{u}}\|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(G.16)

The ESD of
\bm{K}_{0,s}
converges weakly in probability to
\mu
. Its
possible separated covariance outlier is governed by the same equation as in
Lemma
29
, namely

1-\tau T(s)=0,\qquad z^{\prime}(s)>0.

(G.17)

Moreover, uniformly for
\lambda
in compact subsets of

\mathbb{C}\setminus(\mathrm{supp}({\mu})\cup\{0\})
avoiding the possible pole

1-\tau T(s(\lambda))=0
, with

\bm{Q}_{R,0}^{(s)}(\lambda):=(\bm{K}_{0,s}-\lambda\bm{I})^{-1}
on

\operatorname{Range}\bm{\Pi}_{s}
,

\displaystyle{\bm{b}}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{R,0}^{(s)}(\lambda){\bm{b}}

\displaystyle=m_{\mu}(\lambda)+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

(G.18)

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{q}}_{s}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{R,0}^{(s)}(\lambda)\widehat{\bm{q}}_{s}

\displaystyle=m_{\mu}(\lambda)R_{q}(T(s(\lambda)))+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

(G.19)

\displaystyle{\bm{b}}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{R,0}^{(s)}(\lambda)\widehat{\bm{q}}_{s}

\displaystyle=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

(G.20)

for every unit vector
{\bm{b}}
among
{\bm{b}}_{0}:={\bm{m}}/\|{\bm{m}}\|
,
{\bm{v}}_{1}
, and

\widetilde{\bm{v}}_{2}:=\bm{\Pi}_{s}{\bm{v}}_{2}/\|\bm{\Pi}_{s}{\bm{v}}_{2}\|
. Here

\widehat{\bm{q}}_{s}:=\bm{\Pi}_{s}{\bm{q}}/\|\bm{\Pi}_{s}{\bm{q}}\|
and

R_{q}(t)=1+\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\eta}\frac{\tau t}{1-\tau t}=\frac{1-\tau\omega_{q}t}{1-\tau t}.

(G.21)

Proof.

By Lemma
55
,

{\bm{u}}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{s}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2})
. Hence

\|\bm{\Pi}_{s}{\bm{u}}\|^{2}=1-|{\bm{u}}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{s}}|^{2}=1+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, which proves

\tau_{s}=\tau+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
and
\|\widehat{\bm{u}}_{s}-{\bm{u}}\|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. Multiplying

\bm{\Sigma}=\bm{\Sigma}_{0}+\tau{\bm{u}}{\bm{u}}^{\top}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
on both sides by
\bm{\Pi}_{s}
gives
(
G.16
). Since
\bm{\Pi}_{s}\bm{\Sigma}_{0}\bm{\Pi}_{s}
differs
from
\bm{\Sigma}_{0}
by a matrix of rank at most two plus one additional zero
direction, its limiting covariance ESD is still
\nu
; consequently the limiting
ESD of
\bm{K}_{0,s}
is still
\mu
. The multiplicative BBP equation for the
rank-one covariance spike in (
G.16
) is therefore the
same as in Lemma
29
, with
\tau_{s}
in place of

\tau
; since
\tau_{s}\to\tau
, this gives (
G.17
).

It remains to record the anisotropic resolvent limits needed below. The
right-resolvent deterministic equivalent for the base covariance

\bm{\Pi}_{s}\bm{\Sigma}_{0}\bm{\Pi}_{s}
is unchanged by the rank-one compression, because

\widehat{\bm{s}}
is asymptotically orthogonal to the deterministic finite-dimensional
directions considered here and the removed subspace has fixed rank. Adding the
rank-one covariance spike

\tau_{s}\widehat{\bm{u}}_{s}\widehat{\bm{u}}_{s}^{\top}
and applying the Sherman–Morrison
formula yields

\widehat{\bm{u}}_{s}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{R,0}^{(s)}(\lambda)\widehat{\bm{u}}_{s}=\frac{m_{\mu}(\lambda)}{1-\tau T(s(\lambda))}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

(G.22)

whereas every unit vector asymptotically orthogonal to
\widehat{\bm{u}}_{s}
has
quadratic form
m_{\mu}(\lambda)+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, and cross forms with

\widehat{\bm{u}}_{s}
are negligible. The vectors
{\bm{b}}_{0}
,
{\bm{v}}_{1}
, and

\widetilde{\bm{v}}_{2}
are asymptotically orthogonal to
\widehat{\bm{u}}_{s}
, giving
(
G.18
). Furthermore, Lemma
55

gives the decomposition

\widehat{\bm{q}}_{s}=\frac{\kappa}{\sqrt{\eta}}\widehat{\bm{u}}_{s}+\sqrt{\omega_{q}}\,{\bm{e}}_{q}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\qquad{\bm{e}}_{q}\perp\widehat{\bm{u}}_{s},\qquad\|{\bm{e}}_{q}\|=1,

with
{\bm{e}}_{q}
asymptotically isotropic for the base resolvent and asymptotically
orthogonal to
{\bm{b}}_{0},{\bm{v}}_{1},\widetilde{\bm{v}}_{2}
. Combining this decomposition
with (
G.22
) gives

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{q}}_{s}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{R,0}^{(s)}(\lambda)\widehat{\bm{q}}_{s}

\displaystyle=\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\eta}\frac{m_{\mu}(\lambda)}{1-\tau T(s(\lambda))}+\omega_{q}m_{\mu}(\lambda)+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

\displaystyle=m_{\mu}(\lambda)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\eta}\frac{\tau T(s(\lambda))}{1-\tau T(s(\lambda))}}}\right)+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

which is (
G.19
)–(
G.21
). The same
orthogonal decomposition gives the cross estimate (
G.20
).
∎

G.4
Proof of Giant Spike and Bulk Limit

Proof of Theorem
6
(i)–(ii).

The rank-one matrix

\bm{P}_{s}:=\frac{b_{\sigma}\theta}{\sqrt{N}}{\bm{a}}{\bm{s}}^{\top}

has squared singular value

\|\bm{P}_{s}\|^{2}=\frac{b_{\sigma}^{2}\theta^{2}}{N}({\bm{a}}^{\top}{\bm{a}})\|{\bm{s}}\|^{2}=b_{\sigma}^{2}\theta_{0}^{2}\phi\kappa\sqrt{N}\,(1+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)),

using
{\bm{a}}^{\top}{\bm{a}}\to 1
,
\|{\bm{s}}\|^{2}/n\to\kappa
, and
n/N\to\phi
.
All terms in
\bm{Y}_{\rm sw}^{\sharp}-\bm{P}_{s}
have operator norm
O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.
Thus the top eigenvalue of
(\bm{Y}_{\rm sw}^{\sharp})^{\top}\bm{Y}_{\rm sw}^{\sharp}

is
\|\bm{P}_{s}\|^{2}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{N})
, and the corresponding eigenvector has overlap

1-o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
with
\widehat{\bm{s}}
. The feature error in
Lemma
57
changes this eigenvalue by
o_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{N})
, and
Davis–Kahan Theorem gives (
4.18
). Since

{\bm{y}}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{s}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, the normalized label overlap tends to zero.
If
b_{\sigma}=0
,
\bm{P}_{s}
is absent and no diverging spike is produced by
this mechanism.

For the bulk,
\bm{Y}_{\rm sw}^{\sharp}
differs from the finite-SNR random-weight
surrogate
\bm{Y}_{0}^{\sharp}
by two rank-one feature perturbations. Hence the
corresponding Gram matrices differ by a finite-rank matrix. The error

\bm{Y}-\bm{Y}_{\rm sw}^{\sharp}
contributes negligibly to the ESD: its product with
the bounded part has normalized trace norm
o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, and its product with the
single giant rank-one term has rank at most two. Since the random-weight
surrogate has limiting ESD
\mu
by Theorem
3
, so does

\bm{K}
. Compressing by
\bm{\Pi}_{s}
changes at most one eigenvalue, so the same
bulk limit holds for
\bm{K}_{s}
.
∎

G.5
Proof of Order-one Outliers and Label Alignment

Let

\widehat{\bm{q}}_{s}:=\frac{\bm{\Pi}_{s}{\bm{q}}}{\|\bm{\Pi}_{s}{\bm{q}}\|},\qquad\widetilde{\bm{v}}_{2}:=\frac{\bm{\Pi}_{s}{\bm{v}}_{2}}{\|\bm{\Pi}_{s}{\bm{v}}_{2}\|}.

By Lemmas
55
and
56
, the
finite-rank part of
\bm{Y}_{s}^{\sharp}
can be written, up to an
o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

operator-norm error, as

\bm{Y}_{s}^{\sharp}=\bm{G}\bm{\Pi}_{s}+\sqrt{\tau}\,{\bm{a}}_{0}{\bm{b}}_{0}^{\top}+\sqrt{\beta_{\rm lin}}\,{\bm{a}}_{1}{\bm{v}}_{1}^{\top}+\sqrt{\frac{\beta_{\rm lin}}{\kappa}}\,{\bm{a}}_{2}\widetilde{\bm{v}}_{2}^{\top}+\sqrt{\beta_{q}}\,{\bm{h}}\widehat{\bm{q}}_{s}^{\top}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

(G.23)

where

{\bm{b}}_{0}:=\frac{{\bm{m}}}{\|{\bm{m}}\|},\qquad{\bm{a}}_{1}:=\frac{{\bm{g}}_{1}}{\|{\bm{g}}_{1}\|},\qquad{\bm{a}}_{2}:=\frac{{\bm{g}}_{2}}{\|{\bm{g}}_{2}\|}.

The factor
\beta_{\rm lin}/\kappa
in the third term is the loss of the

{\bm{v}}_{2}
-component caused by deflating the giant
{\bm{s}}
-direction.

Set

\Theta:=\mathop{\mathrm{}}{diag}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sqrt{\tau},\sqrt{\beta_{\rm lin}},\sqrt{\beta_{\rm lin}/\kappa},\sqrt{\beta_{q}}}}\right),

\bm{A}:=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\bm{a}}_{0},{\bm{a}}_{1},{\bm{a}}_{2},{\bm{h}}}}\right],\qquad\bm{B}:=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\bm{b}}_{0},{\bm{v}}_{1},\widetilde{\bm{v}}_{2},\widehat{\bm{q}}_{s}}}\right].

Let
\bm{K}_{0}^{(s)}:=(\bm{G}\bm{\Pi}_{s})^{\top}(\bm{G}\bm{\Pi}_{s})
, and let

\bm{Q}_{R}^{(s)}(\lambda):=(\bm{K}_{0}^{(s)}-\lambda\bm{I})^{-1}
on the range of

\bm{\Pi}_{s}
. By Lemma
58
, the compressed null
matrix keeps the covariance spike, now in the direction

\widehat{\bm{u}}_{s}=\bm{\Pi}_{s}{\bm{u}}/\|\bm{\Pi}_{s}{\bm{u}}\|
and with strength
\tau+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.
Thus, with
t=T(s(\lambda))=\lambda s(\lambda)m_{\mu}(\lambda)
,

\bm{B}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{R}^{(s)}(\lambda)\bm{B}=m_{\mu}(\lambda)\mathop{\mathrm{}}{diag}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1,1,1,R_{q}(t)}}\right)+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

(G.24)

where

R_{q}(t)=1+\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\eta}\frac{\tau t}{1-\tau t}=\frac{1-\tau\omega_{q}t}{1-\tau t}.

(G.25)

From the proof of Lemma
58
, we know that

\widehat{\bm{q}}_{s}
has squared overlap
\kappa^{2}/\eta
with the projected
covariance-spike direction
\widehat{\bm{u}}_{s}
, while
{\bm{b}}_{0}
,
{\bm{v}}_{1}
, and

\widetilde{\bm{v}}_{2}
are asymptotically orthogonal to it. Hence only the
quadratic spiked-weight direction sees the covariance pole.
Together with (
G.10
), the finite-rank determinant
for order-one outliers of
\bm{K}_{s}^{\sharp}
is

D_{\rm rel}(\lambda)=\det\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{I}_{4}-t\,\Theta\begin{pmatrix}1&0&0&\rho\\
0&1&0&0\\
0&0&1&0\\
\rho&0&0&1\end{pmatrix}\Theta\mathop{\mathrm{}}{diag}(1,1,1,R_{q}(t))}}\right)+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(G.26)

Expanding the determinant gives

D_{\rm rel}(\lambda)=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\beta_{\rm lin}t}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\frac{\beta_{\rm lin}}{\kappa}t}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[(1-\tau t)(1-\beta_{q}R_{q}(t)t)-\rho^{2}\tau\beta_{q}R_{q}(t)t^{2}}}\right]+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(G.27)

The denominator
1-\tau t
in
R_{q}(t)
is the pole of the covariance outlier
already present in the base matrix
\bm{K}_{0}^{(s)}
, not an additional root. As
in the collision-safe argument for the uninformative cluster in
Appendix
D
, the characteristic determinant is obtained
by multiplying by the covariance factor
1-\tau t
. Using
\rho^{2}=1/3
,

\displaystyle(1-\tau t)D_{\rm rel}(\lambda)

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\beta_{\rm lin}t}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\frac{\beta_{\rm lin}}{\kappa}t}}\right)F_{q}(t)+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

(G.28)

where
F_{q}
is exactly (
4.16
). This is the desired simplification
of the augmented determinant.

Proof of Theorem
6
(iii).

Choose a contour in
\mathbb{C}
enclosing one candidate location
\lambda_{\star}
and disjoint
from
\mathrm{supp}({\mu})\cup\{0\}
and from all other candidate locations. On this
contour, (
G.28
) is a uniform approximation to the
finite-dimensional determinant. Rouché’s theorem gives the same number of
zeros as the deterministic product in (
G.28
), counted
with algebraic multiplicity. These zeros are equivalent to eigenvalues of the
linearized matrix, hence to eigenvalues of
\bm{K}_{s}^{\sharp}
, outside the bulk.
Lemma
57
transfers the conclusion to
\bm{K}_{s}
. The admissibility
condition
z^{\prime}(s)>0
is the usual BBP threshold from
Lemma
42
; roots on branches with
z^{\prime}(s)\leq 0
do not
separate from the limiting support
\mu
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
6
(iv).

We now compute the resolvent in the label direction. Since

{\bm{y}}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{s}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, replacing
{\bm{y}}/\sqrt{n}
by

\bm{\Pi}_{s}{\bm{y}}/\|\bm{\Pi}_{s}{\bm{y}}\|
changes normalized overlaps by
o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. The
linear spike directions
{\bm{v}}_{1}
and
\widetilde{\bm{v}}_{2}
are asymptotically
orthogonal to
{\bm{y}}/\sqrt{n}
, and the same is true of the mean direction

{\bm{b}}_{0}
. More precisely, uniformly for
\lambda
on any fixed contour away
from
\mathrm{supp}({\mu})\cup\{0\}
,

\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bm{B}^{\top}\bm{Q}_{R}^{(s)}(\lambda){\bm{y}}=m_{\mu}(\lambda)\sqrt{\chi_{y}}\,{\bm{e}}_{4}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(G.29)

The possible covariance pole in
\bm{Q}_{R}^{(s)}
does not change this vector,
because
{\bm{y}}\perp{\bm{u}}
. Thus the first three right coordinates

{\bm{b}}_{0},{\bm{v}}_{1},\widetilde{\bm{v}}_{2}
cannot create a pole in the label resolvent. In
particular, the roots of the two linear factors have zero label residue, and a
pure mean-spike cluster has zero label residue. Once
\beta_{q}>0
, however,
the old mean/covariance cluster is no longer a separate pure
{\bm{b}}_{0}
-cluster;
it is absorbed into the coupled factor
F_{q}
. Any label mass at such a shifted
location is caused by its mixing with the quadratic direction
\widehat{\bm{q}}_{s}
,
not by
{\bm{b}}_{0}
itself.

The Woodbury formula for the finite-rank model (
G.23
),
combined with (
G.10
),
(
G.24
), and (
G.29
), gives
uniformly away from the bulk

\frac{1}{n}{\bm{y}}^{\top}(\bm{K}_{s}^{\sharp}-\lambda\bm{I})^{-1}{\bm{y}}=H_{y}(\lambda)+m_{\mu}(\lambda)\frac{\beta_{q}\chi_{y}\,t\,(1-\tau t)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\frac{2}{3}\tau t}}\right)}{F_{q}(t)}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

(G.30)

where
H_{y}
is analytic in any neighborhood not intersecting

\mathrm{supp}({\mu})\cup\{0\}
, and
t=T(s(\lambda))
. The absence of the factors

1-\beta_{\rm lin}t
and
1-\beta_{\rm lin}t/\kappa
from the denominator in
(
G.30
) is the desired zero-alignment statement for the

{\bm{v}}_{1}
- and
\widetilde{\bm{v}}_{2}
-outliers. The absence of a separate

1-\tau t
denominator is the corresponding zero-alignment statement for a
pure mean/covariance cluster. The only non-analytic denominator relevant to the
label is
F_{q}(t)
.

To see the numerator in (
G.30
), restrict the Woodbury
correction to the coupled coordinates
({\bm{a}}_{0},{\bm{h}})
. The relevant

2\times 2
inverse has determinant
F_{q}(t)/(1-\tau t)
, and its

(2,2)
-entry contributes

\beta_{q}[1-(2/3)\tau t](1-\tau t)/F_{q}(t)
. Multiplication by the squared right
overlap
\chi_{y}
gives the displayed singular term.

Let
\Gamma_{\star}
be a positively oriented contour around
I_{\star}
. The
spectral projector satisfies

\frac{1}{n}{\bm{y}}^{\top}\widehat{}\bm{P}_{\star}^{(s)}{\bm{y}}=-\frac{1}{2\pi i}\oint_{\Gamma_{\star}}\frac{1}{n}{\bm{y}}^{\top}(\bm{K}_{s}-\lambda\bm{I})^{-1}{\bm{y}}\,d\lambda.

The approximation
\|\bm{K}_{s}-\bm{K}_{s}^{\sharp}\|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
allows us to replace

\bm{K}_{s}
by
\bm{K}_{s}^{\sharp}
. The analytic term
H_{y}
integrates to zero.
Hence, if the contour contains no root of
F_{q}(T(s))
, the limit is zero.
If the contour contains a single simple root
s_{\star}
of
F_{q}(T(s))
, then

\frac{d}{d\lambda}F_{q}(T(s(\lambda)))\Big|_{\lambda=z(s_{\star})}=F_{q}^{\prime}(T(s_{\star}))\frac{T^{\prime}(s_{\star})}{z^{\prime}(s_{\star})}.

Taking minus the residue of (
G.30
) gives

\Gamma_{\rm sw}(s):=-\frac{m_{\mu}(z(s))z^{\prime}(s)}{T^{\prime}(s)}\frac{\beta_{q}\chi_{y}\,T(s)\,(1-\tau T(s))\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\frac{2}{3}\tau T(s)}}\right)}{F_{q}^{\prime}(T(s))}.

(G.31)

If a contour encloses several simple
F_{q}
-roots, the
same calculation sums their residues. This completes the proof of
Theorem
6
.
∎

Appendix H
Proof for Quadratic Sample-size Regime

In this section we prove the quadratic sample-size result for the
CK matrix in the finite-width regime

n\asymp d^{2},\qquad N\asymp n.

This is the regime in which the empirical random-feature noise is of order one.
Consequently one should not first replace the empirical CK by its population
kernel. The correct object is a sample covariance matrix whose population
covariance is the quadratic kernel. This produces two successive BBP
transitions: one in the quadratic lift of the data, and one from the finite
number of random features.

Throughout this section we use the XOR model (
3.5
)–(
3.8
).
Let

p:=\frac{d(d+1)}{2},\qquad\frac{n}{p}\to\gamma\in(0,\infty),\qquad\frac{n}{N}\to\phi\in(0,\infty),

(H.1)

and assume that the SNR parameter
r=O(1)
is fixed. Since

p=d(d+1)/2
, this is equivalently the quadratic sample-size scaling

2n/d^{2}\to\gamma
. Recall that
\|\bm{y}\|_{2}=\sqrt{n}
and

\bm{y}^{\top}\bm{1}=0
.

Let
(h_{k})_{k\geq 0}
denote the orthonormal Hermite polynomials with respect to

\xi\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
, so that

h_{0}\equiv 1,\qquad h_{1}(x)=x,\qquad h_{2}(x)=\frac{x^{2}-1}{\sqrt{2}}.

We write

a_{k}:=\mathbb{E}[\sigma(\xi)h_{k}(\xi)],\qquad a_{1}=b_{\sigma},\qquad a_{2}=\frac{c_{\sigma}}{\sqrt{2}}.

(H.2)

Assumption
1
states that
\mathbb{E}[\sigma(\xi)]=0
, hence

a_{0}=0
. We further denote that

\alpha_{2}:=a_{2}^{2}=\frac{c_{\sigma}^{2}}{2},\qquad\alpha_{0}:=\sum_{k\geq 3}a_{k}^{2}=1-b_{\sigma}^{2}-\frac{c_{\sigma}^{2}}{2}.

(H.3)

For
i\in[n]
, set
s_{i}:=\|\bm{x}_{i}\|_{2}
and

\zeta_{k}^{(i)}:=\mathbb{E}[\sigma(s_{i}\xi)h_{k}(\xi)],\qquad k\geq 0.

(H.4)

Define the mean vector and the linear-channel diagonal matrix

\bm{\mu}_{\sigma}:=(\zeta_{0}^{(1)},\ldots,\zeta_{0}^{(n)})^{\top},\qquad\bm{A}_{\sigma}:=\mathop{\mathrm{}}{diag}\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\zeta_{1}^{(1)}}{s_{1}},\ldots,\frac{\zeta_{1}^{(n)}}{s_{n}}}}\right).

(H.5)

The nuisance subspace is

\mathcal{U}_{X}:=\mathrm{span}\{\bm{1},\bm{\mu}_{\sigma}\}+\mathrm{Range}(\bm{A}_{\sigma}\bm{X}^{\top})\subset\mathbb{R}^{n},

(H.6)

and we let

\bm{P}_{\sigma}:=\operatorname{Proj}(\mathcal{U}_{X}^{\perp})

(H.7)

be the orthogonal projector onto its complement. We study the nuisance-deflated
CK matrix

\bm{K}_{\sigma}:=\bm{P}_{\sigma}\bm{K}\bm{P}_{\sigma},\qquad\bm{K}=\frac{1}{N}\sigma(\bm{W}\bm{X})^{\top}\sigma(\bm{W}\bm{X}).

(H.8)

Since
\mathrm{rank}(\bm{I}_{n}-\bm{P}_{\sigma})\leq d+2=o(n)
, deflation does not change the
limiting ESD of
\bm{K}
, but it removes the uninformative mean
and linear channels in
\bm{K}
. The label vector is asymptotically preserved by this
projection; see Lemma
59
below.

Define the limiting population law

\nu_{\rm q}:=\alpha_{0}+\alpha_{2}\rho_{\gamma}^{\mathrm{MP}},

(H.9)

that is, the push-forward of the MP law
\rho_{\gamma}^{\mathrm{MP}}
by

x\mapsto\alpha_{0}+\alpha_{2}x
. Let

\mu_{\rm q}:=\rho_{\phi}^{\mathrm{MP}}\boxtimes\nu_{\rm q}.

(H.10)

For this pair
(\nu_{\rm q},\phi)
define the Silverstein–Choi

z
-transform and the overlap map

\displaystyle z(s)

\displaystyle:=-\frac{1}{s}+\phi\int\frac{t}{1+ts}\,\nu_{\rm q}(dt),

(H.11)

\displaystyle\varphi(s)

\displaystyle:=-\frac{sz^{\prime}(s)}{z(s)}.

(H.12)

These are the same transforms as in (
B.38
)–(
B.39
),
with
\nu
replaced by (
H.9
). The upper edge of

\nu_{\rm q}
is

\tau_{+}^{\nu}=\alpha_{0}+\alpha_{2}(1+\sqrt{\gamma})^{2}.

(H.13)

The quadratic lift of XOR has signal strength

\delta:=\frac{r^{2}}{2},\qquad\ell:=\delta^{2}=\frac{r^{4}}{4}.

(H.14)

For later use, define the formal BBP location and overlap maps

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathrm{out}}(\gamma,\ell)

\displaystyle:=(1+\gamma\ell)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{1}{\ell}}}\right)=1+\gamma+\gamma\ell+\frac{1}{\ell},

(H.15)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Align}(\gamma,\ell)

\displaystyle:=\frac{\gamma\ell^{2}-1}{\gamma\ell(\ell+1)}.

(H.16)

These quantities describe an actual separated quadratic-lift outlier only in the
supercritical regime
\ell>\gamma^{-1/2}
.
The induced population spike of the deflated CK covariance is

\Lambda_{y}:=\alpha_{0}+\alpha_{2}\lambda_{\mathrm{out}}(\gamma,\ell).

(H.17)

H.1
QE of Population Covariance Matrix

Lemma 59

(The projection preserves the XOR label)
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
7
,

\mathrm{rank}({\bm{I}_{n}-\bm{P}_{\sigma}})\leq d+2=o(n),

and
\frac{1}{n}{\bm{y}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{\sigma}{\bm{y}}\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}1.

More precisely,
{\bm{y}}^{\top}(\bm{I}_{n}-\bm{P}_{\sigma}){\bm{y}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(d).

Proof.

The rank bound follows directly from
\mathcal{U}_{X}=\mathrm{span}\{{\bm{1}},{\bm{\mu}}_{\sigma}\}+\mathrm{Range}(\bm{A}_{\sigma}\bm{X}^{\top}),

because
\dim(\mathcal{U}_{X})\leq 1+1+d=d+2.
It remains to prove that the projection of the XOR label onto

\mathcal{U}_{X}
has squared norm
o_{\mathbb{P}}(n)
. Set

f_{0}(s):=\mathbb{E}[\sigma(s\xi)],\qquad f_{1}(s):=\frac{1}{s}\mathbb{E}[\sigma(s\xi)h_{1}(\xi)].

Here, we only take expectation with respect to the random variable
\xi\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
. Thus

{\bm{\mu}}_{\sigma}=(f_{0}(\|{\bm{x}}_{1}\|),\ldots,f_{0}(\|{\bm{x}}_{n}\|))^{\top},\qquad\bm{A}_{\sigma}=\mathop{\mathrm{}}{diag}(f_{1}(\|{\bm{x}}_{1}\|),\ldots,f_{1}(\|{\bm{x}}_{n}\|)).

Define the
n\times(d+1)
nuisance design matrix

\bm{B}_{\sigma}:=\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{\mu}}_{\sigma}&\bm{A}_{\sigma}\bm{X}^{\top}\end{bmatrix}.

Its
i
-th row is
{\bm{b}}_{i}^{\top}=\bigl(f_{0}(\|{\bm{x}}_{i}\|),\,f_{1}(\|{\bm{x}}_{i}\|){\bm{x}}_{i}^{\top}\bigr).

Then
\mathcal{U}_{X}=\mathrm{span}\{{\bm{1}}\}+\mathrm{Range}(\bm{B}_{\sigma}).

Let
\bm{P}:=\bm{I}_{n}-\frac{1}{n}{\bm{1}}{\bm{1}}^{\top}.

Since the XOR label is balanced,
\bm{P}{\bm{y}}={\bm{y}}
. Moreover, minimizing over the
constant direction gives
\|\Pi_{\mathcal{U}_{X}}{\bm{y}}\|^{2}=\|\Pi_{\mathrm{Range}(\bm{P}\bm{B}_{\sigma})}{\bm{y}}\|^{2}.

Consequently,

\|\Pi_{\mathcal{U}_{X}}{\bm{y}}\|^{2}=(\bm{B}_{\sigma}^{\top}{\bm{y}})^{\top}(\bm{B}_{\sigma}^{\top}\bm{P}\bm{B}_{\sigma})^{\dagger}(\bm{B}_{\sigma}^{\top}{\bm{y}}).

Let us define the cluster means of the XOR model are

{\bm{m}}_{1}=\frac{r}{\sqrt{d}}{\bm{u}}_{1},\qquad{\bm{m}}_{2}=-\frac{r}{\sqrt{d}}{\bm{u}}_{1},\qquad{\bm{m}}_{3}=\frac{r}{\sqrt{d}}{\bm{u}}_{2},\qquad{\bm{m}}_{4}=-\frac{r}{\sqrt{d}}{\bm{u}}_{2},

with labels
+,+,-,-
. Since
f_{0}
is radial,
\mathbb{E}[f_{0}(\|{\bm{z}}+{\bm{m}}_{a}\|)]

depends only on
\|{\bm{m}}_{a}\|
, and this norm is the same for all four clusters. Therefore the first coordinate of
\sum_{i}y_{i}\mathbb{E}[{\bm{b}}_{i}]
is zero. Here we take expectation with respect to
\bm{X}
.
For the rest coordinates of
\sum_{i}y_{i}\mathbb{E}[{\bm{b}}_{i}]
, radial symmetry implies that, for some scalar

\kappa
depending only on
r/\sqrt{d}
,
\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[f_{1}(\|{\bm{z}}+{\bm{m}}_{a}\|)({\bm{z}}+{\bm{m}}_{a})}}\right]=\kappa{\bm{m}}_{a}
for each cluster
a=1,2,3,4
.
Hence

\sum_{a=1}^{4}y_{a}\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[f_{1}(\|{\bm{z}}+{\bm{m}}_{a}\|)({\bm{z}}+{\bm{m}}_{a})}}\right]=\kappa({\bm{m}}_{1}+{\bm{m}}_{2}-{\bm{m}}_{3}-{\bm{m}}_{4})=0.

Thus
\sum_{i=1}^{n}y_{i}\mathbb{E}[{\bm{b}}_{i}]=0.
Let
\bar{{\bm{b}}}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[{\bm{b}}_{i}]
be the population mean of the rows of
\bm{B}_{\sigma}
,
and define the population centered covariance

\bm{\Sigma}_{B}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\bigl[({\bm{b}}_{i}-\bar{{\bm{b}}})({\bm{b}}_{i}-\bar{{\bm{b}}})^{\top}\bigr].

Because
{\bm{y}}^{\top}{\bm{1}}=0
,
\bm{B}_{\sigma}^{\top}{\bm{y}}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}y_{i}({\bm{b}}_{i}-\bar{{\bm{b}}}).

The preceding population orthogonality gives
\mathbb{E}[\bm{B}_{\sigma}^{\top}{\bm{y}}]=0.

By Assumption
1
, the functions
f_{0}
and
f_{1}
are smooth
with bounded derivatives in a neighborhood of
1
. Together with
Lemma
11
, this implies that the rows

{\bm{b}}_{i}-\bar{{\bm{b}}}
are uniformly sub-Gaussian after whitening by

\bm{\Sigma}_{B}
. Since
d/n\to 0
, the usual sample-covariance concentration for
independent, non-identically distributed sub-Gaussian rows gives, on the support
of
\bm{\Sigma}_{B}
,

\bm{B}_{\sigma}^{\top}\bm{P}\bm{B}_{\sigma}=n\bm{\Sigma}_{B}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(n).

We need the following covariance concentration for the nuisance design. Let

\bar{{\bm{b}}}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[{\bm{b}}_{i}],\qquad\bm{\Sigma}_{B}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\bigl[({\bm{b}}_{i}-\bar{{\bm{b}}})({\bm{b}}_{i}-\bar{{\bm{b}}})^{\top}\bigr].

Then, on
\operatorname{supp}(\bm{\Sigma}_{B})
,

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|\bm{\Sigma}_{B}^{\dagger/2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{n}\bm{B}_{\sigma}^{\top}\bm{P}\bm{B}_{\sigma}-\bm{\Sigma}_{B}}}\right)\bm{\Sigma}_{B}^{\dagger/2}}}\right\|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Indeed,

\bm{B}_{\sigma}^{\top}\bm{P}\bm{B}_{\sigma}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}({\bm{b}}_{i}-\bar{{\bm{b}}}_{\rm emp})({\bm{b}}_{i}-\bar{{\bm{b}}}_{\rm emp})^{\top},

where
\bar{{\bm{b}}}_{\rm emp}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\bm{b}}_{i}.

Let
\bm{U}
be an orthonormal basis of
\operatorname{supp}(\bm{\Sigma}_{B})
, and set

\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{B}:=\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{B}\bm{U},\qquad{\bm{g}}_{i}:=\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{B}^{-1/2}\bm{U}^{\top}({\bm{b}}_{i}-\bar{{\bm{b}}}).

Then
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[{\bm{g}}_{i}{\bm{g}}_{i}^{\top}]=\bm{I}.

By Assumption
1
, the functions

f_{0}(s)=\mathbb{E}[\sigma(s\xi)],\qquad f_{1}(s)=s^{-1}\mathbb{E}[\sigma(s\xi)\xi]=\mathbb{E}[\sigma^{\prime}(s\xi)]

have uniformly bounded derivatives in a neighborhood of
1
. Since the XOR
columns are Gaussian with covariance
\bm{I}_{d}/d
and

\max_{i}|\|{\bm{x}}_{i}\|-1|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
by Lemma
11
, the whitened
rows
{\bm{g}}_{i}
have uniformly bounded sub-exponential norm. Therefore, because
q:=\mathrm{rank}({\bm{\Sigma}_{B}})\leq d+1,

\frac{d}{n}\to 0,

standard covariance concentration for independent, non-identically distributed
sub-exponential rows gives

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\bm{g}}_{i}{\bm{g}}_{i}^{\top}-\bm{I}}}\right\|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\qquad\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\bm{g}}_{i}}}\right\|^{2}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Undoing the whitening gives the claimed relative concentration.
Consequently, with probability tending to one,

\bm{B}_{\sigma}^{\top}\bm{P}\bm{B}_{\sigma}\succeq\frac{n}{2}\bm{\Sigma}_{B}\quad\text{on }\operatorname{supp}(\bm{\Sigma}_{B}),

and hence
(\bm{B}_{\sigma}^{\top}\bm{P}\bm{B}_{\sigma})^{\dagger}\preceq\frac{2}{n}\bm{\Sigma}_{B}^{\dagger}.

Therefore

\|\Pi_{\mathcal{U}_{X}}{\bm{y}}\|^{2}\leq\frac{2}{n}(\bm{B}_{\sigma}^{\top}{\bm{y}})^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{B}^{\dagger}(\bm{B}_{\sigma}^{\top}{\bm{y}})

with probability tending to one.

It remains to bound the whitened score. Since the rows are independent,

\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[(\bm{B}_{\sigma}^{\top}{\bm{y}})^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{B}^{\dagger}(\bm{B}_{\sigma}^{\top}{\bm{y}})}}\right]=\operatorname{Tr}\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\bm{\Sigma}_{B}^{\dagger}\mathrm{Cov}(\bm{B}_{\sigma}^{\top}{\bm{y}})}}\right]\\
=\operatorname{Tr}\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\bm{\Sigma}_{B}^{\dagger}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathrm{Cov}({\bm{b}}_{i})}}\right].

Moreover,
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathrm{Cov}({\bm{b}}_{i})\preceq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\bigl[({\bm{b}}_{i}-\bar{{\bm{b}}})({\bm{b}}_{i}-\bar{{\bm{b}}})^{\top}\bigr]=n\bm{\Sigma}_{B}.

Thus

\mathbb{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[(\bm{B}_{\sigma}^{\top}{\bm{y}})^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{B}^{\dagger}(\bm{B}_{\sigma}^{\top}{\bm{y}})}}\right]\leq n\mathrm{rank}({\bm{\Sigma}_{B}})\leq n(d+1).

By Markov’s inequality,
(\bm{B}_{\sigma}^{\top}{\bm{y}})^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{B}^{\dagger}(\bm{B}_{\sigma}^{\top}{\bm{y}})=O_{\mathbb{P}}(nd).

Consequently,
\|\Pi_{\mathcal{U}_{X}}{\bm{y}}\|^{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(d).
Since
n\asymp d^{2}
,
\frac{1}{n}\|\Pi_{\mathcal{U}_{X}}{\bm{y}}\|^{2}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).
Finally,

\frac{1}{n}{\bm{y}}^{\top}\bm{P}_{\sigma}{\bm{y}}=1-\frac{1}{n}\|\Pi_{\mathcal{U}_{X}}{\bm{y}}\|^{2}\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}1.

∎

Let
\bm{w}\sim\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\bm{I}_{d})
and define the population CK matrix

\bm{\Phi}(\bm{X})\;:=\;\mathbb{E}_{\bm{w}}\big[\sigma(\bm{w}^{\top}\bm{X})^{\top}\sigma(\bm{w}^{\top}\bm{X})\mid\bm{X}\big]\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n},\qquad\bm{\Phi}(\bm{X})_{ij}=\mathbb{E}_{\bm{w}}[\sigma(\bm{w}^{\top}\bm{x}_{i})\sigma(\bm{w}^{\top}\bm{x}_{j})\mid\bm{X}].

The following lemmas derive the quadratic equivalent of population kernel after the projection. The mean and linear terms in the population kernel are killed by
\bm{P}_{\sigma}
.

Let
(h_{k})_{k\geq 0}
be the orthonormal Hermite polynomials w.r.t.
\xi\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
:

\mathbb{E}[h_{k}(\xi)h_{\ell}(\xi)]=\delta_{k\ell}
, with
h_{0}\equiv 1
,
h_{1}(\xi)=\xi
, and
h_{2}(\xi)=(\xi^{2}-1)/\sqrt{2}
. For each
i\in[n]
, set
s_{i}:=\|\bm{x}_{i}\|
and define the scaled activation
\sigma_{(i)}(t):=\sigma(s_{i}t)
. Define its Hermite coefficients

\zeta_{k}^{(i)}:=\mathbb{E}[\sigma_{(i)}(\xi)h_{k}(\xi)],\qquad k\in\mathbb{N},

and the diagonal matrices
\bm{D}_{k}:=\mathop{\mathrm{}}{diag}(\zeta_{k}^{(1)},\dots,\zeta_{k}^{(n)})
. Define the Gram and correlation matrices

\bm{G}:=\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X},\qquad\bm{D}:=\mathop{\mathrm{}}{diag}(s_{1},\dots,s_{n}),\qquad\bm{R}:=\bm{D}^{-1}\bm{G}\bm{D}^{-1}.

Note
\bm{R}_{ii}=1
and
\bm{R}_{ij}=\frac{\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{x}_{j}}{s_{i}s_{j}}
.

Lemma 60

(Hermite expansion of
\bm{\Phi}(\bm{X})
)
.

Condition on
\bm{X}
. Then

\bm{\Phi}(\bm{X})\;=\;\sum_{k\geq 0}\bm{D}_{k}\,\bm{R}^{\odot k}\,\bm{D}_{k},\qquad(\bm{R}^{\odot k})_{ij}:=(\bm{R}_{ij})^{k}.

Proof.

This lemma is derived in the proof of Lemma 5.2 in
[
82
]
.
∎

Define the Hadamard-square matrix
\bm{H}:=\bm{G}\odot\bm{G}
.
For
k=0,1,2
, define the following objects:

\bm{\mu}_{\sigma}:=\bm{D}_{0}\bm{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{n},\qquad\bm{A}_{\sigma}:=\mathop{\mathrm{}}{diag}\!\Big(\frac{\zeta_{1}^{(1)}}{s_{1}},\dots,\frac{\zeta_{1}^{(n)}}{s_{n}}\Big)\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n},\qquad\bm{C}_{\sigma}:=\mathop{\mathrm{}}{diag}\!\Big(\frac{\zeta_{2}^{(1)}}{s_{1}^{2}},\dots,\frac{\zeta_{2}^{(n)}}{s_{n}^{2}}\Big)\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}.

Lemma 61
.

Condition on
\bm{X}
. Then:

\displaystyle\bm{D}_{0}\bm{R}^{\odot 0}\bm{D}_{0}

\displaystyle=(\bm{D}_{0}\bm{1})(\bm{D}_{0}\bm{1})^{\top}=\bm{\mu}_{\sigma}\bm{\mu}_{\sigma}^{\top},

\displaystyle\bm{D}_{1}\bm{R}\bm{D}_{1}

\displaystyle=\bm{A}_{\sigma}\,\bm{G}\,\bm{A}_{\sigma},

\displaystyle\bm{D}_{2}\bm{R}^{\odot 2}\bm{D}_{2}

\displaystyle=\bm{C}_{\sigma}\,\bm{H}\,\bm{C}_{\sigma}.

Proof.

The first identity uses
\bm{R}^{\odot 0}=\bm{1}\bm{1}^{\top}
.
For the second,

(\bm{D}_{1}\bm{R}\bm{D}_{1})_{ij}=\zeta_{1}^{(i)}\zeta_{1}^{(j)}\frac{\bm{G}_{ij}}{s_{i}s_{j}}=\Big(\frac{\zeta_{1}^{(i)}}{s_{i}}\Big)\Big(\frac{\zeta_{1}^{(j)}}{s_{j}}\Big)\bm{G}_{ij}=(\bm{A}_{\sigma}\bm{G}\bm{A}_{\sigma})_{ij}.

Similarly,
(\bm{D}_{2}\bm{R}^{\odot 2}\bm{D}_{2})_{ij}=\zeta_{2}^{(i)}\zeta_{2}^{(j)}\frac{\bm{G}_{ij}^{2}}{s_{i}^{2}s_{j}^{2}}=\Big(\frac{\zeta_{2}^{(i)}}{s_{i}^{2}}\Big)\Big(\frac{\zeta_{2}^{(j)}}{s_{j}^{2}}\Big)\bm{G}_{ij}^{2}=(\bm{C}_{\sigma}\,\bm{H}\,\bm{C}_{\sigma})_{ij}.

∎

Lemma 62

(Population CK after nuisance deflation)
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
7
,

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|\bm{P}_{\sigma}\bm{\Phi}(\bm{X})\bm{P}_{\sigma}-\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\alpha_{0}\bm{P}_{\sigma}+\alpha_{2}\bm{P}_{\sigma}\bm{H}\bm{P}_{\sigma}}}\right)}}\right\|\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}0.

(H.18)

Proof.

Condition on
\bm{X}
. Recall
\bm{D}_{k}=\mathop{\mathrm{}}{diag}(\zeta_{k}^{(1)},\ldots,\zeta_{k}^{(n)})
,
\bm{D}=\mathop{\mathrm{}}{diag}(s_{1},\ldots,s_{n})
, and
\bm{R}=\bm{D}^{-1}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{D}^{-1}.

By the exact Mehler expansion, Lemma
60
,

\bm{\Phi}(\bm{X})=\sum_{k\geq 0}\bm{D}_{k}\bm{R}^{\odot k}\bm{D}_{k}.

(H.19)

The
k=0
term is
\bm{\mu}_{\sigma}\bm{\mu}_{\sigma}^{\top}
and is killed by
\bm{P}_{\sigma}
.
The
k=1
term equals
\bm{A}_{\sigma}\bm{X}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{A}_{\sigma},

by Lemma
61
, and it is also killed by
\bm{P}_{\sigma}
because its
range is contained in
\mathrm{Range}(\bm{A}_{\sigma}\bm{X}^{\top})\subset\mathcal{U}_{X}
.

For the
k=2
term, Lemma
61
gives
\bm{D}_{2}\bm{R}^{\odot 2}\bm{D}_{2}=\bm{C}_{\sigma}\bm{H}\bm{C}_{\sigma}.

By Lemma
11
,

\|\bm{C}_{\sigma}-a_{2}\bm{I}_{n}\|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. Moreover

\|\bm{P}_{\sigma}\bm{H}\bm{P}_{\sigma}\|=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
after the constant direction is removed; this follows
from the quadratic-regime MP limit for the centered lift, proved below in
Lemma
66
. Hence

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\|\bm{P}_{\sigma}\bm{C}_{\sigma}\bm{H}\bm{C}_{\sigma}\bm{P}_{\sigma}-a_{2}^{2}\bm{P}_{\sigma}\bm{H}\bm{P}_{\sigma}}}\right\|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(H.20)

It remains to treat the Hermite tail
k\geq 3
. Write

\bm{T}_{\geq 3}:=\sum_{k\geq 3}\bm{D}_{k}\bm{R}^{\odot k}\bm{D}_{k}.

The diagonal part satisfies, uniformly in
i
,

\sum_{k\geq 3}(\zeta_{k}^{(i)})^{2}=\mathbb{E}[\sigma(s_{i}\xi)^{2}]-(\zeta_{0}^{(i)})^{2}-(\zeta_{1}^{(i)})^{2}-(\zeta_{2}^{(i)})^{2}=\alpha_{0}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

again by
\max_{i}|s_{i}-1|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
and scale-continuity. Hence the diagonal tail
is
\alpha_{0}\bm{I}_{n}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
in operator norm.

For the off-diagonal tail, fix
K\geq 3
. Following the proof of Lemma 5.2 of
[
82
]
, for each fixed
k\geq 3
, we can get

\|\bm{P}_{\sigma}(\bm{R}^{\odot k}-\bm{I}_{n})\bm{P}_{\sigma}\|\leq\|\bm{P}(\bm{R}^{\odot k}-\bm{I}_{n})\bm{P}\|=O_{\mathbb{P}}\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sqrt{\frac{n}{d^{k}}}}}\right)=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Summing over
3\leq k\leq K
gives an
o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
contribution. For
k>K
, use
Cauchy–Schwarz in the Hermite coefficients and

\max_{i\neq j}|\bm{R}_{ij}|\leq\tau_{n}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
to obtain the entrywise bound

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\sum_{k>K}\zeta_{k}^{(i)}\zeta_{k}^{(j)}\bm{R}_{ij}^{k}}}\right|\leq\tau_{n}^{K}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sum_{k>K}(\zeta_{k}^{(i)})^{2}}}\right)^{1/2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sum_{k>K}(\zeta_{k}^{(j)})^{2}}}\right)^{1/2}\leq C\tau_{n}^{K}

with high probability. Taking
K=\lfloor\log d\rfloor
and using Gershgorin’s
bound yields
o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
for the remaining off-diagonal tail. Therefore

\|\bm{P}_{\sigma}\bm{T}_{\geq 3}\bm{P}_{\sigma}-\alpha_{0}\bm{P}_{\sigma}\|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Combining the
k=0,1,2
and
k\geq 3
parts in (
H.19
) proves
(
H.18
).
∎

H.2
The BBP Transition of Population CK

Let
\mathrm{svec}:\mathbb{S}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{p}
be the Frobenius-isometric symmetric vectorization,
with
p=d(d+1)/2
and

\langle\mathrm{svec}(\bm{A}),\mathrm{svec}(\bm{B})\rangle=\langle\bm{A},\bm{B}\rangle_{F}.

Define

\bm{Q}:=[\bm{q}_{1},\ldots,\bm{q}_{n}]\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times n},\qquad\bm{q}_{i}:=\mathrm{svec}(\bm{x}_{i}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}).

(H.21)

Then
\bm{H}=\bm{Q}^{\top}\bm{Q}
and

\bm{H}_{\sigma}:=\bm{P}_{\sigma}\bm{H}\bm{P}_{\sigma}=(\bm{Q}\bm{P}_{\sigma})^{\top}(\bm{Q}\bm{P}_{\sigma}).

(H.22)

Define the deterministic unit-norm
vector

\bm{s}:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathrm{svec}(\bm{u}_{1}\bm{u}_{1}^{\top}-\bm{u}_{2}\bm{u}_{2}^{\top})\in\mathbb{R}^{p}.

(H.23)

This above formula (
H.22
) is also called kernel trick, see Section 3.7 of
[
79
]
. Kernel trick helps us make the nonlinear matrix (quadratic kernel matrix) into a linear sample covariance matrix. Then we can apply standard random matrix results to analyze spikes and ESD.

Lemma 63

(Unique label-dependent direction in the quadratic lift for XOR)
.

Recall
{\bm{s}}
defined in (
H.23
).
Then there exists a deterministic
\bar{\bm{q}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
and a scalar
\delta_{d}>0
such that for all
i
,

\mathbb{E}[\bm{q}_{i}\mid y_{i}]=\bar{\bm{q}}+y_{i}\,\frac{\delta_{d}}{\sqrt{p}}\,\bm{s},\qquad\delta_{d}=\frac{r^{2}}{2}\sqrt{1+\frac{1}{d}}.

In particular,
\delta_{d}\to\delta:=r^{2}/2
as
d\to\infty
, and one may write

\mathbb{E}[\bm{q}_{i}\mid y_{i}]=\bar{\bm{q}}+y_{i}\,\frac{\delta}{\sqrt{p}}\,\bm{s}+\bm{r}_{i,d},\qquad\|\bm{r}_{i,d}\|_{2}\leq C\frac{1}{d\sqrt{p}}.

Moreover, centering removes
\bar{\bm{q}}
:

\bm{Q}\bm{P}=(\bm{Q}-\bar{\bm{q}}\,\bm{1}^{\top})\bm{P}.

Proof.

Fix
i
and condition on
y_{i}
(equivalently, on
a(i)\in\{1,2\}
).
Expand

\bm{x}_{i}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}=\bm{z}_{i}\bm{z}_{i}^{\top}+\bm{z}_{i}\bm{m}_{i}^{\top}+\bm{m}_{i}\bm{z}_{i}^{\top}+\bm{m}_{i}\bm{m}_{i}^{\top}.

Taking conditional expectation given
y_{i}
:

•

\mathbb{E}[\bm{z}_{i}\bm{z}_{i}^{\top}]=\frac{1}{d}\bm{I}_{d}
.

•

Since
\bm{z}_{i}
is mean-zero and independent of
\bm{m}_{i}
,
\mathbb{E}[\bm{z}_{i}\bm{m}_{i}^{\top}+\bm{m}_{i}\bm{z}_{i}^{\top}\mid y_{i}]=\bm{0}
.

•

Since
\bm{m}_{i}=\eta_{i}\frac{r}{\sqrt{d}}\bm{u}_{a(i)}
and
\eta_{i}^{2}=1
,

\mathbb{E}[\bm{m}_{i}\bm{m}_{i}^{\top}\mid y_{i}]=\frac{r^{2}}{d}\,\bm{u}_{a(i)}\bm{u}_{a(i)}^{\top}=\frac{r^{2}}{2d}\big(\bm{u}_{1}\bm{u}_{1}^{\top}+\bm{u}_{2}\bm{u}_{2}^{\top}\big)+y_{i}\frac{r^{2}}{2d}\big(\bm{u}_{1}\bm{u}_{1}^{\top}-\bm{u}_{2}\bm{u}_{2}^{\top}\big).

Hence

\mathbb{E}[\bm{x}_{i}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\mid y_{i}]=\underbrace{\Big(\frac{1}{d}\bm{I}_{d}+\frac{r^{2}}{2d}\big(\bm{u}_{1}\bm{u}_{1}^{\top}+\bm{u}_{2}\bm{u}_{2}^{\top}\big)\Big)}_{=:\ \bar{\bm{\Sigma}}}+y_{i}\underbrace{\Big(\frac{r^{2}}{2d}\big(\bm{u}_{1}\bm{u}_{1}^{\top}-\bm{u}_{2}\bm{u}_{2}^{\top}\big)\Big)}_{=:\ \Delta\bm{\Sigma}}.

Apply the linear map
\mathrm{svec}
:
\mathbb{E}[\bm{q}_{i}\mid y_{i}]=\mathrm{svec}(\bar{\bm{\Sigma}})+y_{i}\,\mathrm{svec}(\Delta\bm{\Sigma}).

Define
\bar{\bm{q}}:=\mathrm{svec}(\bar{\bm{\Sigma}})
.
For the label-dependent part,

\mathrm{svec}(\Delta\bm{\Sigma})=\frac{r^{2}}{2d}\,\mathrm{svec}(\bm{u}_{1}\bm{u}_{1}^{\top}-\bm{u}_{2}\bm{u}_{2}^{\top})=\frac{r^{2}}{2d}\,\sqrt{2}\,\bm{s}=\frac{r^{2}}{\sqrt{2}\,d}\,\bm{s}.

Since
p=d(d+1)/2
, we have
\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}=\frac{\sqrt{2}}{d\sqrt{1+1/d}},
hence
\frac{r^{2}}{\sqrt{2}\,d}=\frac{\delta_{d}}{\sqrt{p}}.

This proves the exact identity with
\delta_{d}
.
Since
\delta_{d}-\delta=O(1/d)
and
\|\bm{s}\|_{2}=1
, the residual
\bm{r}_{i,d}
bound follows.
Finally,
\bm{P}\bm{1}=\bm{0}
implies
\bar{\bm{q}}\bm{1}^{\top}\bm{P}=\bm{0}
, hence
(\bm{Q}-\bar{\bm{q}}\bm{1}^{\top})\bm{P}=\bm{Q}\bm{P}.

∎

Lemma 64

(Lifted noise is asymptotically isotropic)
.

Define the centered lifted noise

\bm{\varepsilon}_{i}:=\bm{q}_{i}-\mathbb{E}[\bm{q}_{i}\mid y_{i}]=\mathrm{svec}\!\Big(\bm{x}_{i}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[\bm{x}_{i}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\mid y_{i}]\Big).

Then
(\bm{\varepsilon}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
are independent, mean-zero, and for each fixed
i
,

\mathbb{E}[\bm{\varepsilon}_{i}\bm{\varepsilon}_{i}^{\top}\mid y_{i}]=\frac{2}{d^{2}}\bm{I}_{p}+\frac{4r^{2}}{d^{2}}\,\bm{U}_{a(i)},

where
\bm{U}_{a(i)}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}
is a deterministic PSD matrix (depending on
\bm{u}_{a(i)}
) satisfying

\mathrm{rank}(\bm{U}_{a(i)})\leq d,\qquad\|\bm{U}_{a(i)}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1.

Equivalently,
\mathbb{E}[\bm{\varepsilon}_{i}\bm{\varepsilon}_{i}^{\top}\mid y_{i}]=\frac{1}{p}\bm{I}_{p}+\frac{2r^{2}}{p}\,\bm{U}_{a(i)}+O\!\Big(\frac{1}{pd}\Big)
.
In particular, the covariance is
isotropic up to a rank-
O(d)
perturbation
of size
\Theta(1/p)
in operator norm.
If
\bm{\Pi}_{a(i)}
denotes the orthogonal projector onto
\mathrm{Im}(\bm{U}_{a(i)})
, then on the orthogonal complement

\mathrm{Im}(\bm{U}_{a(i)})^{\perp}
,

(\bm{I}_{p}-\bm{\Pi}_{a(i)})\,\mathbb{E}[\bm{\varepsilon}_{i}\bm{\varepsilon}_{i}^{\top}\mid y_{i}]\,(\bm{I}_{p}-\bm{\Pi}_{a(i)})=\frac{2}{d^{2}}(\bm{I}_{p}-\bm{\Pi}_{a(i)}).

Proof.

Step 1 (independence and centering).

The columns
\bm{x}_{i}
are independent across
i
, hence so are
\bm{q}_{i}
and
\bm{\varepsilon}_{i}
.
By construction,
\mathbb{E}[\bm{\varepsilon}_{i}\mid y_{i}]=\bm{0}
.

Step 2 (explicit centered matrix representation).

Fix
i
and condition on
y_{i}
(so
\bm{u}:=\bm{u}_{a(i)}
is fixed) and on
\eta_{i}
.
Write
\bm{m}:=\eta_{i}\frac{r}{\sqrt{d}}\bm{u}
and
\bm{z}:=\bm{z}_{i}
so that
\bm{x}=\bm{z}+\bm{m}
.
Using
\mathbb{E}[\bm{x}\bm{x}^{\top}\mid y_{i}]=\frac{1}{d}\bm{I}_{d}+\frac{r^{2}}{d}\bm{u}\bm{u}^{\top}
, we obtain

\bm{x}\bm{x}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}[\bm{x}\bm{x}^{\top}\mid y_{i}]=\Big(\bm{z}\bm{z}^{\top}-\frac{1}{d}\bm{I}_{d}\Big)+\Big(\bm{z}\bm{m}^{\top}+\bm{m}\bm{z}^{\top}\Big).

Therefore,
\bm{\varepsilon}_{i}=\mathrm{svec}\!\Big(\bm{z}\bm{z}^{\top}-\frac{1}{d}\bm{I}_{d}\Big)+\mathrm{svec}\!\Big(\bm{z}\bm{m}^{\top}+\bm{m}\bm{z}^{\top}\Big).

Step 3 (covariance in test-matrix form).

Let
\bm{A},\bm{B}\in\mathbb{S}^{d}
and set
\bm{a}:=\mathrm{svec}(\bm{A})
,
\bm{b}:=\mathrm{svec}(\bm{B})
.
By the
\mathrm{svec}
isometry,

\langle\bm{\varepsilon}_{i},\bm{a}\rangle=\Big\langle\bm{z}\bm{z}^{\top}-\frac{1}{d}\bm{I}_{d},\bm{A}\Big\rangle_{F}+\langle\bm{z}\bm{m}^{\top}+\bm{m}\bm{z}^{\top},\bm{A}\rangle_{F}.

Since
\bm{A}
is symmetric,

\langle\bm{z}\bm{m}^{\top}+\bm{m}\bm{z}^{\top},\bm{A}\rangle_{F}=\mathrm{tr}((\bm{z}\bm{m}^{\top}+\bm{m}\bm{z}^{\top})\bm{A})=2\,\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{A}\bm{m}.

Hence

\langle\bm{\varepsilon}_{i},\bm{a}\rangle=\underbrace{\Big(\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{A}\bm{z}-\frac{1}{d}\mathrm{tr}(\bm{A})\Big)}_{=:T_{2}(\bm{A})}+\underbrace{2\,\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{A}\bm{m}}_{=:T_{1}(\bm{A})}.

Here
T_{2}(\bm{A})
is a centered quadratic form in
\bm{z}
(2nd chaos) and
T_{1}(\bm{A})
is linear in
\bm{z}
(1st chaos).
By symmetry of Gaussian moments,
\mathbb{E}[T_{2}(\bm{A})T_{1}(\bm{B})]=0
for all
\bm{A},\bm{B}
.
Therefore

\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{\varepsilon}_{i},\bm{a}\rangle\langle\bm{\varepsilon}_{i},\bm{b}\rangle\mid y_{i}]=\mathbb{E}[T_{2}(\bm{A})T_{2}(\bm{B})]+\mathbb{E}[T_{1}(\bm{A})T_{1}(\bm{B})].

Step 4 (compute each covariance term).

Since
\bm{z}\sim\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\bm{I}_{d}/d)
, a standard Wick/Isserlis computation gives

\mathbb{E}[T_{2}(\bm{A})T_{2}(\bm{B})]=\mathrm{Cov}(\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{A}\bm{z},\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{B}\bm{z})=\frac{2}{d^{2}}\mathrm{tr}(\bm{A}\bm{B}).

Also,

\mathbb{E}[T_{1}(\bm{A})T_{1}(\bm{B})]=4\,\mathbb{E}[(\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{A}\bm{m})(\bm{z}^{\top}\bm{B}\bm{m})]=4\,(\bm{A}\bm{m})^{\top}\mathbb{E}[\bm{z}\bm{z}^{\top}]\,(\bm{B}\bm{m})=\frac{4}{d}\,\bm{m}^{\top}\bm{A}\bm{B}\bm{m}.

Since
\bm{m}=\eta_{i}\frac{r}{\sqrt{d}}\bm{u}
and
\eta_{i}^{2}=1
,
\frac{4}{d}\,\bm{m}^{\top}\bm{A}\bm{B}\bm{m}=\frac{4r^{2}}{d^{2}}\,\bm{u}^{\top}\bm{A}\bm{B}\bm{u}.
Altogether,

\mathbb{E}[\langle\bm{\varepsilon}_{i},\bm{a}\rangle\langle\bm{\varepsilon}_{i},\bm{b}\rangle\mid y_{i}]=\frac{2}{d^{2}}\mathrm{tr}(\bm{A}\bm{B})+\frac{4r^{2}}{d^{2}}\,\bm{u}^{\top}\bm{A}\bm{B}\bm{u}.

Step 5 (identify
\bm{U}_{a(i)}
and its rank bound).

Define the linear operator
\mathcal{U}_{\bm{u}}:\mathbb{S}^{d}\to\mathbb{S}^{d}
by

\langle\mathcal{U}_{\bm{u}}(\bm{A}),\bm{B}\rangle_{F}:=\bm{u}^{\top}\bm{A}\bm{B}\bm{u}\qquad(\bm{A},\bm{B}\in\mathbb{S}^{d}),

and let
\bm{U}_{a(i)}
be its matrix representation in
\mathrm{svec}
coordinates:
for
\bm{a}=\mathrm{svec}(\bm{A})
,
\bm{b}=\mathrm{svec}(\bm{B})
,

\bm{a}^{\top}\bm{U}_{a(i)}\bm{b}=\bm{u}^{\top}\bm{A}\bm{B}\bm{u}.

Then the covariance identity becomes exactly

\mathbb{E}[\bm{\varepsilon}_{i}\bm{\varepsilon}_{i}^{\top}\mid y_{i}]=\frac{2}{d^{2}}\bm{I}_{p}+\frac{4r^{2}}{d^{2}}\bm{U}_{a(i)}.

The map
\bm{A}\mapsto\bm{A}\bm{u}
has range in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, so
\mathcal{U}_{\bm{u}}
has rank at most
d
,
hence
\mathrm{rank}(\bm{U}_{a(i)})\leq d
.

Moreover, for any
\bm{A}\in\mathbb{S}^{d}
with
\|\bm{A}\|_{F}=1
,

\bm{u}^{\top}\bm{A}^{2}\bm{u}=\|\bm{A}\bm{u}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\|\bm{A}\|_{F}^{2}\|\bm{u}\|_{2}^{2}=1,

so
\|\mathcal{U}_{\bm{u}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1
as an operator on
(\mathbb{S}^{d},\|\cdot\|_{F})
, which implies

\|\bm{U}_{a(i)}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1
in
\mathrm{svec}
coordinates.

Finally, since
p=d(d+1)/2
, we have
\frac{2}{d^{2}}=\frac{1}{p}\cdot\frac{d+1}{d}=\frac{1}{p}+O(\frac{1}{pd})
,
yielding the stated equivalent form.
∎

Proposition 65

(Decomposition of the centered lifted matrix)
.

Let
\bm{y}=(y_{1},\dots,y_{n})^{\top}
be balanced (
\bm{y}^{\top}\bm{1}=0
), and define
\bm{E}:=[\bm{\varepsilon}_{1},\dots,\bm{\varepsilon}_{n}]\bm{P}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times n}.

Then

\bm{Q}\bm{P}=\frac{\delta}{\sqrt{p}}\bm{s}\bm{y}^{\top}+\bm{E}+\bm{R}_{Q},\qquad\|\bm{R}_{Q}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

where
\delta=r^{2}/2
and
\bm{s}
is as in Lemma
63
.
In fact, one may take the explicit remainder

\bm{R}_{Q}=\Big(\frac{\delta_{d}-\delta}{\sqrt{p}}\Big)\bm{s}\bm{y}^{\top},\qquad\|\bm{R}_{Q}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\Big|\frac{\delta_{d}-\delta}{\sqrt{p}}\Big|\,\|\bm{s}\|_{2}\,\|\bm{y}\|_{2}\;\lesssim\;\frac{1}{d}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{n}{p}}=o(1),

since
\delta_{d}-\delta=O(1/d)
and
n/p\to\gamma\in(0,\infty)
.

Proof.

For each
i
, by definition of
\bm{\varepsilon}_{i}
,
\bm{q}_{i}=\mathbb{E}[\bm{q}_{i}\mid y_{i}]+\bm{\varepsilon}_{i}.

By Lemma
63
,

\mathbb{E}[\bm{q}_{i}\mid y_{i}]=\bar{\bm{q}}+y_{i}\frac{\delta_{d}}{\sqrt{p}}\bm{s}.

Stacking columns yields

\bm{Q}=\bar{\bm{q}}\,\bm{1}^{\top}+\frac{\delta_{d}}{\sqrt{p}}\bm{s}\bm{y}^{\top}+[\bm{\varepsilon}_{1},\dots,\bm{\varepsilon}_{n}].

Right-multiply by
\bm{P}
. Using
\bm{1}^{\top}\bm{P}=\bm{0}^{\top}
and
\bm{y}^{\top}\bm{P}=\bm{y}^{\top}
(balanced labels),

\bm{Q}\bm{P}=\frac{\delta_{d}}{\sqrt{p}}\bm{s}\bm{y}^{\top}+[\bm{\varepsilon}_{1},\dots,\bm{\varepsilon}_{n}]\bm{P}=\frac{\delta}{\sqrt{p}}\bm{s}\bm{y}^{\top}+\bm{E}+\Big(\frac{\delta_{d}-\delta}{\sqrt{p}}\Big)\bm{s}\bm{y}^{\top}.

Define
\bm{R}_{Q}:=\big(\frac{\delta_{d}-\delta}{\sqrt{p}}\big)\bm{s}\bm{y}^{\top}
.
The displayed operator-norm bound follows since
\|\bm{s}\|_{2}=1
,
\|\bm{y}\|_{2}=\sqrt{n}
,

\sqrt{p}\asymp d
, and
\delta_{d}-\delta=O(1/d)
.
∎

From the deterministic equivalence in Proposition
65
, we obtain the spiked-rectangular form

\bm{Q}\bm{P}=\frac{\delta}{\sqrt{p}}\bm{s}\bm{y}^{\top}+\bm{E}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\qquad\bm{E}:=[\bm{\varepsilon}_{1},\dots,\bm{\varepsilon}_{n}]\bm{P}.

Thus
\bm{H}_{c}=(\bm{Q}\bm{P})^{\top}(\bm{Q}\bm{P})
behaves like a rank-one deformation of an isotropic Wishart Gram matrix.

Lemma 66

(BBP for the deflated quadratic lift)
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
7
, the ESD of

\bm{H}_{\sigma}
converges weakly in probability to
\rho_{\gamma}^{\mathrm{MP}}
. Moreover, the
quadratic XOR label channel undergoes the following BBP transition.

(i)

If
\ell\leq\gamma^{-1/2}
, then the label channel does not produce a
separated outlier of
\bm{H}_{\sigma}
and any separated nuisance outlier has vanishing
label overlap.

(ii)

If
\ell>\gamma^{-1/2}
and the label outlier is non-colliding with
nuisance outliers, then
\bm{H}_{\sigma}
has a simple label outlier

\widehat{\lambda}_{\mathrm{out}}\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}\lambda_{\mathrm{out}}(\gamma,\ell)=1+\gamma+\gamma\ell+\frac{1}{\ell}.

If
\widehat{\bm{v}}_{Q}
is the associated unit eigenvector, then

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\widehat{\bm{v}}_{Q},\frac{\bm{y}}{\sqrt{n}}}}\right\rangle}}\right|^{2}\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}\mathrm{Align}(\gamma,\ell)=\frac{\gamma\ell^{2}-1}{\gamma\ell(\ell+1)}.

(H.24)

Proof.

By Proposition
65
, with
\delta=r^{2}/2
,

\bm{Q}\bm{P}=\frac{\delta}{\sqrt{p}}\bm{s}\bm{y}^{\top}+\bm{E}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\qquad\bm{E}=[\bm{\varepsilon}_{1},\ldots,\bm{\varepsilon}_{n}]\bm{P},

(H.25)

where the centered lifted noise is isotropic up to a rank-
O(d)
covariance
perturbation, as in Lemma
64
. Since

\bm{P}_{\sigma}\bm{1}=0
, the same decomposition gives

\bm{Q}\bm{P}_{\sigma}=\frac{\delta}{\sqrt{p}}\bm{s}\bm{y}^{\top}\bm{P}_{\sigma}+\bm{E}\bm{P}_{\sigma}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(H.26)

Lemma
59
gives

\|\bm{P}_{\sigma}\bm{y}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n}\to 1
in probability.

The rank-
O(d)
non-isotropic part of the covariance of
\bm{\varepsilon}_{i}
affects at
most
O(d)=o(n)
singular values. It is invisible to the limiting ESD and, by
the same orthogonality argument as in Lemma
59
, its
right singular subspace has vanishing overlap with
\bm{y}/\sqrt{n}
. Therefore the
anisotropic resolvent in the two spike directions
(\bm{s},\bm{P}_{\sigma}\bm{y}/\|\bm{P}_{\sigma}\bm{y}\|)
is
the same, up to
o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, as that of the rectangular spiked model

\widetilde{\bm{Q}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\bm{G}+\delta\sqrt{\frac{n}{p}}\,\bm{s}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\bm{P}_{\sigma}\bm{y}}{\|\bm{P}_{\sigma}\bm{y}\|_{2}}}}\right)^{\top},\qquad[\bm{G}]_{ij}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{i.i.d.}}}{{\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,1).

(H.27)

This reduction is the quadratic-regime universality theorem for kernel random
matrices from

[
65
]
together with
Lemma
64
.
Let
\theta:=\delta\sqrt{\gamma}=\sqrt{\gamma\ell}.

The Benaych-Georges–Nadakuditi rectangular BBP theorem

[
10
]
applied to (
H.27
)
shows that a separated singular-value outlier exists iff
\theta>\gamma^{1/4}
, i.e.
\ell>\gamma^{-1/2}.

In the supercritical case, the outlying eigenvalue of

\widetilde{\bm{Q}}^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{Q}}
is

(1+\theta^{2})\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{\gamma}{\theta^{2}}}}\right)=(1+\gamma\ell)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{1}{\ell}}}\right)=1+\gamma+\gamma\ell+\frac{1}{\ell},

and the squared overlap of the right singular vector with the right spike
direction is
\frac{1-\gamma/\theta^{4}}{1+\gamma/\theta^{2}}=\frac{\gamma\ell^{2}-1}{\gamma\ell(\ell+1)}.

Since
\|\bm{P}_{\sigma}\bm{y}\|_{2}/\sqrt{n}\to 1
, this is exactly
(
H.24
). The subcritical statement and the ESD convergence
follow from the same theorem and the MP law for the null rectangular matrix.
∎

H.3
The BBP Transition of Finite-width CK

Define the conditional covariance of one deflated feature row by

\bm{\Sigma}_{\sigma}:=\mathbb{E}_{\bm{w}}\!\bigl[\bm{P}_{\sigma}\sigma(\bm{w}^{\top}\bm{X})^{\top}\sigma(\bm{w}^{\top}\bm{X})\bm{P}_{\sigma}\mid\bm{X}\bigr]=\bm{P}_{\sigma}\bm{\Phi}(\bm{X})\bm{P}_{\sigma}.

(H.28)

By Lemma
62
,

\bm{\Sigma}_{\sigma}=\alpha_{0}\bm{P}_{\sigma}+\alpha_{2}\bm{H}_{\sigma}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(H.29)

Thus the ESD of
\bm{\Sigma}_{\sigma}
converges to
\nu_{\rm q}
, and the
label outlier of
\bm{H}_{\sigma}
maps to the population covariance outlier

\Lambda_{y}=\alpha_{0}+\alpha_{2}\lambda_{\mathrm{out}}(\gamma,\ell)
.

Lemma 67

(Finite-width sample-covariance BBP for the deflated CK)
.

Assume the hypotheses of Theorem
7
. Conditional
on
\bm{X}
, the nonzero eigenvalues of
\bm{K}_{\sigma}
are those of a sample covariance
matrix with aspect ratio
n/N\to\phi
and population covariance

\bm{\Sigma}_{\sigma}
. Consequently:

(i)

The ESD of
\bm{K}_{\sigma}
converges weakly in probability to

\mu_{\rm q}
from (
H.10
).

(ii)

Let
\tau>\tau_{+}^{\nu}
be any non-colliding population covariance outlier
of
\bm{\Sigma}_{\sigma}
with unit eigenvector
\bm{v}_{\tau}
. If

z^{\prime}(-1/\tau)>0
, then
\bm{K}_{\sigma}
has a sample outlier at
z\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\frac{1}{\tau}}}\right)+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

and if
\widehat{\bm{v}}_{\tau}
is its unit eigenvector, then for every deterministic
or
\bm{X}
-measurable unit vector
\bm{b}
satisfying the usual anisotropic local-law
bounds,

|\widehat{\bm{v}}_{\tau}^{\top}\bm{b}|^{2}-\varphi\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\frac{1}{\tau}}}\right)|\bm{v}_{\tau}^{\top}\bm{b}|^{2}\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}0.

(H.30)

If
z^{\prime}(-1/\tau)\leq 0
, this population outlier sticks to the
bulk and produces no separated sample eigenvalue.

Proof.

Let the rows of
\sigma(\bm{W}\bm{X})\bm{P}_{\sigma}
be denoted by

\bm{r}_{1}^{\top},\ldots,\bm{r}_{N}^{\top}
. Since
\bm{P}_{\sigma}\bm{\mu}_{\sigma}=0
, the rows are centered
conditional on
\bm{X}
:
\mathbb{E}[\bm{r}_{a}\mid\bm{X}]=\bm{0},
and
\mathbb{E}[\bm{r}_{a}\bm{r}_{a}^{\top}\mid\bm{X}]=\bm{\Sigma}_{\sigma}.

Moreover,
\bm{K}_{\sigma}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{a=1}^{N}\bm{r}_{a}\bm{r}_{a}^{\top}.

For globally Lipschitz
\sigma
, Gaussian concentration and the Hanson–Wright
inequality imply the quadratic-form concentration condition

\bm{r}_{a}^{\top}\bm{B}\bm{r}_{a}-\mathrm{Tr}(\bm{B}\bm{\Sigma}_{\sigma})\prec\|\bm{\Sigma}_{\sigma}^{1/2}\bm{B}\bm{\Sigma}_{\sigma}^{1/2}\|_{F}

(H.31)

for deterministic
\bm{B}
and, by a standard net argument, for the resolvent test
matrices used in the anisotropic local law. This is the same verification as
in Lemma
29
; there the conditional rows are

\sigma(\bm{w}^{\top}\bm{Z})-\bm{m}
, while here they are

\sigma(\bm{w}^{\top}\bm{X})\bm{P}_{\sigma}
.

The ESD of
\bm{\Sigma}_{\sigma}
converges to
\nu_{\rm q}
by
(
H.29
) and Lemma
66
. Therefore the
general sample-covariance theorem for nonlinear spiked covariance matrices,
Theorem 12 of
[
81
]
, applies. Its
bulk statement gives

\rho_{\phi}^{\mathrm{MP}}\boxtimes\nu_{\rm q}
. Its outlier and eigenvector
statements give exactly the criterion

z^{\prime}(-1/\tau)>0
, the location

z(-1/\tau)
, and the anisotropic overlap formula
(
H.30
). The same theorem also states that
when the derivative is nonpositive, the associated eigenvalue does not separate
from the limiting support.
∎

Proof of Theorem
7
.

The bulk statement in item

(i)
is Lemma
67
(i).

For the first BBP transition, Lemma
66
gives the
quadratic-lift threshold
\ell>\gamma^{-1/2}
, the outlier location

\lambda_{\mathrm{out}}(\gamma,\ell)
, and the population label overlap

\mathrm{Align}(\gamma,\ell)
. Combining this with the affine population-covariance map
(
H.29
) gives the deflated CK population spike

\Lambda_{y}=\alpha_{0}+\alpha_{2}\lambda_{\mathrm{out}}(\gamma,\ell),

with the same eigenvector and therefore the same label overlap
(
H.24
). This proves item
(ii)
.

Assume now that
\ell>\gamma^{-1/2}
and the label population spike is
non-colliding. Apply Lemma
67
(ii) with

\tau=\Lambda_{y}
and
\bm{b}=\bm{y}/\sqrt{n}
. The outlier separates precisely when
z^{\prime}\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\frac{1}{\Lambda_{y}}}}\right)>0,

and the location is
z\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\frac{1}{\Lambda_{y}}}}\right)=\Lambda_{y}+\phi\Lambda_{y}\int\frac{t}{\Lambda_{y}-t}\,\nu_{\rm q}(dt)=\Lambda_{y}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\phi\int\frac{t}{\Lambda_{y}-t}\,\nu_{\rm q}(dt)}}\right),

which proves (
4.23
). The overlap formula
(
H.30
), together with
(
H.24
), gives

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\widehat{\bm{v}}_{y},\frac{\bm{y}}{\sqrt{n}}}}\right\rangle}}\right|^{2}\xrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}\mathrm{Align}(\gamma,\ell)\,\varphi\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\frac{1}{\Lambda_{y}}}}\right),

which is (
4.24
). Finally, substituting

s=-1/\Lambda_{y}
into (
H.11
) gives

z^{\prime}\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\frac{1}{\Lambda_{y}}}}\right)=\Lambda_{y}^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\phi\int\frac{t^{2}}{(\Lambda_{y}-t)^{2}}\,\nu_{\rm q}(dt)}}\right)

and

z\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\frac{1}{\Lambda_{y}}}}\right)=\Lambda_{y}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\phi\int\frac{t}{\Lambda_{y}-t}\,\nu_{\rm q}(dt)}}\right).

Therefore

\varphi\!\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\frac{1}{\Lambda_{y}}}}\right)=-\frac{(-1/\Lambda_{y})z^{\prime}(-1/\Lambda_{y})}{z(-1/\Lambda_{y})}=\frac{1-\phi\int\frac{t^{2}}{(\Lambda_{y}-t)^{2}}\,\nu_{\rm q}(dt)}{1+\phi\int\frac{t}{\Lambda_{y}-t}\,\nu_{\rm q}(dt)},

which proves (
4.25
). If
\ell\leq\gamma^{-1/2}
, Lemma
66
shows that the
quadratic lift has no separated label outlier; hence, by
(
H.29
), the population covariance
\bm{\Sigma}_{\sigma}
has no
label-aligned population outlier. If
\ell>\gamma^{-1/2}
but

z^{\prime}(-1/\Lambda_{y})\leq 0
, then
Lemma
67
(ii) says that the population spike
sticks to the sample bulk. In both cases there is no separated label-aligned
CK eigenvalue generated by the quadratic XOR channel. Any separated outlier
arising from a different population spike satisfies the anisotropic overlap
formula (
H.30
); since the corresponding
population eigenvector is asymptotically orthogonal to
\bm{y}/\sqrt{n}
by
Lemma
66
and Lemma
59
, its
sample eigenvector also has vanishing label overlap. This proves item

(iii)
.
∎
